# Project rules — icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing

## ⚠️ CRITICAL — the real Firebase project ID is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, NEVER `icoccha`

**Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §44): `icoccha` and `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` are two entirely separate Firebase/GCP projects** (different project numbers, confirmed via `firebase projects:list`), not an alias or shorthand for each other. Every `firebase`/patched-`firebase.js` CLI command in a large, indeterminate portion of this project's history — confirmed spanning at least Phase 6 through Phase 9 of this session, likely further back — used `--project icoccha`, silently deploying Cloud Functions, Firestore rules, and Firestore indexes to the WRONG project every time, while every verification step in the same sessions (`firebase functions:list --project icoccha`, etc.) checked the SAME wrong project and consistently, self-confirmingly reported success. **The compiled app that real users run connects to `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`** — confirmed directly from the generated app's own live Firebase config (`generated_code/android/app/google-services.json`'s `project_id` and `generated_code/ios/Runner/GoogleService-Info.plist`'s `PROJECT_ID`), not inferred. `firebase/.firebaserc` in this workspace already declares `"default": "icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing"` and `firebase projects:list` marks it `(current)` for this directory — the bare `firebase` command with NO `--project` flag at all would have used the CORRECT project automatically; it was the explicit `--project icoccha` override on every command that caused this.

**Before running ANY `firebase deploy`/`firebase functions:list`/`gcloud ... --project=...` command in this workspace, use `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` explicitly, or omit `--project` entirely and let `.firebaserc`'s default resolve it.** Never type `--project icoccha` — it is a real, different, wrong project that will silently accept the deploy and report success. If you see `--project icoccha` (without the `-new-mockup-9a6ing` suffix) anywhere in your own command history this session, in an existing doc, or about to be typed from habit, stop and correct it before running it.

**This bug class is dangerous specifically because nothing about a "successful" deploy reveals it** — the CLI has no way to know a human meant a different project than the one explicitly named. The only way this was caught was re-verifying a specific resource's live state (composite index build status) directly against the CORRECT project ID via `gcloud`, independent of whatever the deploy command itself reported — the same "don't trust a clean push alone, verify the actual resource" discipline this project's own rules already establish for DSL pushes, just not previously applied with enough suspicion toward the `--project` flag's own correctness. **After ANY deploy in this project going forward, spot-check at least one specific claim (an index's build state, a function's existence, a rule's content) against `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` explicitly** — don't rely solely on the deploy command's own stdout, and don't reuse whatever `--project` value appeared in a prior command without re-confirming it's the real one.

## Mandatory pre-task ritual

Before every task:

1. Read `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` in full (the change log — what's been done, why, and how it was verified).
2. Read this file (`.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`) in full.
3. For product/business requirements, consult `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` (as-is state of the workspace and live project) and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (the phased build plan and full requirements catalog) — these are the authoritative sources for "what exists" and "what's required," not something to re-derive from memory.
4. Inspect relevant source (DSL, `generated_code/`, live `inspect` output) before answering or coding — **never guess**.
5. After the task, update `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (append a new `§N` change-log entry) and this file (if a new convention, constraint, or SDK-level quirk was discovered) — and update `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`/`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` too if the task changed something they document as current/planned state.

## Mission & change policy

- This is `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, a FlutterFlow AI DSL workspace for the **guest/cast-facing Icoccha mobile app** (booking, chat, payments, cast profiles, affiliate) — a separate FlutterFlow project from the sister admin panel, `icoccha-admin-dashboard` (see "Relationship to the sister project" below).
- **Product truth (priority order):** (1) the client's own spec PDFs and chat log, as already catalogued in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; (2) `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`'s documented as-is state; (3) whatever the live FlutterFlow project/`generated_code/` actually shows, if it's drifted from what either document says. Bridge gaps deliberately — do not invent product rules. Where a requirement is genuinely ambiguous or two source documents conflict, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` already has a section for calling that out explicitly (§4) — extend that discipline rather than silently picking a side.
- **Minimize diffs.** Preserve existing architecture, naming (including known typos — e.g. `res_ic` for `res_id`, camelCase `extension_payments` keys — see `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §2), and coding style unless explicitly instructed otherwise, or unless a task specifically calls for a migration.
- Do not rewrite FlutterFlow pages into a different architecture (Riverpod, Clean Architecture, etc.) without an explicit request.
- Do not expand scope: no drive-by refactors, no unrelated cleanup beyond what a task needs, no unsolicited docs beyond the required knowledge updates.
- **This project is worked through the FlutterFlow AI SDK/CLI**, per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §1.3: schema/collection changes go through `app.*`/brownfield helpers in `dsl/edit.dart`; single-widget literal-value edits go through the `flutterflow_ai__patch` fast lane (see the base `CLAUDE.md` for the mandatory-first-attempt rule); anything structural goes through `dsl/edit.dart` + `flutterflow ai run`. Cloud Functions are a separate Node/TypeScript deliverable, deployed via the Firebase CLI, not via FlutterFlow AI — confirm the actual current state of that backend (per `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md`) before assuming any given callable already exists.
- When a requirement is ambiguous or a design decision has real product consequences (money, security, data loss), ask the user or record an explicit decision in `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` rather than guessing.

## Relationship to the sister project (`icoccha-admin-dashboard`)

Same overall product family, but a **separate FlutterFlow project** (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` here vs. `icoccha-admin-dashboard2-3se8y2` there), in a separate local workspace (`/home/ari/Documents/Task/new/icoccha_admin_dashboard`). **Do not assume a shared Firebase backend, Firestore schema, or security rules without checking** — `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §2 notes this project's Firebase project id as `icoccha`, distinct from the admin dashboard's `icoccha-admin-dashboard`; this hasn't been independently re-verified from this workspace, and this project's own known schema typos already diverge from the admin side's conventions. Confirm before porting any specific backend assumption (a Cloud Function name, a collection field, a security rule) across the two projects.

What **is** safely shared and already carried over below: FlutterFlow AI SDK/tooling behavior (DSL surface, proto quirks, codegen behavior, CLI mechanics) — this is the same tool regardless of which FlutterFlow project it points at. The "FlutterFlow quirks learned the hard way" section below is inherited wholesale from the admin-dashboard project's own rules file for exactly this reason.

## FlutterFlow quirks learned the hard way

**Inherited wholesale from the sister project's own rules file** (`icoccha-admin-dashboard/.cursor/rules/project_rules.md`, as of 2026-08-09) — every entry below is about FlutterFlow AI SDK/DSL/codegen/CLI behavior, not `icoccha-admin-dashboard`'s own business logic, so it applies directly to this project too even though the two are separate FlutterFlow projects. Citations like "(§18.x)" or "PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.x" refer to that OTHER project's own change log, not this one — kept as-is for traceability back to where each lesson was originally learned, in case the full original context is ever needed. Some entries reference specific `icoccha-admin-dashboard` page/widget names as the illustrative example the bug was first found on — the underlying lesson (a proto field, a codegen behavior, a CLI quirk) is what transfers, not the specific page name.

**Treat these as "likely still true, worth checking first" rather than blindly assumed** — this is a different FlutterFlow project, so re-verify anything version-sensitive (an SDK behavior could change between when it was learned and now) before relying on it for something high-stakes. When a NEW quirk is discovered in THIS project — whether it's a fresh rediscovery of one of these, a variant, or something genuinely new — add it below the inherited list (under its own "New quirks found in this project" subheading) rather than editing the inherited entries in place, so the provenance boundary between "carried over" and "found here" stays clear.

- **App State is session-sticky even when not "Persisted."** A variable with `Persisted: false` only resets to its declared default on a genuinely fresh app load — it is *not* reset between page navigations within one continuous Test Mode session. If a list/filter page's fetch silently returns an empty-but-successful result, check whether it reads a shared App State variable last written elsewhere (e.g. a filter dialog's Apply button) before assuming a data or backend bug. (Root-caused the KYC review list bug, §18.18.)
- **Boolean action arguments cannot be left truly "unset."** Unlike String/enum args (which can sit at a real "Unset"/`null`), a Boolean action argument always resolves to `true`/`false` — a bound Boolean App State defaults to `false` from page load, before any admin ever opens a filter dialog. Use a dedicated `ignore*Filter` (`bool?`) parameter that omits the field from the payload entirely when set, rather than trying to unset the Boolean itself (pattern already in `admin_get_users.dart`'s `isFrozen`/`ignoreFrozenFilter`). Also remember: omitting a filter must mean the payload key is *absent*, not present-with-`null` — a present `null` still reads as "filter on it" server-side and matches zero documents.
  - **This bit a real multi-tab save-button design (システム設定管理, §18.27), not just filters.** A single Custom Action shared across several UI sections, with `bool?` params meant to be left Unset by whichever section doesn't use them, doesn't work — confirmed live: 11 unrelated `xxxActive` params on a save button that should only have touched a different tab all rendered as `Value: False` in FlutterFlow's action-argument panel, not `Unset`. If saved, that would have silently overwritten unrelated data with `false` on every save. **Lesson: don't design one shared Custom Action with many optional params covering several independent UI sections/tabs when any of those params are Boolean.** Prefer one dedicated action per section/tab instead, with that section's own params **required** (non-nullable) — a button never needs another section's fields at all, so there's nothing to accidentally leave in a bad "Unset-but-not-really" state. Also double-check String-typed "Unset" args before trusting them: FlutterFlow can show `[Empty String]` instead of `Unset` for an untouched string picker, which still passes a Dart `!= null` check and is not the same as omitting the field.
- **To isolate a "no data" bug as FlutterFlow-side vs. backend-side, test the callable directly, bypassing Test Mode.** From Node: `admin.auth().createCustomToken(uid)` for the real admin's UID (via `firebase-admin` + `key.json`), then `signInWithCustomToken` with the client `firebase` SDK (reuse the API key from `lib/backend/firebase/firebase_config.dart`), then call the callable directly with `httpsCallable`. This proves in seconds whether data/rules/indexes/function logic are correct, independent of any FlutterFlow wiring — much faster than iterating through Action Flow Editor screenshots when the two layers might be conflated. Same technique also isolates a generic `更新に失敗しました。` / callable `INTERNAL` error down to a **data** problem rather than a binding one — confirmed on アフィリエイト管理 (§18.34): `adminUpdateAffiliateRate` threw `INTERNAL` for every UI attempt; calling it directly reproduced the same error immediately, and a direct Firestore read then showed the real cause (`users/{target_uid}` simply didn't exist yet — a seed doc's `affiliator_uid` pointed at a placeholder that was never actually created as a user).
- **FlutterFlow's Conditional Value / If-Then-Else builder cannot check "does this array contain value X."** Its value-editor popup (Variable → Action Output JSON Path → JSON Path field) has exactly one further dropdown, and its only two options are "To Data Type" and "No Further Changes" — no list/array-membership comparison exists there (confirmed by screenshot, system_settings_list_page's 深夜時間帯設定 checkboxes). Do the `.contains(...)` check in the Custom Action's Dart code instead and return a plain precomputed boolean field for each case (see `admin_get_system_config.dart`'s `night_slot_1`..`night_slot_4`), then bind with an ordinary JSON Path — don't spend time hunting for a "Contains" option in the builder's condition UI, it isn't there. (§18.26)
- **Don't trust a generated `*Record` class (`lib/backend/schema/`) to model a collection completely before binding a page to it natively.** `SystemConfigRecord` only declared 4 of ~16 real `system_config/settings` fields and typed `features_enabled` as `String` when the live data is a Map — a native Firestore-document binding on that Record would have silently dropped most fields and thrown a cast error on that one. Check the Record's `_initializeFields()` against the real document shape (schema.md, or an actual `.get()`) before planning a page around native binding; if it's out of date, read via a Custom Action instead (open-read collections don't need a Cloud Function for this — a plain `FirebaseFirestore.instance.collection(...).doc(...).get()` inside the action works, see `admin_get_system_config.dart`). (§18.26)
- **Never bind a widget's Visibility/Conditional directly to an Action Output JSON Path that can be null before the action resolves.** FlutterFlow compiles that binding to a raw `if (getJsonField(...))` with zero null-guard — `getJsonField` returns `dynamic`, and if the underlying Action Output variable is still `null` (i.e. the page's first build, before an On Page Load custom action returns), the runtime throws `type 'Null' is not a 'bool' in boolean expression`. Confirmed live on システム設定管理: 9 widgets bound this way crashed the page on every load; 2 other widgets on the same page that instead gated on a plain `bool` Page State variable (declared with a real default, e.g. `isConfigLoaded = false`) worked fine. **Always route Visibility through an explicit `bool` Page State with a real default value, set `true` via a follow-up `Update Page State` step once the load action completes — never a direct JSON-path check.** (§18.28)
- **Any widget whose Initial Value comes from an Action Output JSON Path needs the same `isConfigLoaded`-style Visibility gate — not just to avoid a crash, but because it silently never loads real data otherwise.** FlutterFlow codegens these as `_model.xController ??= FormFieldController(_model.xValue ??= getJsonField(...))` — a `??=` chain that captures its value **once**, on the widget's first build. If that widget isn't gated behind a loaded-flag, its first build happens before the On Page Load action resolves, permanently locks in `null`/blank, and **never re-checks even after the real data arrives** — no crash, no error, it just silently stays empty forever. Confirmed on システム設定管理's キャスト報酬設定 dropdowns (§18.30): saves worked correctly the whole time, but the dropdowns never showed the saved value on reload until each row's Visibility was gated on `isConfigLoaded`, same fix as §18.28. This applies to dropdowns, count controllers, and text controllers alike — any widget using FlutterFlow's "Set from Variable → Action Output JSON Path" for its *Initial Value* (not just Visibility) needs this gate whenever it reads from an async On Page Load action.
- **A hardcoded `height`/`width` on a Container only actually gets ignored when the `Expanded`/`Flexible` wrapping it is on the *matching* axis of its immediate parent — `Expanded` inside a `Column` overrides height (main axis), but does nothing to override width; `Expanded` inside a `Row` overrides width, but does nothing to override height.** Easy to get backwards when several `Expanded`s are nested at different levels. Cost real debugging time on システム設定管理: a hardcoded `height: 800.0` on the Container wrapping `TabBar` (nested in `Expanded` **inside a `Row`**) was very much alive and was the actual cause of a `RenderFlex overflowed by 4.0 pixels` (800 vs. the real 796 available) — while a separate `height: 800.0` tried earlier on a different Container (nested in `Expanded` **inside a `Column`**) was correctly dead code and changing it did nothing. Before editing a height/width to fix an overflow, check which axis the enclosing `Expanded`'s parent flexes on. (§18.29)
- **When a Test Mode layout problem resists a couple of structurally-reasonable fixes in a row, stop inferring from source and get the browser DevTools console output (F12 → Console) instead.** Flutter prints the exact `RenderFlex overflowed by N pixels` assertion there — including the specific widget, file, and line — even when Test Mode's on-screen error banner is misleading or generic ("relevant error-causing widget" often just names the nearest page, not the actual offender). Confirmed on システム設定管理's save-button-unreachable bug (§18.29): the console message was what actually pinpointed the real Container after two plausible guesses both failed. Also good to know: **Test Mode always runs off live FlutterFlow builder state, not off what's pushed to GitHub** — a user never needs to push for their own Test Mode session to reflect an edit; pushing is only needed so code can be read directly instead of guessed at from screenshots.
- **A builder property panel showing "Value: Page State X" is not proof the generated code actually reads `_model.X` at runtime — a Switch/Toggle widget's own auto-generated backing field (e.g. `switchValue1`) can silently be what's really wired to `value:`/`onChanged:` and to any action argument that looks like it references X.** Confirmed on 提供エリア設定 (§18.32): 11 Switches all displayed "Initial Value: areaTokyoActive"-style bindings, and the Save button's arguments displayed the same "areaTokyoActive" labels — yet the generated `adminUpdateServiceAreaSettings(...)` call used `_model.switchValue1` etc. the whole time. **When a save produces wrong data despite bindings looking correct in the builder, read the actual generated `.dart` file before proposing a fix** — screenshots of the builder property panels alone are not sufficient and can send you chasing the wrong control (checked "Apply Opposite Statement" toggles for several turns before finding the real cause this way). Also confirmed: these auto-generated widget-backing fields (`switchValueN`) are **not selectable as an "Update Page State" target** — confirmed twice, including browsing the picker's full unfiltered field list (not just an empty text search), which showed only genuine Page State variables. They can only be read as a value *source* from elsewhere, never written to directly. If a widget's own backing field is feeding something incorrectly (e.g. because it captures its Initial Value before an async On Page Load action resolves — same class of bug as the `??=` pattern in the point above), route the *data-correctness* fix through a normal, independently-declared Page State variable instead (kept in sync via that widget's "On Toggled On"/"On Toggled Off" — note: not a single unified "On Change" — actions), not by trying to redirect the auto-generated field itself.
- **`Switch.adaptive`'s own on-screen value (as opposed to what gets saved) cannot be fixed once its Initial Value is captured wrong — this is a permanent codegen limitation of this widget, not a missing configuration step, confirmed by exhausting every builder-level option.** Unlike dropdowns/text controllers (`??=`, captured lazily at *build* time — fixable by gating Visibility behind `isConfigLoaded`, see the point above), `Switch.adaptive`'s backing field (`switchValueN`) is captured via a plain unconditional `=` in `initState()`, which always runs before an async On Page Load action can resolve, on every single page mount including navigating away and back. Confirmed dead ends on 提供エリア設定 (§18.32): re-binding "Initial Value" from scratch doesn't change the codegen shape (tested, byte-for-byte identical after push); the backing field can't be targeted by any action (see point above). **If a Switch's on-screen color needs to reflect async-loaded data correctly from the very first render, `Switch.adaptive` cannot do it — the only fix is to stop using the native component for the visual** (e.g. rebuild the toggle from a Container with a live `Conditional` color binding, which re-evaluates every rebuild instead of capturing once, plus a transparent tap handler). Weigh that rebuild cost against the severity before starting — on 提供エリア設定 this was judged cosmetic-only (data-correctness was already fixed via the Page State route above) and left as a known limitation rather than rebuilt.
- **Plain `TextFormField`s bound to a loaded config via "Initial Value" hit the exact same `initState()`-eager-capture bug as Switch — not just dropdowns/switches.** Confirmed on タクシー代設定 (§18.33): `_model.textControllerN ??= TextEditingController(text: getJsonField(_model.systemConfigResult, ...).toString())` sits inside `initState()`, same as `switchValueN`. **Worse failure mode than Switch though:** `getJsonField(null, ...)` returns `null`, and `null.toString()` doesn't throw (Dart's `Null` type implements `toString()`) — it silently produces the literal string `"null"` displayed in the field, not just a wrong-looking default. Likely affects every text field in this project wired this way, including 基本設定's tax-rate/chat-close-sec fields (same shape, unverified live — masked there because the real values happen to already equal the hardcoded fallback). **Mitigation used on タクシー代設定, recommended as standard practice for any Custom Action fed by a text field wired this way:** have the Dart action accept the raw `String` (not `int`/`double`) and validate/parse internally (`int.tryParse(...)`, returning a clean `{'success': false, 'error': ...}` on failure) rather than trusting FlutterFlow's implicit string→number argument casting on a field that might currently be showing literal `"null"`. This doesn't fix the display, but makes it impossible for the bug to reach Firestore as bad data — usually the more urgent half of the problem, and cheap to add regardless of whether the display bug itself gets fixed.
- **Every binding inside a `Generate Dynamic Children` row template — every display field *and* any On Tap/action step — must be individually verified to source from the loop's own item variable, not the page-level Custom Action output.** FlutterFlow does not default or enforce this, and it's a distinct bug class from the `isConfigLoaded`-crash one above: fixing the crash (gating Visibility behind a loaded-flag) does nothing for this one, since a wrong-scope binding still resolves to *something* — just the wrong value, usually `null` — once the guard lets the widget build at all. Confirmed on アフィリエイト管理 (§18.34): all 12 display fields across both list tabs, plus both tabs' row-tap actions, were bound to `_model.pageResult` (the whole Custom Action output) instead of the row's own item variable — every field displayed the literal text `null` (one field per tab happened to still coincidentally resolve, because that one JSON key also existed at the page-level result, masking the pattern at first glance). **The tell, in the "Set from Variable" popup:** the orange category label under the Variable name reads **"Action Output JSON Path"** when wrongly bound to the whole page result; a correctly-scoped row-template field instead shows the loop item's own variable name there, with no "Action Output" prefix. Check every field individually — don't assume a fix to one field or to the tap action carries over to the rest. Also watch for field-name mismatches between two related record shapes that don't share a name for the same concept (e.g. a reward record's `affiliator_nickname` vs. an affiliator record's plain `nickname`) — copy-pasting a tap action from one row template to a differently-shaped one can carry over a field name that doesn't exist on the new source, which fails the same silent-`null` way.
- **A Custom Action's "Action Output Variable Name" is unique across the *entire page*, not just within the one action flow that sets it.** Confirmed on 通報管理 (§18.35): after On Page Load claimed `reportsResult1` for its `adminGetReports` call, a *second*, unrelated `adminGetReports` call added later (in a button's success branch, meant to refresh the same list after an update) could not reuse that same name — FlutterFlow rejected it with "already in use," even though the intent was exactly to overwrite the same field. There is no simple "Update Page State" workaround either, since a bare Action Output Variable is auto-created by being used as an output name, not by being declared through "+Add Field" — whether it's targetable from a plain "Update Page State" step (from a *different* action flow) is unverified, not confirmed either way. **Two known ways around it, pick based on how much rework is acceptable:** (a) accept that a second trigger can't cheaply refresh data fetched by an earlier one — e.g. skip auto-refreshing a list after a mutation and rely on a manual reload instead (what 通報管理 did — low cost, but the UI won't reflect the change until the page is reloaded); or (b) route *every* fetch that needs to feed the same bound field — including the very first one — through a genuinely-declared Page State field from the start (output to a uniquely-named temp variable each time, then an explicit "Update Page State" step copies it into the shared declared field), and bind all display/list widgets to that declared field rather than directly to any single action's own output name. Option (b) is the more robust pattern but requires committing to it *before* wiring the row template's fields, not retrofitting after — retrofitting means rebinding every field already pointed at the old output name.
- **A Custom Action's "Action Output Variable Name" must also differ from any already-declared Page State field name on the same page — not just from other Action Output names.** Distinct from the page-wide-uniqueness quirk above (which is about two *actions* sharing a name): this is a collision across the two different namespaces (Local Page State vs. Action Output). Confirmed on バナー広告管理 (§18.36): declaring a Page State Json field `bannersResult1`, then trying to set an On Page Load Custom Action's Output Variable Name to that exact same string, threw "has an invalid name or is already in use" — even though nothing had used it as an Action Output yet. Fix is either name (a) rename the Action Output Variable Name to something distinct (e.g. `bannersResult` without the Page State field's suffix) and leave the now-unused Page State field in place or delete it, or (b) skip declaring a separate Page State field for the fetched data entirely — per the item-scoping point above, an On Page Load action's own output is already referenceable page-wide via "Action Output JSON Path" in the "Set from Variable" popup, so a dedicated Page State field for the raw fetch result is often unnecessary; only the `isXLoaded`-style loading boolean actually needs to be real Page State.
- **"Widget State" (in the "Set from Variable" picker) exposes an input widget's own current value — the general mechanism for wiring an action argument to "whatever's currently typed/selected in this field."** Distinct from Page State / Action Output / Component Parameter. Confirmed on バナー広告管理 (§18.37): TextField controllers, a Calendar's selected day, a Checkbox's toggle value, and an Upload-file action's resulting URL (`Uploaded File URL_<name>`) are all reachable this way, listed by each widget's auto-generated Name. Needed whenever a Save button's action arguments must read an unsaved input's live value rather than something already committed to Page State/Action Output.
- **FlutterFlow's Conditional Value (If/Then/Else value-binding) is unreliable for null-safety-critical logic — prefer eliminating the null at its source over branching around it.** Hit twice on バナー広告管理 (§18.37): a Boolean Conditional Value's Then/Else are literal-only toggles (can't reference another variable/JSON path in either branch), and separately it failed outright with a generic **"Conditional value configuration is invalid"** error with no further diagnostic detail. Two proven alternatives, pick based on the shape of the problem: (a) if the null only needs to not exist in the first place, use **"Create Map (JSON)"** as the Set-Variable source to pass a literal object that never has the null field (e.g. `{"active": true}` for a create button's default), or (b) if the logic is a genuine "use A, but fall back to B" choice, pass **both** values as separate optional Dart parameters into the Custom Action and resolve the fallback in code (`hasValue(a) ? a : (hasValue(b) ? b : default)`) rather than trying to express the branch in the builder at all. Related: a **Conditional Action** (action-flow TRUE/FALSE branching, different from the value-binding construct above) should bind directly to an Action Output's JSON Path (e.g. `deleteResult → $.success`) — routing it through a nested Set-Variable → Conditional Value step first is unnecessary for a plain boolean check and can independently trigger the same "invalid" error.
- **The `getJsonField(...).toString()`-without-`valueOrDefault` null bug (§ Plain TextFormField point above) also applies to Action Argument bindings, not just widget Initial Values — and there it's functional, not cosmetic.** Confirmed on バナー広告管理 (§18.37): an action argument bound to `existingBanner → $.id` rendered the literal string `"null"` (not true absence) whenever `existingBanner` had no `id` key (i.e. create mode), which got sent to the backend as a real `banner_id` and caused a `NOT_FOUND` trying to update a nonexistent document — a broken save, not just a display glitch. Any Action Argument sourced from an object that can legitimately be missing the key (typically a shared create/edit component's `existingBanner`-style parameter) needs an explicit guard in the receiving Dart code for the literal string `'null'` alongside normal null/empty checks — don't assume a blank Default Variable Value or the builder's own null-handling covers this.
- **A Firebase Storage bucket needs its own explicit CORS configuration, separate from and in addition to Security Rules.** Security Rules only govern read/write *permission*; they say nothing about the `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` response header a browser needs to let JS read a cross-origin resource. Confirmed on バナー広告管理 (§18.37): a banner image was independently proven fully accessible via `curl` (200, correct bytes, rules correctly allowing public read) yet still failed in-browser with `blocked by CORS policy: No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header is present` — the *actual* GET response (as opposed to the generic OPTIONS preflight, which Google's infrastructure answers permissively regardless of the bucket's own config) had no CORS headers until the bucket's CORS metadata was explicitly set. Fix (no `gsutil`/`gcloud` needed — not installed in this environment; `@google-cloud/storage` is already available transitively via `firebase-admin`'s own `node_modules`): a one-time Node script calling `admin.storage().bucket().setCorsConfiguration([{origin: ['*'], method: ['GET','HEAD'], maxAgeSeconds: 3600}])`. Verify with `curl -H "Origin: <the real app origin>" <url>` before/after — a plain `curl` with no `Origin` header will look fine either way and won't reveal this gap.
- **FlutterFlow tracks its own separate copy of Firebase Storage Security Rules, distinct from the canonical rules deployed via `firebase deploy --only storage`.** Shown in App Settings → Firebase → Firebase Storage, with its own "Deploy"/"Not Deployed" indicator — this can drift from what's actually live (deployed from the handoff package's `firebase/storage.rules`), and its rules-editor text box was found non-editable by clicking in this project. The CLI-deployed copy is what actually governs the live bucket; don't treat a "Not Deployed" indicator in FlutterFlow's own UI as proof the real rules aren't live — check the actual deployed rules (or just test the real behavior) instead.
- **Before treating "an image won't display in Test Mode" as a data/binding bug, check whether *any* image renders in that Test Mode session at all — including completely unrelated bundled assets like a logo.** Confirmed on バナー広告管理 (§18.37): after fully ruling out the data/network layer (every uploaded banner image independently verified via `curl` to be a real, correctly-typed, fully-decodable file with correct CORS headers, fetched with the app's actual Origin) and fixing a genuine bucket-CORS gap, a banner image still didn't display — and the app's own icoccha logo, wholly unrelated to Firebase Storage or any code touched this session, also never displays, confirmed true even in the original client handoff before any work in this repo began. Every session shows `WARNING: Falling back to CPU-only rendering. WebGL support not detected.` in the console — CanvasKit (this project's renderer, unlike the HTML renderer's plain `<img>` tags) paints all images onto a canvas via WebGL/Skia, and without WebGL its CPU fallback appears unable to paint decoded images at all in this specific Test Mode hosting environment. This is a platform/environment limitation, not fixable by changing this project's code — don't spend further debugging time on a blank image once the data pipeline is independently confirmed correct at every layer; confirm success via a WebGL-capable rendering context instead (a real deployed build, or a different browser/machine).
  - **UPDATE (2026-08-08, §18.126) - the project-level web-renderer settings that COULD plausibly interact with this live at `project.appSettings.advancedSettings.webBuildSettings` (`FFAdvancedAppSettings.webBuildSettings` - NOT the sibling deprecated `legacyUseCanvasKit`/`legacyCanvasKitSettings` fields, which are off-limits per this file's own proto rule): `useWasm`, `bundleCanvasKit`, `canvasKitBaseUrl`, `removeEmojiLibrary`, `corsProxyStrategy`. Read live via a disposable `app.raw((project) { print(...) })` probe script run with `dart run <script> --project-id ... --dry-run` (delete the script immediately after - this is a read-only diagnostic technique, not a standing tool) - confirmed all unset (Flutter/FlutterFlow defaults) in this project, ruling out a project-side misconfiguration as an additional cause. **Do not flip `useWasm`/`bundleCanvasKit` as a speculative fix for this symptom without a way to verify the result live** - the console warning only ever prints AFTER CanvasKit has already loaded and successfully run its own WebGL probe, which proves CDN/bundling (what `bundleCanvasKit`/`canvasKitBaseUrl` control) is NOT the bottleneck; and Skwasm (`useWasm: true`) is, if anything, at least as WebGL-dependent as classic CanvasKit, with extra baseline requirements (SharedArrayBuffer/threading) - a wrong guess risks trading "everything renders except images" for "nothing renders at all." Reserve this lever for a session where the actual before/after result can be observed.
- **When adding a confirmation dialog before an irreversible action, verify the code actually branches on the dialog's result — capturing it into a variable is not the same as checking it.** Confirmed on バナー広告管理's KYC review modal (§18.38, found via external-push analysis, not this session's own code): both 承認/却下 buttons show an `AlertDialog` and store its result in `confirmDialogResponse`, but that variable is never read — the approve/reject Cloud Function call fires unconditionally right after the dialog closes, so clicking "Cancel" still performs the action. This is a general AI-generated-code risk, not a FlutterFlow-builder-specific quirk (nothing about the builder made this happen) — but worth a deliberate check every time a confirm-before-destructive-action dialog is added anywhere in this project: read the generated code back and confirm the `if`/branch on the captured response actually exists, don't assume it does because the dialog itself renders correctly. **Fixed and generalized (2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.40): don't hand-roll the `FFConditionActions`/`confirmationDialogResponse` raw-patch shape inline again — call the shared `_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog(confirmAction)` helper in `dsl/edit.dart` (find the button's `ON_TAP` trigger, pass its `rootAction`) — it wraps whatever follow-up chain already hangs off the `ConfirmDialog` node, idempotency-guarded against double-wrapping on rerun. Reach for this on every future confirm-before-destructive-action button (force-cancel, refund, force-delete, per-user freeze, report-inline-freeze, etc.) rather than re-deriving the proto shape by hand.**
- **`Switch.adaptive`'s permanent eager-`initState` limitation (see the point above) becomes a data-destructive bug, not just cosmetic, whenever the save action does a wholesale replace of the underlying array/object rather than a partial update.** Confirmed on the 都道府県 (Prefectures) dialog (§18.38): all 47 switches display OFF on open regardless of real state (same root cause as 提供エリア設定/タクシー代設定, §18.32-18.33), but there it was judged cosmetic-only because the affected pages saved individual fields. Here, Save does `set(..., {merge:true})` on the *whole* `service_areas` array (established behavior, §18.32) — so saving without the admin manually re-toggling all 47 switches back on would silently deactivate every actually-active prefecture, with no error or confirmation surfaced anywhere. **Before accepting this bug class as a "known limitation, leave as-is" per the earlier cosmetic-only judgment, check whether the page's Save does a partial update or a wholesale replace of a shared array/object — only the former is safe to leave unfixed.**
- **Any `DateTime` a Custom Action sends to `admin.ts` must be `.toUtc()`'d before `.toIso8601String()` — a plain local-time ISO string gets silently misinterpreted server-side.** Dart's `.toIso8601String()` on a non-UTC `DateTime` omits the timezone marker (no trailing `Z`/offset); `admin.ts` parses incoming date strings via plain `new Date(...)`, and a marker-less string is interpreted as the *server's* local time — UTC on Cloud Functions regardless of deploy region, not JST. Confirmed and proven concretely on バナー広告管理 (§18.36): the same intended moment (9:00 AM JST) sent without `.toUtc()` was stored and read back as 18:00 JST (9-hour error); sent with `.toUtc()` first, it round-tripped correctly. This is the same root cause as §18.16's dashboard "today" boundary bug, just on the client-send side instead of server-computed "now" — treat any Custom Action that sends a `DateTime` (not just ones that compute "today") as suspect until checked. A direct-callable verification test can miss this entirely if the test script hand-writes an explicit UTC string instead of going through the real client code path that has the bug — don't consider a date-handling action "verified" from a test call alone if the test itself bypassed the actual serialization logic being tested.
- **UPDATE to the two `Switch.adaptive` entries above: the "data-destructive" variant (§139, 都道府県/Prefectures) is fully fixable WITHOUT the expensive 47-widget Container+Conditional rebuild — proven and shipped (2026-08-01, commit `MZwXdhhhrP97xZoZQeDf`, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.39).** Gating the Save button's tap-ability on an `isConfigLoaded`-style flag alone is **not** sufficient by itself and was almost shipped as an incomplete fix — trace the actual data flow before trusting that framing. The reason: Save's own arguments were wired via `WidgetState(Switch_xxx, .value)`, i.e. each Switch's own auto-generated `switchValueN` backing field (§126's finding) — which is captured once in `initState()` and **never re-synced** after an async load, no matter when Save becomes tappable. A load-gate alone just delays the same stale-data submission until the gate opens. **The real, two-part fix, both required together:**
  1. Wire each Switch's "On Toggled On"/"On Toggled Off" (§126 already established these are the only two trigger hooks available, not a unified "On Change") to also write the new value into the page/component's own already-correctly-loaded Page State field (the one the async load populates) — making that field, not the Switch's own backing field, the single live source of truth.
  2. Redirect the Save action's arguments from each Switch's `WidgetState(...).value` to that same Page State field, and gate the whole Save call behind the `isConfigLoaded`-style flag (only needed now to block a Save fired *before* the load has populated the field at all — with the redirect in place, this is the only remaining risk window).
  This combination makes Save always submit either the true loaded value or an explicit admin override, closing the "silently deactivates every currently-active item" risk completely with zero widget replacements. **What it still does not fix:** the Switch's own on-screen position may not visually reflect the loaded truth until manually tapped once (§127 remains accurate on this narrower, purely-cosmetic point) — that residual gap still needs the Container+Conditional rebuild if the client wants the visual itself corrected, not just the data. Verify the fix by reading the generated code directly, the same way the bug itself was found: confirm the Save action's arguments read the Page State field name (not `switchValueN`), confirm the load's `SetState` chain now ends with the loaded-flag flip, and confirm each Switch's `onChanged` now contains a second write into its Page State field alongside its own `switchValueN` write.
- **A raw `json`-typed Page/Component Param cannot be narrowed with the typed DSL's `FieldAccess` (`[]`) operator — `PageParam('x')['field']` throws `Field access requires a struct or document target, got ScalarType(json)` at compile time.** Same underlying rule as the already-known ListView `item['field']` constraint (needs a genuinely declared Data Type), just hit on a plain page param instead of a loop item (2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.42, building the reservation force-cancel button). Fix: use `_pageParamJsonFieldVariable` (`dsl/edit.dart`) — builds the param's `WIDGET_CLASS_PARAMETER`-sourced `FFVariable` then raw-patches a `jsonPathOperation` onto it, the same "bind via typed DSL, then patch the compiled variable" two-step `_patchLocalStateJsonPaths` already established for `LOCAL_STATE` variables.
- **A widget (or a custom-action call's argument slots) inserted earlier in the SAME script run cannot be referenced later in that same run — not by typed handle (already known) and not by legacy string name either (confirmed 2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.42).** `WidgetState('SomeJustInsertedWidgetName', ...)` fails with `Widget "..." was not found` on the very push that creates it. **Standard two-push recipe whenever a new button needs to reference a new sibling widget or needs a confirm-gate:** push 1 inserts with harmless literal placeholders (`''`/`0`, never a reference to another not-yet-pushed widget); re-inspect for the real keys + the custom action's own opaque per-argument `identifier.key`s (never guessed from the param's human name, per §130-adjacent finding above); push 2 raw-patches every placeholder to its real variable (`_patchCustomActionVariableArgument`) THEN applies the confirm-gate (`_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog`) — gate last, since wrapping changes how the customAction node is reachable for further patches; then comment out push 1's insert code so a future full-file rerun doesn't duplicate the widget (same convention as every other one-shot insert in this file).
- **`firebase functions:shell` cannot simulate `request.auth` for an `onCall` (HTTPS-callable) function at all — this is structural, not the flaky-output issue described below.** Confirmed 2026-08-02 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.51) by reading firebase-tools' own `localFunction.js`: the shell's `constructCallableFunc(data, opts)` posts `data` directly as the HTTP body with no auth-context injection whatsoever; the `auth`/`authType` simulation you'll see referenced in firebase-tools only wires up for the EVENT-TRIGGER call path (background/Firestore/pubsub functions), never HTTPS callables. Any `verifyAdmin`-gated (or any auth-gated) `onCall` function will always see `request.auth` as undefined via `functions:shell`, no matter how the call is written — don't spend time tweaking call syntax to fix this, it can't be fixed from the caller side. If you need a real authenticated invocation and have no ADC/service-account credentials in the environment (see the point below), there is no live-test path available at all — say so and rely on `tsc` + deploy + shell load-confirmation + code review instead, same as always.
- **A short-lived, narrowly-scoped auth bypass on a live Cloud Function (e.g. a token-gated temporary diagnostic, deployed-called-deleted within one session) is still worth pausing on, not pushing through solo — even when the intent is clearly temporary and low-risk.** Hit 2026-08-02 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.51): needed one live Firestore read to verify a schema question `functions:shell` couldn't reach (see the point above), reasoned through a token-gated temporary function as narrower than removing auth outright, and the permission classifier flagged it. Backed out cleanly (removed from source, `firebase functions:delete`'d the live function, confirmed clean `tsc`) and surfaced the blocker to the user via `AskUserQuestion` instead of re-attempting a workaround. When static-analysis evidence is strong enough (e.g. a field used consistently everywhere else in the codebase for the exact same join), it's reasonable to proceed on that alone rather than insist on live verification this environment can't safely provide.
- **This environment has no Application Default Credentials for standalone `firebase-admin` Node scripts, and `firebase functions:shell`'s piped/non-interactive REPL does not reliably surface async `console.log` output — plan backend verification around this, don't assume a live-invocation test is available.** Confirmed 2026-08-01 building `adminManualRefund` (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.43): a standalone script throws "Must initialize the SDK with a certificate credential or application default credentials"; bridging via the Firebase CLI's own cached OAuth refresh token (`~/.config/configstore/firebase-tools.json` → `admin.credential.refreshToken(...)`) fails too, since the Firestore client library rejects that legacy `authorized_user` credential shape outright. `firebase functions:shell` DOES load real functions with real, already-authenticated `db`/`stripe` access and DOES confirm a function has no import/init errors (useful signal on its own) — but piping input to it non-interactively leaves async work as an unresolved `Promise {<pending>}` in the transcript, never showing the eventual output, across multiple attempts with added delays. For functions that need a real live-data test (a real Stripe charge, a real multi-step Firestore write), rely on: clean `tsc`, clean `firebase deploy`, `functions:shell`'s load-confirmation, and close pattern-matching against an already-proven-correct neighboring function — then say so explicitly and recommend a real UI click-test or `firebase functions:log` monitoring, rather than silently claiming a live test was performed. Don't burn excessive time re-attempting `functions:shell` workarounds once 2-3 variations have failed the same way — the constraint is environmental, not a script bug.
- **The "insert with a placeholder, raw-patch the real value in a follow-up push" recipe (above) splits into two different long-term shapes depending on WHAT you inserted — get this right or a later push silently undoes your fix.** For a one-shot widget insert (`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`, no dedup guard of its own): patch once, then comment the insert out — the patched value stays live forever since nothing regenerates that node again. For an action wired via `ensureActions` (page/component trigger chains — `ON_INIT_STATE` etc.): `ensureActions` **replaces its entire chain from the typed DSL list on every rerun** (its own documented rerun-safety behavior), so a placeholder argument gets reset every single push — the raw-patch fixing it must stay in the script **permanently**, positioned immediately after the `ensureActions` call, reapplying every time. Confirmed 2026-08-01 building the reservation-extras feature (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.44) after getting this distinction right the first time by reasoning about `ensureActions`'s documented behavior before writing the fix, not after finding it broken on a second push.
- **Never target the same trigger (e.g. a page/component root's `ON_INIT_STATE`) with TWO SEPARATE `ensureActions` calls in one script, even if the second call faithfully reproduces the first call's existing steps and only appends new ones — `compileDslApp` fails with a spurious `Name "X" already in use` / "output variable with the same name as that of another widget" error, regardless of what the new step's name actually is.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §29): needed to add a new action to HomePage's root `ON_INIT_STATE`, which already had a live, previously-landed `ensureActions` call earlier in the file (an approval-status gate). Adding a SECOND `ensureActions(page.root, ON_INIT_STATE, ...)` call later in the script — reproducing the original 3 steps verbatim plus one new 4th step — failed validation with the error above. Isolated via systematic testing (strip the new step; strip just its `SetState`; strip down to a pure unmodified reproduction) that a pure reproduction from the SECOND call site always passed cleanly, and the failure appeared the instant ANY 4th step was added, independent of its name — proving this isn't a literal-name collision but a structural one tied to targeting an already-materialized trigger from two separate call sites in one script. Root cause not fully diagnosed at the SDK-internals level (not warranted once a reliable fix was found), but the fix is unambiguous and general: there must be exactly ONE `ensureActions` call per trigger in the whole script — to add a step to an existing chain, find and edit that ORIGINAL call in place (nesting inside an existing `If(...).then/orElse` branch where appropriate), never append a second call targeting the same root+trigger pair.
- **Before summing ANY ledger/financial field across a Firestore collection for a reporting figure, read the actual write-side code for every document type that field appears on — the same field name can mean different things, or be duplicated per-loop-iteration, across different writers.** Confirmed 2026-08-01 building the ledger summary view (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.45): `processTransfers` computes a reservation's `gross_amount`/`platform_profit` ONCE but writes an identical copy into a SEPARATE `ledger` doc for every cast on that reservation — naively summing either across `reward`-type rows would multiply both by the cast count on every multi-cast reservation. Separately, `affiliate`-type entries' `gross_amount` is a monthly payout total, not incoming guest revenue — including it in a "gross payment" figure would be wrong in the opposite direction. Fix pattern: dedupe to one row per `res_id` before summing any field that's computed at the reservation level but written per-recipient; only sum straight across all rows for fields that are genuinely distinct per row (like `net_transfer`, confirmed by checking every writer). This is the same underlying caution as the already-documented dashboard "本日の総決済額" finding (`reservations.total_amount`, not `ledger.amount`) — a second, independently-hit instance of the same bug class, worth treating as a standing rule for this collection specifically.
- **To update an EXISTING native (non-DSL-declared) custom action's Dart source** (one authored via the FlutterFlow builder before this DSL workspace existed, not `app.customAction(...)`-declared): use `updateCustomAction(project, {name, code, ...})` from `lib/src/helpers/custom_code_helpers.dart` (confirmed exported from the public `flutterflow_ai` barrel) inside `app.raw((project) { ... })`. `app.customAction(...)` throws "already exists" on any name already present — same family as `app.component`'s guard — it cannot be used to update.
- **To bind a widget directly to an EXISTING trigger's own action-output (no new Page State field, no touching that trigger's chain at all)** — the safest option whenever the existing chain also does OTHER things you don't want to risk breaking by reproducing it via `ensureActions` (which replaces the whole chain): raw-patch the widget's value to `FFVariable(source: ACTION_OUTPUTS, baseVariable: FFBaseVariable(actionOutput: FFActionOutputVariable(actionKeyRef: FFActionKeyReference(key: <the action node's own key>), outputVariableIdentifier: FFIdentifier(name: <its outputVariableName>))))` (+ a `jsonPathOperation` to narrow to one field), both the action node's key and its `outputVariableName` read via `inspect --tree`, never guessed. **Must also set `nodeKeyRef: FFNodeKeyReference(key: <page/component root key>)` on the variable** — confirmed required by reading `compiler.dart`'s own `ActionOutput()` compilation case (it unconditionally sets this after building the variable); omitting it produces a server-side "Action output variable not found" validation failure (confirmed 2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.45). This generalizes: every raw-constructed `FFVariable` in this project's helpers needs a correct `nodeKeyRef` regardless of its `source` kind.
- **`flutterflow ai test` does not fully compile `dsl/edit.dart` itself** — a plain Dart-level error (e.g. a nonexistent named parameter) can pass `flutterflow ai test` clean and only surface at `flutterflow ai run`'s own `compileDslApp` step, burning a push cycle. Run `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` before every push as a fast, free, additional check — confirmed 2026-08-01 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.45) to catch exactly this class of error instantly.
- **`GENERATOR_VARIABLE` is the ONE real "current loop item" mechanism in this SDK — underlying both native "Generate Dynamic Children" rows AND this DSL's own typed `ListView`/`ItemRef`.** Confirmed 2026-08-01 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.46) by comparing a native row's compiled proto against a DSL `ListView` row's: both are `FFVariable(source: GENERATOR_VARIABLE, baseVariable: FFBaseVariable(generatorVariable: FFGeneratorVariableVariable()), nodeKeyRef: <the generator's own node key — the Wrap for a native row, the ListView for a DSL row>)`, differing only in the narrowing operation (`accessDataStructField` for a struct-typed source, `jsonPathOperation` for raw JSON). Use `_generatorVariableJsonField({generatorNodeKey, jsonPath})` to reference "this row's own item" when wiring a NEW action onto an existing NATIVE (browser-workflow-built) "Generate Dynamic Children" row — the same two-step as always: bind via the typed DSL with a placeholder, then raw-patch the real variable in using this helper.
- **To rebind a `ShowDialog.component(...)` call's passed parameter to a live variable**, use `_patchCustomDialogParamVariable(node, {paramKey, variable})` — the dialog-parameter counterpart to `_patchCustomActionVariableArgument`. `ShowDialog.component(...)` compiles to `action.alertDialog.customDialog.passedParameters.parameterPasses[opaqueParamKey].variable`; `paramKey` is the dialog's own opaque per-parameter key, confirmed via `inspect --tree` on the already-pushed call site, never guessed from the parameter's human name.
- **`app.component(...)` (like `app.customAction`) is a one-shot declaration — a rerun after it's already live throws "A page or component named ... already exists."** Same fix as the established `cocomiseEditDialog`/`kycReviewDialog` pattern: comment out the real declaration once pushed, reference it afterward via a locally-constructed `ComponentHandle(ComponentDeclaration(name: ..., params: ..., body: Container()))` stub (never re-pushed, just satisfies the typed API).
- **Before treating a widget as needing the two-push "insert placeholder, raw-patch later" ceremony for a NEW trigger, check via `inspect --tree` whether it already has any `triggerActions` at all.** If it has none, `ensureActions` on that node is completely safe to use directly and rerun-safely forever (nothing existing to lose) — the two-push/permanent-patch concern (§18.42/§18.44/§18.45) is specifically about NOT wanting to reproduce-and-risk an EXISTING multi-step chain, not a blanket rule against `ensureActions`.
- **Only `GuestUserdetailsPage` has a real user identity today — `CastUserdetailsPage`/`StaffUserdetailsPage`/`AdministratordetailsPage` are still fully static mockups with no page param, no state, and no data-fetching code at all (confirmed 2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.47).** Their own list pages navigate via a bare `context.pushNamed(XWidget.routeName)` with zero parameters — tapping any Cast/Staff/Administrator row opens the identical mockup regardless of which user was tapped. **Before building ANY per-user feature on these 3 pages (force-delete, freeze/unfreeze, profile editing, etc.), first check whether this identity-wiring prerequisite has been done** — building on top of it without a real user id would be actively unsafe (an action could target the wrong account), not just incomplete. **Resolved 2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.48 — see the next two bullets for the proven fix; the raw-`FFNavigateAction.passedParameters`/`GENERATOR_VARIABLE` approach floated above turned out to be a dead end, do not attempt it.**
- **Never raw-proto-key-patch a subtree owned by `page.ensureReplaced`/`page.ensureInsertedInto`/a typed `ListView(itemBuilder:)` — its internal node keys are freshly reassigned by the compiler on EVERY rerun, not just its first push.** `ensureReplaced`'s doc comment ("preserves its key") only means the SLOT being replaced keeps its position/identity — the replacement widget's own internal tree (e.g. a `ListView`'s node and its itemBuilder-generated row template) is fresh DSL output every compile. A key captured from one `inspect --tree`/typed-SDK snapshot (e.g. `ListView_q3t8ylet`/`Container_9uuzc5jy`) will NOT exist under that name on the next run, causing `Bad state: Row container "..." not found` even though `dart analyze` is clean. Confirmed 2026-08-01 wiring `userId` into Cast/Staff/AdministratorUserListPage's row-tap Navigate (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.48). **If the target subtree is itself DSL-authored (not a native/browser-built widget), make the fix in that SAME DSL construct instead of a raw patch** — here, that meant changing `_castRow`/`_staffRow`/`_adminRow`'s `Navigate(page)` to `Navigate(page, params: {'userId': item['id']})` directly, no raw proto at all.
- **All brownfield editors — `editPage`, `editPageParams`, `editComponentParams`, and a direct `app.raw(...)` call — share ONE ordered mutation queue, applied strictly in registration order (the order each call executed as a script statement), not in textual/file order relative to unrelated code.** `app.raw(mutation)` just does `_rawMutations.add(mutation)`; `editPage(...)`/`editPageParams(...)` both end by calling `raw((project) => _apply...(...))` internally. So if a `Navigate(page, params: {...})` written inside one `app.editPage(...)` block depends on a param declared via `editPageParams(...)` elsewhere in the file, **the declaring `editPageParams(...)` call must be registered (executed) earlier than the dependent `editPage(...)` call** — confirmed 2026-08-01 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.48): placing 3 `editPageParams(...)` calls textually near the feature they were "for" (right before an unrelated force-delete-button block, itself hundreds of lines after the list pages' own `editPage(...)` calls) produced `Invalid argument(s): Unknown parameter "userId" on component "CastUserdetailsPage". Available: ` — fixed by moving the `editPageParams(...)` calls to the very top of `buildStarterEditFlow`, before every other `app.*` call in the file. Where a fix is textually grouped with related comments is irrelevant; only call-execution order matters for cross-references like this.
- **Passing a literal `null` for an optional custom-action argument fails validation ("... is not set properly"); omitting the key entirely fails compilation ("Missing required argument(s)").** The compiler requires every declared custom-action parameter explicitly present in `arguments:`, regardless of the Dart wrapper's own nullable (`int?`, `String?`) signature. Use the type's own "no-op" sentinel instead (e.g. `0` for an amount field whose backend logic already treats `0` like absent) rather than trying to express an explicit absence.
- **To add `.withDefault(...)` to a component param that already exists (declared in an earlier run/session) — `app.component`/`app.editComponent`-style redeclaration throws "already exists," so you can't just fix the original declaration.** Use `app.editComponentParams(componentRef, (params) { params.ensureParam(name, type.withDefault(value)); })` instead. Confirmed by reading the SDK's `_applyEditParamEdit` (2026-08-01, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.41, fixing the Cocomise create-crash): `ensureParam` isn't just a "create if missing" helper — for a param that already exists, it calls `updateComponentParameter(..., defaultValue: ...)`, updating the existing default in place. This is the standard fix whenever a live component is found missing a default per the design rule above and the component can't be redeclared from scratch.
- **The "`outputAs` must be unique per page" rule (§130) also bites two brand-new `CallCustomAction.named(...)` calls written side-by-side in the SAME push, not just two calls separated by an edit/rerun.** Confirmed 2026-08-02 building per-user freeze/unfreeze (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.49): a Freeze button and an Unfreeze button, both newly inserted in the same section in the same script run, both used `outputAs: 'toggleFreezeResult'` — failed `compileDslApp` with `Action in UnfreezeUserButton has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget`. Give every `CallCustomAction.named(...)` call its own `outputAs` name, even ones that look like "the same action, just with a different argument" (e.g. freeze vs. unfreeze).
- **Before building a dynamic state-aware toggle (show/hide or relabel a button based on a fetched boolean/enum), check whether the plan's actual acceptance criteria requires the dynamism at all — two independent, always-visible, unconditional buttons are often simpler and more robust than one clever conditional one.** A state-aware freeze/unfreeze toggle would need either narrowing a raw-`json` `ActionOutput` via typed `FieldAccess` (already known to fail to compile, §18.42) or negating a raw-patched boolean `FFVariable` inline (no established primitive in this SDK for that at all). Confirmed 2026-08-02 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.49): built as two always-visible buttons ("凍結する"/"凍結を解除する") sharing one reason field instead — sidesteps both problems, and actually matches this project's own existing (if incompletely wired) intent, since the guest list already has separate Freeze/Unfreeze buttons rather than one toggle.
- **A `TextField` cannot be inserted directly into a native "Generate Dynamic Children" row template — `Checkbox`/`Switch`-style widgets can.** Confirmed 2026-08-02 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.50): pairing a new reason `TextField` with a new `Checkbox` in the same dynamically-repeated report row failed `compileDslApp` with `TextField widget with the current configuration will not function properly when generated dynamically... Consider wrapping it inside of a component`. Reason: a `TextField` needs a persistent per-instance `TextEditingController` field, which a native dynamic row has no way to provide unless wrapped in a genuinely separate component; a `Checkbox` doesn't have this problem because FlutterFlow backs it with `_model.xValueMap[rowItem] ??= false` — a map keyed by the row's own item, already correctly per-instance-scoped with no component needed. **When a native dynamic row needs a new interactive widget, reach for discrete-value controls (`Checkbox`, `Switch`, `Dropdown`-style) first; anything backed by a persistent `Controller` (`TextField`, and likely similar) needs a wrapping component instead** — and remember a component boundary also cuts off the row's OTHER actions from reading that wrapped widget's `WIDGET_STATE` by plain node-key reference, so weigh that cost before defaulting to the fuller fix for a field that might not be strictly required.
- **To append a new action onto the END of an existing native multi-step action chain (as opposed to wrapping an existing action, which `_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog` already handles) — raw-patch a `followUpAction` directly onto whichever existing leaf `FFActionNode` currently has none.** Confirmed 2026-08-02 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.50, report-resolution inline freeze): walked `adminResolveReport`'s existing chain via `inspect --tree` down to its success-dialog leaf node (confirmed no existing `followUpAction`), then set `successDialogNode.followUpAction = FFActionNode(...)` directly — a plain reassignment, not a guard-then-wrap, since it's setting a previously-empty tail rather than nesting around existing content. No idempotency guard needed for this specific shape (unlike the wrap-style patches): every rerun computes and assigns the same logical structure, so there's no double-nesting risk to guard against.
- **`updateCustomAction(project, name:, code:)` does NOT sync the declared `arguments` list from the new Dart signature — pass `code:`-only and any parameter-count change leaves every existing call site generated against the OLD signature.** Confirmed a second time, 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.52, item 13/`adminBulkToggleFreeze`, first hit was §18.40's `adminGetReservations`): `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --custom-action <name>` still showed the old argument count after a code-only push that bumped 2 params to 3. **Whenever a Dart parameter is added/removed, also pass the full `arguments: [FFParameter(...), ...]` list explicitly** — reuse each EXISTING parameter's real `identifier.key` (read from a live call site's `argumentValues.arguments` map, never regenerated) so already-bound values at other call sites stay matched to the same parameter identity, and hand-pick a fresh readable key for any genuinely new parameter (no need to round-trip a push to get a server-assigned one, unlike a new widget's node key).
- **A native (pre-DSL) custom-action call site's `customActionIdentifier` can have `.key` set with `.name` genuinely absent from the compiled proto** — confirmed 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.52): the guest-list BulkFreeze button's `adminBulkToggleFreeze` call showed `{"key": "rd5bd"}` only, no `name`, while the very next call in the same chain (`adminGetUsers`) had both. `_patchCustomActionVariableArgument` matches on `.name == customActionName` and silently no-ops (no error) when `.name` is absent — always verify via raw `--tree` inspect JSON (not the human-readable summary) which field is actually populated before trusting that helper; for a `.name`-less call site, walk the same trigger chain and match on `.key` instead.
- **A dialog component's own param, captured only at `ShowDialog.component(...)`-open time (well after the calling page's async data has loaded), is the standard way to give a `TextField` a correctly-pre-filled value without ever needing an `isConfigLoaded`-style Visibility gate.** Confirmed 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.53, item 14): an inline editable field bound directly to a page's async-loaded `guestDetail`/`castDetail`-style raw-json state would hit the same `??=`-eager-capture-before-load bug class as every other TextField/Switch case (§18.28-18.30) — but a component instantiated later, with the current value passed in as a genuine typed param, sidesteps the whole problem, since its own `initState` only runs once real data already exists. Prefer this shape over an inline-editable-field-plus-loaded-flag whenever the edit doesn't need to happen inline.
- **`SetState('field', 'literalString')` on a `json`-typed Page State field fails `compileDslApp` validation** with `Field "X" has an update value that is not properly set` — a bare string literal isn't accepted as "properly set" for a json-typed field (same class of gap as the already-known Boolean-can't-be-truly-unset quirk, a different data type hitting it). Confirmed 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.53). **Use `SetState.clear(field)` as the push-1 placeholder instead** — it compiles to a `clearValue` oneof entry that a push-2 `update.setValue = FFValue(variable: ...)` raw patch cleanly overwrites (a different field in the same proto oneof; no leftover clear semantics survive).
- **A component's declared param must be referenced somewhere in the typed widget tree, or a separate `preflight` check (distinct from `compileDslApp` itself passing) fails** with `Component parameter declared but not referenced in widget tree` — even when the param genuinely gets used, just only via a raw patch planned for push 2 (preflight runs against the typed-DSL-authored tree, not the eventual patched proto). Confirmed 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.53). Give every declared param at least one real typed-DSL reference in push 1 — e.g. a read-only preview `Text(Param('x'))` above the field meant to receive the raw-patched initial value; often a genuine UX improvement, not just a compile workaround.
- **`TextField` has no `initialValue`/initial-text constructor parameter at all** — confirmed by reading the DSL widget class directly: `label`/`hint` are its only string-ish fields, both plain `String?`, not `DslExpression` (unlike `Text`/`Image`, whose first positional argument accepts a live expression directly and auto-binds via the compiler when non-static). Confirmed 2026-08-03 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.53). Any pre-filled TextField content always needs a raw patch: `node.props.ensureTextField().initialText = FFText(textValue: FFStringValue(variable: ...))`, mirroring the already-established `Switch.initialValueValue` raw-patch shape exactly. `_pageStateJsonFieldVariable(project, {widgetClassName, stateFieldName, jsonPath})` (dsl/edit.dart) is the reusable `LOCAL_STATE` counterpart to `_pageParamJsonFieldVariable` for building the variable to patch in.
- **A `List<JSON>`-typed App State field cannot be written into via `UpdateAppState`/`UpdateAppStateMutation` from this DSL — a `List<DataStruct<X>>`-typed one can.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.56, item 24) via 2 independently-shaped failed attempts on `reservationList` (declared generically `List<JSON>` in `app_state.dart`) — a raw-json `ACTION_OUTPUTS` narrow AND a properly struct-typed `UpdateAppState.set` both failed identical `compileDslApp` validation (`Field "X" has an update value that is not properly set`), while the proven precedent `kycUserListStruct` (declared `List<DataStruct<KycUserItem>>`) works fine. Check the field's declared `typeName` in `lib/flutterflow_project/app_state.dart` before planning to write a new fetch result into an existing App State list field — if it's generic `List<JSON>`, route the data through a NEW struct-typed field or a dialog component param instead.
- **A struct `returnType:` asserted only at a `CallCustomAction` call site doesn't satisfy a downstream typed `FieldAccess` consumer (e.g. a `ShowDialog.component` param) when the target is a pre-existing NATIVE custom action never formally re-declared to match.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.56, item 24): asserting `returnType: reservationsFetchResult` on a `CallCustomAction.named('adminGetReservations', ...)` call, then passing `ActionOutput(name)['field']` into a dialog param, failed with `Parameter "X" ... is not properly set` — because `adminGetReservations` is a native action whose own server-side declaration was never updated to match. The one proven-working precedent for this pattern (item 12's `adminGetReportChatLog`) was declared FRESH via `app.customAction(..., returns: ..., code: '''...Struct.fromMap({...})...''')`, with its Dart implementation itself constructing the typed struct — the declaration and the call-site assertion agreed. Before asserting a struct `returnType:` on a pre-existing native action, confirm its own implementation already returns that shape; if not, either formally redeclare it via `updateCustomAction(..., returnParameter: ...)` first (no public DslType→FFParameter conversion exists for this — would need hand-building the raw `FFParameter`) or avoid downstream typed `FieldAccess` on its output entirely.
- **Deleting a widget's `ensureActions` call from the script does NOT undo that widget's already-live trigger chain from an earlier successful push — it freezes whatever was last pushed, silently.** `ensureActions` only replaces a chain when it is actually invoked in the current run (already documented, §18.44/§161); the corollary, missed on first pass and caught only by a full self-review, is that simply removing the call (e.g. when abandoning a feature attempt) leaves the OLD chain live forever, not reverted to a no-op. Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.56, item 24): a "cheap win" first push had wired a button to fetch data then `UpdateAppState.clear(...)` a shared App State list as a placeholder for a follow-up patch; that follow-up never landed (a separate validation failure), the feature was abandoned, and the DSL's `ensureActions` call for that button was deleted as part of cleanup — but the fetch-then-clear chain stayed live in production, silently wiping a real page's on-screen list on every tap, because nothing had told the compiler to replace it with something else. **To truly neutralize an abandoned widget's chain, keep calling `ensureActions` with an explicit safe replacement (e.g. a bare `Snackbar(...)`) — never just delete the call and assume the widget goes back to doing nothing.** After abandoning any partially-wired feature that got at least one successful push, always check `generated_code/`'s actual compiled `onPressed`/trigger handler for that widget before calling it safe, not just re-read the DSL script.
- **A component/page param's DSL-declared type and `.withDefault(...)` do NOT make the generated Dart widget-class field non-nullable — it is always `Type?`, unconditionally, and a custom-action call argument sourced from that param does NOT get auto-force-unwrapped by the compiler the way a built-in Flutter widget property does.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.57), reported via FlutterFlow's own Test Mode "Analyzer Errors" screen (`argument_type_not_assignable: String? vs String`) — this bug class is invisible to `flutterflow ai run`/`compileDslApp`, which never runs Flutter's own `dart analyze` against the exported project. `ProfileEditDialogComp`'s `userId` param, declared `string.withDefault('')`, still generated as `final String? userId;`; feeding it straight into `Text(widget!.currentSelfIntroduction!, ...)` (a same-component, same-declared-type param) got auto-unwrapped by the compiler, but feeding the analogous `userId` into a custom action's Dart call (`actions.adminUpdateUserProfile(widget!.userId, ...)`) did not. **When declaring/updating a custom action whose argument will be sourced from a page/component param (`Param('x')`/`PageParam('x')`), default that argument's own Dart parameter type to nullable (`String?` etc.) from the start** rather than assuming the DSL's non-nullable `string` declaration carries through to the generated call site. Fix for an already-live mismatch: `updateCustomAction(project, name:, code:)` with the Dart signature relaxed to nullable — `code:`-only is fine (no `arguments:` needed) as long as parameter COUNT doesn't change (§183). After any such fix, verify with `cd generated_code && dart analyze` — this workspace's own `dart analyze dsl/edit.dart` checks the DSL script, NOT the exported Flutter project, so it cannot see this class of bug; run the full `generated_code/` analyze whenever a client reports a Test Mode analyzer error.
- **`EditWidgetPatch.size(width:, height:)` (and the same shape on `padding`/`margin`/similar multi-field patches) REPLACES the whole underlying value, it does NOT merge — omitting one field doesn't preserve its previous value, it clears it.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.121), by reading the SDK's own `_applySizePatch`: for a `Container`, it unconditionally does `node.props.container.dimensions = conv.convertDimensions(width:, height:)` — a wholesale reassignment. A width-only `patch.size(width: 380)` on a container that already had `height: 400` silently wiped the height to unset. If that container's child tree has an `Expanded` inside a `Column`/`Row` relying on a bounded ancestor size, the result is an unbounded-height/width `RenderFlex` crash at RUNTIME (not caught by `dart analyze` or `compileDslApp` — both pass clean) — manifests as a totally blank page, the exact bug class already seen from unbounded `ListView`s (§18.79/94/95/98) but from a different root cause. **Whenever changing only ONE dimension/field of a multi-field size-style patch, always pass the OTHER field(s) too, using their current live value** (read from `generated_code/` or `flutterflow ai inspect` first if not already known) — never assume an omitted field survives untouched.
- **`page.ensureReplaced(byPath(X), ...)` paired with `page.ensureInsertedBefore/After(byPath(X), ...)` sharing the SAME static path is only safe for the ONE push where the insert first lands — it silently breaks on any LATER push that reruns both calls unchanged.** This extends the within-a-single-call stale-index-path rule (line 292 below) to ACROSS pushes. On push 1, `ensureReplaced` runs first (safe — replace doesn't shift siblings) and the `ensureInsertedBefore` right after it, reusing the same static path, correctly lands the new widget immediately before the replaced one.
