But that insert has now become a permanent live sibling sitting BEFORE the replaced node — so on push 2 (the exact same script, unmodified, run again for an unrelated reason), the SAME static path string no longer resolves to the replaced node, it resolves to the INSERTED widget from push 1. `ensureReplaced` then swaps that inserted widget for a brand-new copy of the "replacement" content (leaving the ORIGINAL replaced node, with its own internal names/keys, untouched elsewhere in the tree), and `ensureInsertedBefore` adds ANOTHER copy of the inserted widget in front of it. Net effect: two copies of both nodes now live, invisible until the compiler's own uniqueness validation catches a byproduct of it (confirmed via `Action in <ButtonName> has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget` — the duplicated content's internal buttons collided on a fixed `outputAs` name; also visible as the SAME node name appearing twice in `compileDslApp`'s own "Go to X" navigation hints in its output). **The already-established rule "comment out `ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` once it lands" (line 165 above) is not optional politeness here — it is the actual fix.** The moment such an insert's push succeeds, comment out BOTH it and any `ensureReplaced`/other call sharing its static path, even if the `ensureReplaced` itself would technically be safe alone — leaving either live risks this duplication the next time the script runs for any unrelated reason, anywhere else in the same file.
- **Two widgets in the same `Stack` silently overlap and fight over taps if neither overrides the Stack's own default `alignment` - this is easy to introduce accidentally by `ensureInsertedBefore`-ing a NEW widget into an EXISTING Stack without also giving it its own position.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.130): a Save button was added to a dialog's header via `ensureInsertedBefore` targeting the existing close-icon's slot inside a `Stack(alignment: (1.0, 0.0))` - the insert compiled and pushed cleanly (no error, since a Stack accepts any number of children), but neither the new button nor the pre-existing close icon had its own per-child alignment override, so both rendered anchored to the exact same corner. `Stack` paints children in list order with the LAST one on top, and gives that same topmost child hit-test priority in the overlapping region - the later-declared close icon silently intercepted every tap meant for Save, with no error and no visual crash, just a button that "did nothing" when clicked. **Whenever inserting a new interactive widget into an EXISTING `Stack`, explicitly check (and likely override) its position relative to every sibling already there - don't assume a Stack "just works" the way a Column/Row does.** A node CAN be moved out of a `Stack` (or between any two parents) after the fact via direct `app.raw((project) { ... })` proto manipulation: find the source parent, `indexWhere` for the target child's key, `removeAt` it, then `.add`/`.insert` it into the destination parent's own `children` list - this is a genuine structural MOVE (preserves the node's key, styling, and any already-patched triggers/gates intact), not a re-declaration, and is naturally idempotent (rerun finds `indexWhere == -1` in the now-empty source and no-ops).
- **`EditWidgetPatch.margin(...)` is not universally supported - its own doc comment says "replaces margin on SUPPORTED widgets," and `Button`-type nodes are apparently not among them.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.130): a `component.update(buttonSelection, (patch) => patch.margin(EdgeInsets.only(top:, bottom:)))` call compiled and pushed with zero errors, but a follow-up `flutterflow ai inspect --selector-key <key> --tree` showed no margin had actually landed on the live proto - a silent no-op, not a validation failure. Don't assume an `EditWidgetPatch` method succeeded just because the push was clean; for a property whose effect matters, verify it landed via a fresh `inspect --tree` (or `generated_code/`) on the specific node, the same "confirmed, not assumed" discipline already standard for structural edits in this file.
- **A boolean gate like `_model.xLoaded` that's set `true` unconditionally at the end of an async load sequence only proves the load COMPLETED, never that it SUCCEEDED - gating a destructive action (like a bulk Save that overwrites many fields at once) on completion alone lets a silently-failed load's SAFE-DEFAULT response (e.g. `_withSafeDefaults`, §18.100 - all-false, by design, so read-only tabs never crash) get persisted as if it were real data.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.129): `Prefectures_DialogComp`'s Save button was already gated behind `prefConfigLoaded` (an earlier fix, for a different bug) - but a transient backend failure (network blip, cold start, the exact class of timing gap §18.51 already documented) still lets the load sequence run to completion, setting `prefConfigLoaded = true`, while every one of the 47 `area_xxx_active` fields silently holds the FAILURE-PATH safe default instead of real data. Save then persists that all-false snapshot, wiping out any previously-active non-canonical entries with zero visible error. **Whenever a load's own "safe-defaults-on-failure" design (correct for READ-ONLY display, per §18.100's own goal) feeds a LATER bulk-overwrite Save, the Save's gate must check the load's own `success` field specifically, not just "did the load sequence run."** A `success`-based condition strictly narrows an "already ran" condition (never breaks the happy path) and should also get a `falseAction` (a Snackbar is enough) so a blocked Save is visible/actionable instead of silently inert.
- **A page/component-load variable referenced via `_model.xxx` in generated code is not necessarily a declared Local State field - it may be an ACTION OUTPUT, auto-named by being used as a custom action's own output variable, and the two need different `FFVariable` sources (`LOCAL_STATE` vs `ACTION_OUTPUTS`) when raw-patching a condition/binding against it.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.129): assumed `prefDialogConfigInit` (the load's own response, referenced as `_model.prefDialogConfigInit` throughout the generated widget) was a declared state field like its sibling `prefConfigLoaded`, and built a condition via `_localStateVariable`/`_pageStateJsonFieldVariable` - failed at compile time with `State field "prefDialogConfigInit" not found`, revealing it was actually an action output the whole time. To locate the producing action's own node key (required for `_actionOutputJsonFieldVariable`, the `ACTION_OUTPUTS` counterpart) when it isn't already known, walk the widget class's own trigger chains looking for the `FFActionNode` whose `action.outputVariableName` matches - don't guess or hardcode a key that could drift across pushes. Before assuming a `_model.xxx` reference is Local State, check whether it also appears as an `outputAs:`/output-variable-name target anywhere in that same load chain.
- **`ensureReplaced`'s replacement content does NOT inherit an `Expanded`/`Flexible` wrapper the ORIGINAL node had from its parent - that "this child is flex-wrapped" relationship lives on the PARENT's reference to the child slot, not on the child node itself, so it is silently dropped unless the replacement explicitly re-supplies it.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.128): a panel originally sitting inside `Expanded(child: Column(...))` was rebuilt via `ensureReplaced` with a bare `Column(scrollable: true, ...)` (no `Expanded` of its own) - the regenerated output showed the `Expanded` had vanished entirely, leaving the new Column with no bounded ancestor. This made an otherwise-correct `scrollable: true` fix completely inert: without a bounded ancestor, the resulting `SingleChildScrollView` had nothing to scroll WITHIN, so it just sized itself to its full natural content height (identical to having no scroll wrapper at all) - and the overflow that should have been eliminated instead just reappeared one level further up the tree (against whatever ancestor WAS still bounded), with a DIFFERENT pixel count, making it look like a mysterious new/different bug rather than the same one un-fixed. **Whenever `ensureReplaced` targets a node that was flex-wrapped by its parent (check `generated_code/` for `Expanded(child: <this node>)` or `Flexible(child: <this node>)` before replacing), wrap the ENTIRE replacement content in the same wrapper** (`Expanded(name: '...', theRealContent)` - the DSL's `Expanded` needs its own `name:` when it's the ROOT of an `ensureReplaced`/insert payload, confirmed by the compiler's own `ensureReplaced requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name` error). Don't assume a `scrollable`/`shrinkWrap` fix took effect just because it compiled and pushed clean - re-read the actual `generated_code/` ancestor chain to confirm the wrapping survived the replacement.
- **Don't assume a `Colors.primary`-style theme token is safe for text contrast in every theme variant of a project - light and dark definitions can diverge in more than hue, including whether the color is even fully opaque.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.128): this project's DARK theme defines `primary` as `Color(0xB2262D34)` - note the alpha channel (~70% opacity) - while the LIGHT theme's `primary` (`Color(0xFF709DAA)`) is fully opaque. Text colored via `Colors.primary` alpha-blends against its background in dark mode, producing a barely-legible smear that a first glance can misread as "an unstyled/broken box" rather than "readable text in the wrong color" - while sibling text using OTHER tokens (that never touch `primary`) looks completely normal in the same screenshot, which is the tell that it's a color choice, not a broken layout. **Before trusting any `Colors.*` token for text meant to stay legible across both themes, read the actual hex values for both variants in `flutter_flow_theme.dart` (or `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/flutter_flow_theme.dart`) rather than assuming the token name implies "solid, high-contrast, theme-safe."** `primaryText`/`secondaryText` are this project's own tokens explicitly designed to invert correctly (near-black on light backgrounds, white/light-gray on dark ones) - prefer them over `primary`/`secondary` for any text whose legibility matters more than matching the brand accent hue.
- **`shrinkWrap: true` on a `ListView` (fixing "unbounded ancestor" per §18.63/74) and `scrollable: true` on the ENCLOSING `Column` (fixing "bounded-but-now-too-small ancestor") are two DIFFERENT fixes for two DIFFERENT constraint shapes - removing a fixed height from a container can trade one failure mode for the other, and both need checking, not just one.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.127): §18.124 correctly added `shrinkWrap: true` after removing a panel's fixed `height:` (avoiding "vertical viewport given unbounded height"), but didn't re-examine that panel's OWN ancestor - an `Expanded` a few levels up, sized for the OLD fixed height, now had a child that could legitimately be taller than its own bounded budget, producing a hard `RenderFlex ... BOTTOM OVERFLOWED BY N PIXELS` banner instead. Fixed by adding `scrollable: true` to the intermediate `Column` between the `Expanded` and the actual content (compiles to a real `SingleChildScrollView`) - distinct fix, distinct symptom, distinct DSL parameter. **Whenever removing a fixed height/`Expanded` to let content size itself naturally, trace the FULL ancestor chain, not just the immediate parent** - an unbounded-ancestor fix downstream doesn't rule out a bounded-but-now-insufficient ancestor further up.
- **Whenever a custom action's payload is changed via `updateCustomAction(...)`, its ORIGINAL `app.customAction(...)` declaration becomes a landmine for the next unrelated push - comment it out in the SAME session that adds the `updateCustomAction` call, don't leave it for a future push to trip over.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.127): §18.125 correctly updated `adminCreateWorkPost`'s live payload via `updateCustomAction`, but left the ORIGINAL `app.customAction('adminCreateWorkPost', ..., returns: json, ...)` declaration live/uncommented. One push later, for a completely unrelated layout fix, that stale declaration blocked the push with `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action... with a different payload` - the exact class of self-inflicted blocker this file has hit repeatedly for `app.component`/`app.struct`/other `app.customAction` declarations, but this time the DELAY between cause (the `updateCustomAction` call) and symptom (a LATER, unrelated push failing) made it look like a new bug rather than a known, preventable pattern. Treat "I just called `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` on something with a still-live original `app.*(...)` declaration" as an immediate signal to comment out that original declaration right then, not after it resurfaces.
- **This DSL's `TextField` widget has no `validator:`/`required:` parameter at all (confirmed by reading its class definition directly) - there is no native-FlutterFlow-style inline form validation available through the typed DSL.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.125, CreateWorkPostDialogComp's missing empty-field validation). The workaround, consistent with this project's own established "no And/Or combinator, use a custom function" pattern (already used for `canHireWorkPostApplicants`/`hasExtraServiceAreas`): write a small custom function taking each required field's current text (`WidgetState(fieldName, WidgetStateProperty.text)`) and returning a single bool (trim + non-empty checks, combined with `&&`), then gate the submit button's WHOLE action chain behind `If(CustomFunction(thatFn, args: {...}), then: [the real submit chain], orElse: [Snackbar('please fill in required fields')])` - the backend is never called on an invalid submission, and the validation message is a plain Snackbar (the only "on-screen message" primitive reliably available here), not inline per-field error text.
- **`app.editComponent(componentRef, (component) => component.ensureActions(component.findByKey(key), triggerType:, actions:))` is the correct, safe way to REPLACE an EXISTING component's button/trigger chain in place - same `EditWidgetEditor` API as `app.editPage`, confirmed by reading `edit.dart`'s own `editComponent` definition (identical `findByKey`/`ensureActions`/`update` surface).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.125): needed to add validation + success/failure branching to a component's existing "作成する" button whose chain had already shipped in an earlier push. `ensureActions` replaces the same trigger chain on rerun rather than duplicating it (its own documented rerun-safety, already established for pages) - this generalizes cleanly to components too, and is safe to leave live in the script permanently, unlike a structural insert.
- **A `Column`’s children split via `Expanded(flex:)` ratios against a FIXED outer height silently squeezes/hides content whenever any child's real content size is bigger than its flex share - and this is invisible to `dart analyze`/`compileDslApp`, only found by actually working the pixel budget.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.124, 提供エリア設定's "added region doesn't show up" report): a card fixed at `height: 520` split two lists via `Expanded(flex: 3)`/`Expanded(flex: 2)` - after subtracting fixed-height siblings (header/label row/button), the FIRST list (always exactly 11 rows) only got ~242px of its own ~452px real content need, forcing it to scroll internally with no visible scrollbar affordance. New content landing in the SECOND list's own cramped strip read as "nothing happened" even though the underlying data was completely correct - confirmed by an unrelated, independent backend re-verification finding zero issues there. **When a list/card's content is a MIX of fixed-count (always N rows) and open-ended (0-M rows) sections, don't budget a guessed fixed pixel height split via flex ratios - let the container size itself to its own content instead**: this DSL's `Column` already defaults to `mainAxisSize.min` (§18.63/§18.74's own established rule), so removing the outer fixed `height:` and replacing every `Expanded(child: ListView(...))` with a plain `Container(child: ListView(..., shrinkWrap: true))` makes the whole card grow to fit whatever it actually contains, with zero risk of a squeeze regardless of how the content count changes later. `shrinkWrap: true` is REQUIRED, not optional, the moment `Expanded` is removed - a bare `ListView` under a `mainAxisSize.min` Column throws "vertical viewport was given unbounded height" outright.
- **Before concluding a client's "still not working" follow-up means your previous fix failed, verify the CURRENT real data/state directly - a stale client-side session showing an OLD view is a fully separate failure mode from "the fix didn't work," and conflating them wastes an entire investigation cycle.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.124): after §18.123's backend redeploy, the client reported the exact same "region not added" symptom again. Read `system_config/settings` directly via the Admin SDK (bypassing the client and Firestore rules entirely, same technique as §18.34/§18.51) and found the data ALREADY correctly reflected a successful test (3 real non-canonical prefectures marked active) - cross-checked against `firebase functions:log` showing the matching call pairs succeeding with zero errors in that exact window. This is definitive, first-party evidence the backend fix DID work - it redirected the entire investigation toward a frontend rendering bug (found: the fixed-height squeeze above) instead of re-litigating an already-fixed backend, and toward telling the client to hard-refresh/use a fresh session rather than assuming their report was simply wrong. **Whenever a "still broken" report follows a real fix, check the actual current data/logs FIRST, before re-touching the same code path** - it tells you immediately whether you're chasing a persistence bug, a display bug, or a stale-cache non-bug, three completely different fixes.
- **A Cloud Function's deployed generation (1st Gen vs 2nd Gen) can silently drift from what its source file currently defines, because Firebase refuses an in-place upgrade between generations - `firebase deploy` on a changed-generation function fails outright rather than updating it, so a plain "clean deploy" history can mask a function that has NEVER actually picked up its current source.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.123, 提供エリア設定 "functionality not working" report): `admin.ts` defines `adminUpdateSystemConfig` via the v2 `onCall` import, same as every other function in the file - but `firebase functions:list` showed it as **v1**, while its read-side counterpart `adminGetSystemConfig` correctly showed **v2**. Attempting `firebase deploy --only functions:adminUpdateSystemConfig` made the mismatch undeniable: `Error: Upgrading from 1st Gen to 2nd Gen is not yet supported.` The live function had been silently stuck on whatever it was before some earlier source rewrite, with every subsequent `firebase deploy` for it either skipped or quietly failing this exact way. **Whenever a feature's read and write paths both go through the same Cloud Functions file but only one side is reported broken, check `firebase functions:list` for a generation (or runtime) mismatch between the paired functions before assuming a data-shape or logic bug** - `grep`-ing the source alone won't reveal this, since the source can look perfectly correct and consistent while the deployed artifact is a stale snapshot from before a generation rewrite. **Fix requires DELETE then a fresh CREATE deploy** (`firebase functions:delete <name> --region <region> --force`, then `firebase deploy --only functions:<name>`) - there is no in-place migration path. Treat this as a real production action needing the same care as any other delete: confirm the function's current source is generic/caller-agnostic (safe for every existing caller) before deleting, and get user confirmation first if the function is shared across multiple features - the auto-mode classifier will independently flag the delete command as worth pausing on, which is a correct signal, not friction to route around.
- **UPDATE to the "`ShowDialog.component(...)` positioning fields are a confirmed codegen gap" finding two entries above (§18.96/97): that gap is NOT a durable/permanent SDK limitation - re-test before assuming it still applies.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.122, "standardize every modal to open centered"): a project-wide sweep setting `isGlobal: true`/`followerAnchor: (0,0)` on EVERY `customDialog` trigger project-wide (via a new recursive `_centerCustomDialogsInTree` walker calling the existing `_setCustomDialogIsGlobal` on every node in every widget class) was pushed expecting it to fix only NATIVE (browser-authored) dialogs, with the ~9 DSL/`ShowDialog.component(...)`-authored dialogs (KYC review, JobBoard create/hire, PaymentLedger Stripe viewer, Report chat log, Guest/Cast profile editor, both date pickers) still needing the heavier custom-action-bypass rebuild §18.96 proved necessary. Instead, re-scanning `generated_code/` after that ONE push showed `showAlignedDialog` had disappeared from the ENTIRE project - every one of those ~9 dialogs ALSO now compiles to a plain, correctly-centered `Dialog(alignment: AlignmentDirectional(0.0, 0.0))`, with no bypass needed. The exact mechanism is unconfirmed (whether `isGlobal: true` alone flips the codegen template, or something changed server-side in the ~2 days between §18.96 and this fix) - but don't assume the §18.96 gap is still live without a fresh `inspect --tree` + `generated_code/` check; a one-push, project-wide `isGlobal`/`followerAnchor` sweep may now be sufficient where it previously wasn't.
- **Two proto shapes look structurally identical (`FFCustomDialogAction` with `isGlobal`/`followerAnchor` set) but can compile through genuinely different codegen templates depending on how the trigger action originated - always confirm which one you're looking at via `generated_code/`, not just `inspect --tree`.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.122): a dialog trigger authored NATIVELY (FlutterFlow's own browser "Show Custom Dialog" action) compiles to a plain `Dialog(alignment: AlignmentDirectional(x, y))`, which reliably reads `followerAnchor` off the proto - confirmed on a trigger with ZERO `aiProvenance` (i.e. never touched by any AI session) already showing this shape as its original, un-edited state. A dialog trigger authored via the DSL's `ShowDialog.component(...)` compiles (per §18.96/97, and see the update immediately above) through a different path entirely. When debugging a dialog-positioning issue, grep the SPECIFIC trigger's own compiled output for `showAlignedDialog(` vs a bare `return Dialog(` before deciding which fix applies - don't assume from the DSL source alone which codegen path a given trigger takes.
- **When auditing "every X across the project" (a broad, cross-cutting client directive rather than a single-page bug report), scan the compiled `generated_code/` output directly with a script, not the DSL source.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.122): a Python regex sweep over all of `generated_code/lib/**/*.dart` found every dialog-opening call site and its actual resolved alignment value in one pass (53 triggers found, meaning many were previously undocumented anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart`'s own comments) - far more reliable than trying to enumerate every `ShowDialog.component(...)`/native custom-dialog call site by reading 24,000+ lines of DSL source, which would have missed natively-authored triggers the DSL never touched at all. Re-run the same script after each fix to get an exact before/after count instead of spot-checking a handful of pages and hoping the pattern generalized.
- **Not everything that visually "pops up" on tap is a "modal" that a broad centering/standardization directive should touch - a hamburger/nav-drawer menu and a `showModalBottomSheet`-based bottom sheet are both genuinely different UI paradigms, not off-center dialogs.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.122): while auditing every dialog-opening call for a "standardize modal positioning" directive, found `MainMenuCompWidget` (opened via the same native custom-dialog mechanism as everything else) and `ResetPasswordFormWidget` (opened via `showModalBottomSheet(isScrollControlled: true, ...)`, a fundamentally different widget). Centering either would have been a UX regression, not a fix - a nav drawer is persistent chrome, not a discrete action-triggered popup, and a bottom sheet's entire design point is anchoring to the bottom edge. When a broad audit surfaces a component that doesn't semantically fit the category the client named, exclude it and document why, rather than mechanically "fixing" everything a keyword/pattern search turns up.
- **To reference an already-live Data Struct or Component from DSL without re-triggering its one-shot `ensure*` declaration (needed whenever a LATER unrelated push has to touch the same script file again) — construct a pure, non-mutating local handle instead of calling `app.struct(...)`/`app.component(...)` again.** `StructHandle(name, {fields})` and `ComponentHandle(ComponentDeclaration(name:, description:, params:, body:))` are both confirmed (via reading `app.dart`) to be plain public `const`-style constructors — they build a typed reference usable anywhere the real thing would be (`listOf(structHandle)`, `ShowDialog.component(componentHandle)`, a `_workPostRow`-style helper's parameter) without ever mutating server state. This generalizes §172's `ComponentHandle`-stub pattern two ways: (1) it applies to structs too, not just components — useful when `app.struct(...)`'s own ensure-mismatch check rejects a rerun for a reason unrelated to the actual field list (e.g. list-of-struct fields are represented differently by `app.struct(...)`'s compiler than a hand-constructed raw-proto field addition, confirmed 2026-08-08, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.121/§18.120 — updating the `app.struct(...)` declaration to match did NOT resolve the mismatch, switching to `StructHandle` did); (2) the handle does not need to be a minimal stub — passing the FULL real `body`/`params` content (byte-for-byte identical to what's already live) works exactly as well as an empty placeholder, and keeps the reconstructed handle's typed shape genuinely accurate for anything downstream that inspects it, at the cost of more text in the script.
- **`FFActionCondition.variable` (an action's `conditionActions` gate, and therefore the typed `If(...)` action too — confirmed it compiles to the exact same proto shape via `Actions.conditional`) only accepts a COMPLETELY BARE stored-field variable: `LOCAL_STATE`/`APP_STATE`/`WIDGET_STATE`/`ACTION_OUTPUTS` sourced, with ZERO operations attached (no `jsonPathOperation`, no `negate`, nothing).** Confirmed 2026-08-04 across 2 separate tasks, 3 total failed attempts, all producing the identical `Conditional execution for action is improperly set` error (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.58 then corrected/broadened by §18.59 the same day — read §18.59, §18.58's own original conclusion was too narrow): (1) a `FUNCTION_CALL`-sourced variable using `EXISTS_AND_NON_EMPTY`; (2) the same, using negated `EQUAL_TO`; (3) — the one that forced the correction — an `ACTION_OUTPUTS`-sourced variable (the confirmed-working SOURCE kind from attempts 1-2's fix) with a `jsonPathOperation` narrow attached, which ALSO failed identically. So the rule isn't "must be ACTION_OUTPUTS/LOCAL_STATE/etc. instead of FUNCTION_CALL" — it's "must have no operations at all, regardless of source." The one pre-existing precedent for a raw action condition in this file (`_fixPrefecturesSaveButton`'s `prefConfigLoaded` gate) is a plain field with zero operations, consistent with the corrected rule. **Fix: whatever value the condition needs, compute it FULLY inside a preceding `CallCustomAction` step (a tiny pure-Dart custom action if no network call is needed, matching `guestListHasSelection`'s shape) so that action's own return type is ALREADY the bare boolean you need, then branch on its bare `ActionOutput(...)` via the typed `If(condition, then:, orElse:)` action** — never try to narrow (jsonPath, FieldAccess, negate) at the condition site itself, no matter which mechanism (raw proto or typed `If`) builds the surrounding conditional, since both compile to the identical restrictive shape. Also confirmed: `Actions.conditional`/`Actions.snackBar`/`Actions.terminate` (the static `Actions` class from `ui/actions.dart`, referenced in some documentation) are internal-SDK-only and NOT reachable from `dsl/edit.dart`'s plain `import 'package:flutterflow_ai/flutterflow_ai.dart';` — only `src/dsl/actions.dart`'s public final classes (`If`, `Snackbar`, `ConfirmDialog`, `CallCustomAction`, etc.) are exported to edit scripts; use those, not `Actions.xxx()`.
- **Before deleting an existing section's nodes from the tree, grep the WHOLE file for every OTHER permanent (non-one-shot) raw-patch block that might reference any of that section's descendant keys.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.59): relocating GuestUserdetailsPage's 強制退会/凍結 sections (deleting the old nodes, inserting a rebuilt replacement elsewhere) orphaned 2 PERMANENT raw-patch blocks written by earlier, unrelated items (items 9 and 10) that still referenced the OLD buttons' keys (`Button_2mzdco3x`, `Button_kdozxja0`/`Button_xuee2535`) — these blocks don't live anywhere near the new insertion code (they were written sessions/items ago, for the section BEFORE it existed in its current form), so they're easy to miss. The resulting error (`Bad state: ForceDeleteUserButton node not found.`) had to be traced by the literal ERROR STRING, not the key, since 2 different throw sites in this file happen to produce similarly-worded messages for different keys.
- **`getJsonField(...).toString()`-without-default (§18.33's established bug class) also reaches component dialog PARAM PASSES, not just custom-action arguments or native widget bindings — and `FFJsonPathOperation.returnParameter.defaultValue` does NOT fix it there.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.59): `ProfileEditDialogComp`'s `currentSelfIntroduction` param, sourced via a raw jsonPath narrow with no guard, pre-fills the edit dialog with the literal string `"null"` for any guest with no self-introduction set. Attempted the real, non-deprecated `FFParameter.defaultValue` field (available via `FFJsonPathOperation.returnParameter`) hoping it would trigger the same `valueOrDefault<String>(...)` codegen wrapping seen on NATIVE bindings elsewhere on this same page — pushed clean, but confirmed via the regenerated code that it had ZERO effect on codegen; still bare `.toString()`. Whatever causes `valueOrDefault` wrapping for native bindings, it isn't this field, at least not in this position. No safe client-side fix found for this specific case within this DSL's current surface.
- **`EXISTS_AND_NON_EMPTY`/`DOES_NOT_EXIST_OR_IS_EMPTY` (`FFCondition_Relation`) are only ever emitted by this SDK's own compiler via a narrow "text emptiness" shortcut for `TextValue`/`WidgetState(..., text)` targets — not a general list/collection-emptiness primitive.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.58) by reading `_matchTextEmptinessConditionShortcut` in compiler.dart directly: it only pattern-matches `Not(Equals(textLikeThing, ''))`-shaped expressions. Using `EXISTS_AND_NON_EMPTY` on a `List<String>` field (outside that shape) was rejected server-side. For a genuine "is this list non-empty" check, don't reach for this relation — use the negated-`EQUAL_TO`-against-empty-literal approach (or, per the point above, an `ActionOutput`-sourced boolean from a small custom action) instead.
- **Component-local state (`editComponentState`/`state.ensureField(...)`) cannot back a `ListView`'s `source:` (and likely no other dynamic-children/`visible:` binding) — only page-local state and App State can.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.60), reproduced identically twice: `ListView(source: State('activityLog'), ...)` inside `Info_DialogComp` (a native component) failed `compileDslApp` with `Dynamic children variable not properly configured for ListView` + `Widget class state field not found` — once with the state field declared in the same push as the ListView (ruled out as a timing issue), and again with the field confirmed already live from a separate prior successful push (definitively ruling out timing). The second error message ("Widget class state field not found") is the tell. The one other `ListView(source: State(...))` precedent in this project (`GuestUserdetailsPage`'s own `reservationsList`) is page-local state and works fine — confirming the distinguishing factor is component-scope vs. page-scope, not the `State(...)` binding itself. **Fix: route dynamic list/conditional data for a COMPONENT through App State instead (`app.state('name', type)`, `UpdateAppStateMutation.set('name', ...)`, `ListView(source: AppState('name'))`) — reusing the already-proven `kycUserListStruct` pattern. For a PAGE, page-local `State(...)` is fine as-is.** Also confirmed: `app.state('name', type)` returns `void`, but `AppState('name')`/`UpdateAppStateMutation.set('name', ...)` accept a plain string directly, usable in the SAME script as the declaration — no barrel regen needed (see `references/edit_restyle_and_enhance_dsl.dart`'s own `buildBaseProject`). Like `kycUserListStruct`, `app.state(...)` itself does not tolerate an identical-payload rerun once the field exists.
- **When relocating a native section by deleting the old nodes and inserting a rebuilt replacement, the delete and the insert MUST land in the same push — not insert-then-delete-in-a-later-push.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.60): inserting a new admin-actions section (via `page.ensureInsertedInto`) while the old section's identically-named buttons (e.g. `ForceDeleteUserButton`, same `outputAs: 'forceDeleteUserResult'`) were still live failed `compileDslApp` with `Action in ForceDeleteUserButton has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget` — the new and old widgets' output-variable names collide while both exist on the page simultaneously. Combine the insert (`page.ensureInsertedInto`) and the delete (`FFNode.children.removeWhere(...)` against the old section's container keys) into ONE `app.raw`/one push. The gating/re-binding follow-up patch (needing the new nodes' real keys, only known after that push lands) is the genuinely separate "push 2." Corollary: before that delete step runs, also grep for and update/remove any OTHER permanent raw-patch block (from earlier, unrelated items) that still references the doomed old keys — see the point above about orphaned permanent raw-patches; once every page referencing a shared generic patch helper has been migrated off it, delete the now-fully-dead helper block entirely rather than leaving it as unreachable code.
- **`ensureActions`'s no-op-on-matching-action-shape short-circuit (`_triggerSemanticallyEquals`) also blocks ADDING a passed-parameter to an existing trigger, not just changing its target component.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.61): re-running `page.ensureActions(nativeInfoButton, ...ShowDialog.component(Info_DialogComp, params: {'targetUserId': PageParam('userId')})...)` against a NATIVE node whose original trigger already opens `Info_DialogComp` with zero params silently kept the OLD zero-param chain live in production — `_triggerSemanticallyEquals` treated the "same dialog, now with one more param" trigger as unchanged, same underlying short-circuit already documented above for the Search-icon `_patchDialogTarget` case (component-swap), just triggered here by a param-map difference instead. Neither existing raw-patch helper covers this: `_patchDialogTarget` repoints the target component (wrong tool when the component is already correct); `_patchCustomDialogParamVariable` only UPDATES a key already present in `parameterPasses` (wrong tool when the key was never present on the original zero-arg call). **Fix: walk the existing trigger's `ShowDialog.component` action node directly and INSERT a new `FFParameterPass` into `passedParameters.parameterPasses`, keyed by the target component's own live param identifier (looked up dynamically, e.g. `infoDialogClass.params.values.firstWhere(name == 'targetUserId').identifier.key` — never hardcode this key, brownfield component params resolve to a generated key distinct from the human-readable name).** General rule: any time `ensureActions` is asked to modify a trigger whose action TYPE isn't changing (same dialog, same custom action, same navigate target — only an argument/param differs), verify the change actually landed in `generated_code/` before trusting it; don't assume the semantic-equality short-circuit only fires on the narrower "target changed" case.
- **On a tabbed detail page, `Expanded(SingleChildScrollView(...))` wrapping belongs to whichever CHILD Column has its own "enable scrolling" flag set — not to the outer Column that holds it as one of several children.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.61): inserting a new section into "the container holding the page's main content" (the プロフィール tab's outer Column, e.g. `Column_wt43itye`/`Column_s1unw162`) landed it as a SIBLING of that scrollable inner Column, not a descendant. Fixing this WAS a real tree-shape improvement, but **it was NOT the cause of the overflow the client actually reported** (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.62 found the real cause below) — recorded here as a legitimate secondary finding, not as an overflow fix to reach for on its own.
- **A page's outer content Column not being marked `scrollable` is a genuine, page-specific native-builder gap, and the actual cause of "page overflows even though a structurally-identical sibling page doesn't."** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.62): `CastUserdetailsPage`'s tab area sits in a Container with a hardcoded `height: 2000.0` (pre-existing, unrelated to any DSL work) inside an outer Column (`Column_rs5lbbon`) with `scrollable: false, primary: true`. `GuestUserdetailsPage`'s structurally-identical ancestor Column (`Column_cf93huzh`) has `scrollable: true, primary: false` and correctly wraps its own (also hardcoded-height) container in a `SingleChildScrollView`, so Guest never overflowed while Cast did. A fixed pixel `height:` on a Container is not itself the bug (see the `Container_gccy1a21`/percentOfScreenSizeValue precedent above) — the failure mode is always a Column somewhere UPSTREAM of it not being `scrollable`, leaving the fixed height with no escape hatch. **When one page overflows and a structurally-identical sibling page doesn't, `inspect --tree` both pages' equivalent outer Column and diff `props.column.scrollable`/`primary` FIRST — it's one boolean, and was the entire difference here.** Fix: `node.props.column.scrollable = true; node.props.column.primary = false;` via `page.mutateNode`/a raw patch, matching the working sibling page's own values exactly.
- **`mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min` on a Column is a silent trap for any `ListView` (or other viewport-based widget) inserted into it later without `Expanded` or `shrinkWrap: true` — it compiles clean and only throws at runtime, inside the dialog/component's own `build()`.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.62): `Info_DialogComp`'s activity-log `ListView.separated(...)` (added by §18.60's `ensureReplaced`) was a direct, non-flexible child of a `mainAxisSize.min` Column with nothing bounding its height. `mainAxisSize.min` gives non-flexible children an UNBOUNDED height constraint in the main axis, and a bare `ListView` under that constraint throws Flutter's "vertical viewport was given unbounded height" exception the instant the widget builds — which manifests as: the dialog's modal barrier renders (a visible dim), but the dialog's own content never paints (no close button ever renders either), leaving the app stuck behind an undismissable barrier. This is invisible to `compileDslApp` (a runtime-only layout exception) and was shipped without being click-tested live. **`shrinkWrap: true` stops the crash but is NOT the correct end state — PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.63 found it reintroduces overflow once the list has enough content, since it sizes the ListView to ALL of its content instead of the space actually available. Prefer `Expanded(...)` around the ListView whenever it already sits inside a bounded flex ancestor** (true here - the dialog's outer `Expanded(child: Container(...))` already bounds it) — this gives the ListView a real bounded constraint and lets it scroll internally, rather than either crashing (bare ListView) or overflowing at scale (`shrinkWrap` alone). Every OTHER dynamic `ListView` in this project currently uses `shrinkWrap: true` (`StaffUserListView`/`AdministratorListView`/etc.) rather than `Expanded` — they haven't hit visible overflow only because their lists happen to have enough headroom, not because `shrinkWrap` is actually the more correct choice; `Expanded` would be the safer fix there too if revisited. **Before authoring or extending a `ListView` inside a component/dialog, check whether its containing Column uses `mainAxisSize.min` - if so, wrap the ListView in its own `Expanded`, not `shrinkWrap: true`, unless there's a specific reason the list must size to its own content (e.g. it's the ONLY child and nothing else needs the remaining space).**
- **A STATIC boolean literal passed to `visible:` (e.g. `visible: false`) inside `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedInto` is not reliably honored — only a DYNAMIC bound condition (`visible: Not(AppState(...))`, `visible: someBoolExpression`) reliably compiles to a real guard.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.63): `Text(Param('targetUserId'), name: 'TargetUserIdRef', visible: false)` from §18.60 compiled to a bare, always-visible `Text(widget!.targetUserId, style: TextStyle())` in `generated_code/` — no `Visibility`/conditional wrapper at all — while the sibling empty-state `Text(..., visible: Not(AppState('infoDialogHasActivity')))` in the SAME `ensureReplaced` call correctly compiled to a real `if (!FFAppState().infoDialogHasActivity) Text(...)`. **Before trusting a `visible: <literal bool>` to hide a widget, check `generated_code/` for an actual `if`/`Visibility` guard - if it's missing, either bind a real (even trivial) dynamic condition instead of a literal, or just omit the widget from the tree entirely if it serves no functional purpose** (the fix used here, since this Text existed only as leftover reference scaffolding - the custom actions it superficially "backed" read `widget!.targetUserId` directly in Dart, never through this node).
- **When asked to make a widget visually match an existing native one, `inspect --tree` the ORIGINAL node first and check `props.icon.iconDataValue.inputValue.isCustom`/`family`/`codePoint` before reaching for the string-name `icon(name)` patch API — that API only resolves built-in icon packs (Material/Phosphor/etc.) and silently produces a DIFFERENT, merely-plausible-looking icon for any custom UPLOADED icon font, with no compiler error to flag the mismatch.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.62 → corrected by §18.63): Guest/Cast's "info" button icon is a project-specific custom icon font (`codePoint: 59417, family: 'Icoccha_app_mypage_icon', name: 'chatCenteredTextG', isCustom: true`, confirmed via `inspect --tree`), not a Material icon - `patch.icon('info')` compiled without error but produced the generic `Icons.info` glyph instead, a visible mismatch the client had to catch and report. **Fix for reproducing a custom icon-font glyph exactly: raw-patch `node.props.icon.iconDataValue = FFIconDataValue(inputValue: FFIconData(codePoint: ..., family: ..., name: ..., isCustom: true))` with the exact values read from the original node** - `FFIconData(...)` is a generated protobuf factory, not `const`-constructible (use `final`, not `const`, when assigning it to a local).
- **When a prior write-up says a rollout was "scoped to pages X/Y because Z doesn't have this feature yet," that describes the CODE at the time, not necessarily the actual boundary of the client's request — re-check against what the client asked for before treating the prior scoping as settled.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.62): §18.60 correctly scoped its dynamic "info" modal work to Guest/Cast only, since Staff/Administrator had no `Info_DialogComp` reference at the time — but the client's actual ask (uniform behavior across all 4 user-type detail pages, reachable via the dashboard's "Today's New Registrations" section as well as the 3 list pages) implied all 4 needed it, and the client's re-report confirmed this (Staff/Administrator still showed the old "search" button). Converting the 2 remaining pages used the exact same technique as the passed-parameter fix above (`_patchDialogTarget`-style `componentClassNodeKeyRef` rewrite, since `ensureActions` no-ops here too — the action shape, `alertDialog.customDialog`, is unchanged even though the target component differs) plus a `targetUserId` passed-parameter insert, a dialog resize to match the working pages, and an icon/text relabel.
- **After shipping a fix for a client-reported bug, re-derive the fix against the client's OWN reported specifics (exact screenshot, exact pixel count, exact symptom sequence) before considering it closed — a hypothesis that reads as plausible from static code alone is not the same as confirming it against what was actually reported.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.61 → corrected by §18.62): a prior session's already-diagnosed hypothesis (about `Expanded(SingleChildScrollView(...))` sibling placement) was carried forward and "fixed" without re-checking it against the client's own screenshot (`~/Pictures/55.png`, showing a specific `height: 2000.0` container overflowing by a specific, later-explainable 1204px) - the fix compiled clean, looked structurally correct, and was simply wrong for the reported symptom. The client's re-report ("remains unchanged") was the first real signal; re-reading the actual screenshots and the FULL page structure (not just the one subtree the inherited hypothesis pointed at) found the real cause in one pass. When a live screenshot or exact error string exists, read it (or re-read it) before trusting a second static-code hypothesis.
- **`ensureReplaced` reassigns its replacement content a FRESH key on EVERY execution, not just the first — this applies to ANY target, not only ListViews.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): two blocks from earlier the same day (an `Info_DialogComp` Column rebuild, and a companion raw-patch keyed to a node inside it) were left live/uncommented; the very next push failed with `byKey("...") expected exactly one widget, found 0`, since that key only existed transiently between two pushes. **There is no such thing as a safely-left-live `ensureReplaced` call — comment it out immediately once it lands, exactly like `app.customAction`/`app.component`/`app.state`, regardless of what kind of widget it targets.**
- **Many successful reruns of a live (uncommented) `app.customAction`/`app.struct`/`app.component` call is NOT evidence it tolerates a changed payload — it only proves the payload hasn't changed yet.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): `app.customAction('adminGetUserListItems', ...)` had been left live since §18.48 and survived many pushes, which this session initially (wrongly) read as "these ensure*-backed declarations are more rerun-tolerant than `app.state`." The moment a companion `updateCustomAction` call actually changed the SAME action's live definition, the very next push failed with `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action named "..." with a different payload. 'ensure*' helpers are create-if-missing only.` — confirming it was exactly as strict as `app.state` all along (matching what line ~172 above already correctly documents for `app.component`). **Treat every `app.customAction`/`app.struct`/`app.component`/`app.state` call as create-once and comment it out as soon as it lands, full stop — don't take "it's survived N reruns" as license to leave it live.**
- **A custom action's own declared PARAM LIST is subject to the same registration-order rule as page params/state — any `updateCustomAction` call that changes it must be registered (run) before any `CallCustomAction.named(...)` call site that references it.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): placing the `updateCustomAction` extension near the end of the file, after 3 pages' own `CallCustomAction.named('adminGetUserListItems', ...)` calls, failed `compileDslApp` with `Custom action argument "..." is not specified` for every param — including ones already correctly passed - because `updateCustomAction` reassigns EVERY param a fresh opaque key, and a call site registered earlier resolves its argument names against the OLD key set, which the later update immediately orphans. Fix: move the `updateCustomAction` call to immediately after the action's original declaration.
- **`ensureActions` no-ops when only an action's ARGUMENT VALUES change (not its type/target) — confirmed on a 3rd distinct trigger shape: an ON_INIT_STATE custom-action call, not just a dialog target-swap or a passed-parameter add.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): rewriting `page.ensureActions(page.root, ON_INIT_STATE, [CallCustomAction.named('adminGetUserListItems', arguments: {...7 keys...})])` from a 2-argument to a 7-argument map compiled clean but had zero live effect - `generated_code/` still showed the bare 2-argument call after the push. **Never trust `ensureActions` to pick up a changed argument VALUE on an otherwise-unchanged action - verify against `generated_code/` after every push, and default to a raw argument patch (`_patchCustomActionArgument`) whenever only values, not the action type or target, are changing.** (Still write the fully-correct `ensureActions` call in the DSL source anyway, per this file's own convention - it documents true intent even when a supplementary raw patch is what actually applies it.)
- **`_patchCustomActionVariableArgument`'s plain `followUpAction`-only walk never finds a `CallCustomAction` nested inside an `If(...)`'s `then:`/`orElse:` branch — use `_patchCustomActionVariableArgumentDeep` (dsl/edit.dart) whenever the target call might be inside a conditional.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): patching the Bulk Freeze/Unfreeze buttons' `reason` argument via the original helper compiled clean, ran without error, and had ZERO effect - `adminBulkToggleFreeze` sits inside `If(hasSelection, then: [...])`, and `FFActionNode` stores a conditional as a MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE alternative to a plain `action` (`current.hasConditionActions()`, a top-level oneof case distinct from `current.hasAction()`), with branches in `conditionActions.trueActions[i].trueAction`/`conditionActions.falseAction` - both fresh `FFActionNode` chain roots, invisible to a walk that only follows `followUpAction`. **Any raw-patch helper that walks a trigger's action chain looking for a nested action must recurse into `If`/conditional branches unless the target is provably never inside one - a linear walk silently finds nothing and reports no error, so this bug class is invisible until you diff `generated_code/` line by line.**
- **`ensureInsertedInto` (unlike `ensureReplaced`) IS safely rerunnable without creating a duplicate or reassigning its root's key.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.64): 3 Bulk-action-row insertions were left live across 2 more pushes with no duplication and a stable key each time - no need to comment out an `ensureInsertedInto` call the way `ensureReplaced`/`app.customAction`/etc. must be. Also: any widget passed to `ensureInsertedInto`/`ensureReplaced` as the TOP-LEVEL inserted/replacement root must have a non-empty `name:` or the push fails immediately with `requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name` - easy to forget when the root is a plain `Container`/`Column` wrapper that wouldn't otherwise need one.
- **`Dropdown(...)` has NO `width`/`height` constructor params** (confirmed by reading `widgets.dart` directly) - unlike `TextField`, it must be wrapped in `Container(width:, height:, child: Dropdown(...))` for any explicit sizing. Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.65) after `compileDslApp` rejected `Dropdown(..., width: 200, height: 40)` with `No named parameter with the name 'width'` on 7 separate call sites in one push.
- **`Equals(...)` compiles to a `FUNCTION_CALL`-sourced `FFVariable` (`FFVariable(source: FUNCTION_CALL, functionCall: FFFunctionCall(condition: FFCondition(relation: EQUAL_TO), values: [...]))`) - and this IS usable for a widget's `visible:` property, even though a FUNCTION_CALL-sourced variable is REJECTED for `FFActionCondition.variable` (action gating).** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.65) by reading `compiler.dart`'s own `Equals()` case directly. These are two DIFFERENT proto paths with different validation rules - the already-documented "zero operations, completely bare variable" restriction (used for `If(...)`/conditional actions) does not carry over to `FFBooleanValue.variable` (what `visible:` compiles through), which already had a working precedent for operation-bearing variables (`visible: Not(AppState(...))`, proven since §18.60). **When you need conditional visibility on a widget that already exists from a prior push (so the typed `visible: Equals(WidgetState(...), ...)` route isn't available - see the fresh-widget-reference rule elsewhere in this file), hand-build this exact shape via `node.props.ensureVisibility().visibleValue = FFBooleanValue(variable: FFVariable(source: FUNCTION_CALL, functionCall: FFFunctionCall(condition: FFCondition(relation: EQUAL_TO), values: [FFValue(variable: ...), FFValue(inputValue: FFParameterValue(serializedValue: ...))])))` (see `_patchVisibleEqualsWidgetState` in `dsl/edit.dart`) - don't assume it's blocked just because the sibling action-condition restriction is a known landmine.**
- **`WidgetState(widget, property)` resolves a bare `String` argument as the widget's own `name:` (a "legacy name" lookup) - NEVER its auto-generated `key:`. This is the OPPOSITE convention from this file's own raw-patch helpers (`_textFieldValueVariable(key)`, `findByKey(...)`), which need a real key via `FFNodeKeyReference`.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.65): using a widget's KEY (learned via `inspect --outline`, e.g. `'DropDown_idohcilt'`) inside a TYPED `WidgetState(...)` call failed `compileDslApp` with `Bad state: Widget "DropDown_idohcilt" was not found on "..."`. Fixed by switching to the widget's own `name:` string instead - which is also MORE robust long-term, since a chosen `name:` survives a future `ensureReplaced` rerun (which reassigns keys) while a captured key does not. **Before writing `WidgetState('...', ...)`, check whether the string in hand is a NAME (author-chosen, stable) or a KEY (server-generated, reassigned on every `ensureReplaced`) - only names belong in `WidgetState(...)`; only keys belong in a raw-patch's `FFNodeKeyReference`/`findByKey(...)`.**
- **`ShowDialog.component(...)`'s DEFAULT output has NO explicit size at all when the target component has no useful intrinsic size of its own - the popover technically opens (barrier + `Material` exist) but renders at zero/degenerate size, indistinguishable from "does nothing" to whoever tests it.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.66): `StartDatePickerDialogComp`/`EndDatePickerDialogComp`, opened via `ShowDialog.component(...)` with no follow-up sizing, compiled to `showAlignedDialog(..., builder: (dialogContext) => Material(color: transparent, child: StartDatePickerDialogCompWidget()))` - no `Container(width:, height:)` anywhere, confirmed by contrast against every OTHER dialog in this project that HAS been explicitly sized (`_patchDialogSize`), which always shows an explicit sized `Container` wrapper in `generated_code/`. A dialog trigger's `Container` wrapper is emitted BECAUSE dimensions were set, not by default. **Whenever wiring `ShowDialog.component(...)` to open a component that isn't already known to size itself (most native "dialog content" components assume an external Container gives them bounds, matching how the ORIGINAL native trigger this project keeps reproducing always wrapped them explicitly), immediately follow up with `_patchDialogSize(triggerNode, width:, height:)` using whatever size the original native equivalent used - don't treat a missing explicit size as a safe default.**
- **`_patchDialogSize` (and by extension any raw-patch helper mutating a proto submessage) must use that submessage's `ensureXxx()` accessor, not the bare getter, unless every call site is proven to already have that field set.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.66): `dialog.customDialog.dimensions.width = ...` had worked for 3 prior call sites (all targeting dialogs that already had SOME dimensions set, even if wrong) but threw `Unsupported operation: Attempted to change a read-only message (flutterflow.FFDimensions)` the first time it targeted a dialog whose `dimensions` had never been set at all - an unset submessage returns a shared READ-ONLY default instance in protobuf-dart. Fixed by switching to `.ensureDimensions()` (auto-vivifies if unset, returns the existing mutable instance otherwise - correct either way), matching the SAME `ensure*`-vs-bare-getter pattern already used for `ensureArgumentValues()`/`ensureVisibility()`/`ensureComponentClassNodeKeyRef()` elsewhere in this file. A helper that "has worked every time so far" is not the same as "always safe" - check whether its bare-getter accesses have ever actually been exercised against a genuinely-unset target before trusting them on a new one.
- **When relocating a widget to "the same location" as a reference layout, match its exact GROUPING (parent/sibling structure), not just its visual proximity — bundling two widgets that only LOOK adjacent in the reference into one Row/Container cell can overflow even though each widget individually fits.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.67): moving the Bulk Freeze/Unfreeze buttons into Guest's title Row (per client request "same location as Guest") also bundled the reason `TextField` into the same fixed-width Container, because the TextField visually sits near the buttons in Guest's UI — but Guest's own `generated_code/` shows the TextField is actually a separate, later sibling in the outer Column, never part of the title Row at all. The bundled Container (now wider, holding a TextField + 2 buttons) exceeded the space left by the title Row's `Expanded` sibling and threw a `RenderFlex` overflow, which broke that page's entire rendering — the client reported it as "the list page isn't displaying," not as a visible overflow banner. **A `RenderFlex` overflow in a page's own top-level layout (as opposed to inside a dialog, §18.63) can present as a blank/non-rendering page rather than a visible error — if a client reports a page "not showing" right after any title-row/flex-row insertion, re-check that page's own `generated_code/` for a layout exception before assuming the bug is in navigation.** Always re-derive a reference's actual parent/sibling structure from `generated_code/` before grouping widgets into one insertion, not just its rendered position.
- **When a client asks to make widget X "identical" to a reference widget, don't stop at style (color/size) - re-read the reference's own conditional/visibility logic too, since "identical style" and "identical functionality" are separate axes that can both be wrong independently.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.68): 3 rounds of work (§18.64/§18.65/§18.67) built Cast/Staff/Administrator's Freeze/Unfreeze buttons as an unconditional pair (both always visible), never noticing Guest's OWN native buttons only show ONE at a time (`if (!FFAppState().activeIsFrozen) [Freeze] if (FFAppState().activeIsFrozen) [Unfreeze]`, set by its search dialog's frozen-status filter) - confirmed by reading a PRE-EXISTING permanent raw-patch block already in `dsl/edit.dart` (`styleButton`/`isFrozenVariable`) that had been sitting there, untouched, the whole time. Before declaring a "match the reference" task done, grep the reference's OWN `generated_code/` for `if (` / `Visibility(` around the widget in question, not just its `FFButtonOptions`/color/size.
- **`ensureInsertedInto`'s "safely rerunnable" property (§18.64) only guarantees the OUTERMOST inserted node's key survives a content-changing rerun - not any nested child's.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.68), reading the SDK's own `_InsertIntoOp.apply`/`_replaceNodePreservingKey` directly: when a rerun's widget content differs from what's already there, it preserves `existing.key` (the root being updated) but takes a full `deepCopy()` of the newly-compiled replacement for everything inside it - so a raw-patch keyed to a nested child (e.g. a button inside a re-styled row) breaks the same way `ensureReplaced` reruns always have, even though the OUTER call is `ensureInsertedInto`, not `ensureReplaced`. Generalizes §18.64's `ensureReplaced`-only version of this rule to any edit operation with update-in-place semantics.
- **A raw-string `AppState('name')`/`State('name')` reference is only accepted while the typed handle doesn't exist yet in the currently-loaded SDK context (bootstrapping a field in the same push that declares it via `app.state(...)`) - once that push lands and the typed SDK refreshes, `compileDslApp` starts REQUIRING the typed handle for that same field on every subsequent push.** Confirmed 2026-08-04 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.68): `AppState('castActiveIsFrozen')` compiled fine in the push that also declared `app.state('castActiveIsFrozen', bool_)`, but the very next push failed with `Use ff.AppState.castActiveIsFrozen instead of a raw app-state name "castActiveIsFrozen"` once `ff.AppState.castActiveIsFrozen` existed. Switch string references to the typed `ff.AppState.*`/`ff.Pages.*.state.*` handle as soon as the declaring push has landed - don't leave them as strings past that point.
- **`compileDslApp` succeeding is not proof every raw-patched key reference in `dsl/edit.dart` still resolves against LIVE server state - it only validates the shape of what the CURRENT run constructs/patches, not whether a PAST push's raw-bound key still exists.** Confirmed 2026-08-04/05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.69): an external edit (outside this workspace, via the FlutterFlow builder directly) deleted `StaffBulkFreezeReasonField`; re-running `dsl/edit.dart` self-healed the deletion via `ensureInsertedInto` (safe, per the entries above) but necessarily gave the re-inserted field a NEW key, since `ensureInsertedInto` only preserves a key when updating an EXISTING same-named match, not on a genuine re-insert-after-deletion. An earlier push's `reason` binding (a raw-patched `FFVariable` pointing at the OLD key) was left dangling - `compileDslApp` passed clean every time, and NO `inspect` variant (`--outline`/`--tree`/`--debug`/`--dsl-json`) surfaced it, because none of them cross-check "does this bound variable's target key still exist." **Only the FlutterFlow builder's own Issues panel (bell/warning icon, top toolbar) caught it** ("Custom action argument 'reason' is not set properly"), and only the full Flutter codegen pass (`flutterflow ai refresh-context`/`codegen refresh`, or `run`'s own export step) fails on it - a stricter, later validation layer than anything `compileDslApp` runs. When `compileDslApp` succeeds but codegen still fails with no further CLI detail (`Error generating code for the project. Make sure there are no project errors.` is the full message, no specifics), ask the client for a screenshot of the builder's Issues panel rather than continuing to guess from CLI output alone - it names the exact broken property.
- **`generated_code/firebase/firestore.rules` is FlutterFlow's OWN tracked copy of the rules, not necessarily what's actually live-deployed, on any project (like this one) also managed by a separate `firebase deploy`-based workflow outside FlutterFlow.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.69): this file shows `create`/`read: if true` on almost every collection except `users` - directly contradicting §18.54's confirmed, curl-verified LIVE tightening from 2026-08-03. Reconciled via CLAUDE.md's own intro: this workspace is "carried over from a separate local git-based workflow," and that OTHER workflow's `firebase deploy` bypasses FlutterFlow's own rules tracking entirely - FlutterFlow's internal copy (and the "Firestore Rules Setup" builder warning checking against it) never learned about the external tightening. **This workspace's DSL has no Firestore-rules-authoring surface and no "Has Private Data" toggle at all** - there is no tool here to make that warning disappear. Never touch Firestore rules speculatively based on what this file shows; it can be stale in EITHER direction. Flag the warning text to the client and let them route it to whichever surface actually owns the live rules, rather than guessing from this file.
- **`.withDefault(...)` on a page/component param only accepts static literals (String/num/bool/Map/List, serialized via `jsonEncode`) - it can never reference `AppState(...)` or any other dynamic expression, so it can't redirect a param's data source, only give it a fallback VALUE within the same source.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.70), reading the SDK's own `_serializeTypeDefault`/`_serializeLiteral` in `compiler.dart` directly. If a JSON-typed page param's TRANSPORT itself is suspected as a failure point (e.g. a large object round-tripped through a URL query string), giving it a default only fixes the "missing/required" sub-case, not a transport-level failure - the real fix needs a different data channel (AppState) plus rebinding every consumer of the old param, which is NOT achievable via `.withDefault(...)` or any other param-level DSL config.
- **`ReservationdetailsPage` no longer has a `reservation` page param at all (superseded twice, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.71 then §18.72) - it reads `AppState('selectedReservation')` instead, set by `ReservationListPage`'s row immediately before it navigates, with NOTHING passed via the URL for this route anymore.** The original JSON-page-param design (§18.22) was the cause of a client-reported blank screen (a large object round-tripped through a URL query string, unlike every other detail page in this project, which passes a short String id and re-fetches via a Custom Action). §18.70's `.withDefault(...)` fixed only the "missing param" sub-case; §18.71 moved every WIDGET-level consumer off the param onto AppState but LEFT the param still being passed via the Navigate action's `queryParameters` (reasoned as "harmless since nothing reads it" - wrong, since the ROUTER itself still had to decode it before the widget could even be built); §18.72 finally removed the param declaration entirely and stripped the pass, confirmed via `generated_code/` that the router's builder for this route takes zero params and the Navigate call has no `queryParameters:` at all. **A page param being unread by any widget binding does not make it safe to keep passing - the router's own decode of a `ParamType.JSON` value happens independently of whether anything downstream uses the result.** Verify via `generated_code/` (`grep -c "FFAppState().selectedReservation"` vs `grep -c "widget!.reservation"` on the widget file, AND check the router's `FFRoute` builder signature in `lib/flutter_flow/nav/nav.dart` takes no arguments) if in doubt.
- **`flutterflow ai run` has a PREFLIGHT validation stage distinct from and running AFTER `compileDslApp` - a script can compile clean and still be rejected at preflight.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.72): removing a Navigate action's parameter pass while the destination page still DECLARED that param (even with a default value) compiled fine but failed with `Preflight failed: <Page>. — Navigates to <Page> but may be missing param "<name>"`. FlutterFlow won't let a Navigate skip a param the destination still declares. If removing a param pass, remove the destination's param DECLARATION too (`params.removeParam(name)`) in the same push, not just the pass.
- **A raw-patch operation documented as a "no-op on rerun" is not the same as the WHOLE block being safe to leave live indefinitely - argument EXPRESSIONS feeding that operation still evaluate every run, even when the operation itself won't apply the result.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.72): two old blocks (ForceCancel/ManualRefund's `resId` argument patches) had documented themselves as permanent no-ops once their target became unreachable behind a confirm-dialog `conditionActions` wrapper from an earlier push - true for the PATCH itself, but each one's argument was still built via `_pageParamJsonFieldVariable(..., paramName: 'reservation', ...)`, a call that does its own lookup and throws if the param no longer exists, regardless of whether the patch it feeds would have done anything. Removing the `reservation` param (the fix above) turned these previously-silent no-ops into hard `compileDslApp` failures. When retiring something a param/field/key depends on, grep for every construction of a variable/argument that names it, not just every PATCH operation that uses it - a dead-on-arrival patch can still have a live, throwing setup step.
- **When rebinding many independent widget-level variable references (e.g. moving a whole page's field bindings from one data source to another), there is no single "read once" point to patch at the DSL/proto level - each widget holds its OWN `FFVariable`, and a generic recursive tree-walker mutating `source`/`baseVariable` in place (leaving each widget's own `operations`/JSONPath untouched) is the only way to do this without touching dozens of individual bindings by hand.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.71): `ReservationdetailsPage` had ~29 `Text` widgets each independently bound to `PageParam('reservation')['ownField']` - no shared variable/local point existed anywhere in the compiled tree to intercept. Wrote a generic walker (`_rebindParamTextVariablesToAppStateDeep`) that recurses through `node.children`, and at each node checks `props.text.textValue.variable` for a match by source+param-key, rewriting only `source`/`baseVariable` (and clearing the now-meaningless `nodeKeyRef`) while leaving `operations` alone. Reusable pattern for any future "migrate a whole page off one data source onto another" task.
- **`src/ui/*.dart`/`src/helpers/*.dart` internal SDK files are excellent reference material for learning a raw proto shape, but a class/function found there is not necessarily callable from `dsl/edit.dart` - check `flutterflow_ai.dart`'s own barrel export before relying on it.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.71): `src/ui/actions.dart`'s `Actions.updateAppState(...)`/`StateFieldUpdate`/`generateActionKey()` looked exactly right for building a raw "set AppState" action but aren't exported at all (only `src/dsl/actions.dart`'s differently-scoped, SAME-NAMED typed `Actions` class, e.g. `Actions.chain([...])`, is public) - failed with `Undefined name 'Actions'`. Fixed by reconstructing the same shape directly from plain proto message types (`FFAction`, `FFLocalStateUpdate`, `FFLocalStateFieldUpdate`, `FFValue`), which ARE exported via `flutterflow_schema.dart` even when the convenience wrapper isn't - the message types are essentially always available; the ergonomic helper functions sometimes aren't.
- **Before adding a new raw patch for a custom-action argument, `grep` the whole file for that argument's own opaque key first - if a comment elsewhere says a block "must reapply every rerun" for that same argument, THAT block (whichever is registered LAST) is what actually wins, not yours.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.71): a brand-new patch for `adminGetReservationExtras`'s `resId` argument (key `bqmvcmk0`) compiled clean and pushed clean, but was silently overwritten by a PRE-EXISTING permanently-reapplied patch for the exact same argument, registered ~4900 lines further down the same file, paired with an `ensureActions` that resets a placeholder value on every rerun. `compileDslApp` succeeding is not proof a raw patch's effect survived to the end of the script - if another block touches the same target later in registration order, IT wins. The right fix is to edit that other (winning) block directly, not add a second competing one and hope for the best.
- **The `firebase` CLI is available and pre-authenticated in this environment (confirmed 2026-08-05, PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.73) - `firebase functions:log --project icoccha-admin-dashboard --only <functionName>` reads REAL server-side stack traces, and `firebase firestore:indexes --project icoccha-admin-dashboard` reads the REAL live index list (never trust the local `firestore.indexes.json` - confirmed empty/stale yet again this round, matching the standing warning already in this file).** `generated_code/` and `dart analyze` can only diagnose the CLIENT side - they cannot see a backend exception. When a bug involves a Custom Action anywhere in the failing flow (even one that looks tangential, e.g. a page-load side-fetch unrelated to the reported symptom) and client-side static analysis has been exhausted, reach for `firebase functions:log` BEFORE further frontend changes - it can find in one command what days of `generated_code/` reading cannot (in this case: an `Unhandled error... FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index`, the exact same recurring "new filter/sort combination, no index yet" bug class already hit multiple times in this project's history, §18.18 among others).
- **Firestore composite indexes are DIRECTION-SENSITIVE per field - an index with `created_at DESCENDING` does not satisfy a query sorting `created_at ASCENDING`, even though every other field matches.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.73): `adminGetReservationExtras` needed `res_id ASC, type ASC, created_at ASC, __name__ ASC`; an index already existed for the SAME fields except `created_at DESCENDING` (built earlier for `adminGetTipsByReservation`'s own descending-sort query) and did not cover it. Firestore's own error message includes a base64-encoded `create_composite` URL parameter naming the EXACT index needed field-by-field, including direction - decode it (`base64.urlsafe_b64decode`, pad to a multiple of 4 first) rather than guessing from the human-readable text, since the text alone doesn't show direction. When adding a composite index to fix a `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, always reconstruct the FULL current live list first (`firebase firestore:indexes`) and write the complete file back with the new entry added - `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` replaces the whole file's contents, so deploying a partial list would delete every index not included.
- **A "blank screen, zero console errors" symptom is a DIFFERENT bug class than "blank screen with a network/console error" - don't keep investigating the same layer once the error disappears.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.74): a `ListView` with no `shrinkWrap: true` inside a `Column(mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min, ...)` throws "vertical viewport was given unbounded height" as a `RenderBox`/layout-constraint assertion, not a thrown Dart exception in application code - it does not reliably appear as a browser `console.error` the way an uncaught Future rejection (e.g. a failed Cloud Function call) does. When a client reports a console error, then reports "still blank, but now no errors" after that specific error is fixed, treat this as a signal that a SECOND, independent bug was masked behind the first, not that the first fix failed - go looking for a silent layout/constraint bug (this project's own established pattern from §18.63: `Column(mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min)` + a bare `ListView`/`GridView`/other Sliver-based viewport with no `shrinkWrap: true` or `Expanded` wrapper) rather than re-litigating the data-transport layer.
- **A `Viewport`-based widget (`ListView`, `GridView`, etc.) needs a bounded parent constraint to lay out AT ALL - it fails identically whether its `itemCount` is 0 or 50, so an empty/never-populated list does NOT prove this class of bug is absent.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.74): two `ListView`s on `ReservationdetailsPage` had this exact bug from the day they were built, but it stayed invisible because the data feeding them (`reservationExtrasList`) never successfully populated - the backend call feeding it was failing for an entirely unrelated reason (§18.73's missing Firestore index) the whole time. Fixing an unrelated upstream bug can be what finally exposes a pre-existing, never-before-reached downstream bug - this is not evidence the upstream fix was wrong, it's what let execution finally reach further than it ever had before. When a fix is confirmed correct but the symptom persists in a new form, check whether the fix simply advanced the failure point further into the flow rather than assuming the diagnosis was wrong.
- **`removeParam(...)` and any raw-patch that deletes a map entry (e.g. `someMap.remove(key)`) are ONE-SHOT operations, exactly like `ensureReplaced`/`app.state`/`app.customAction` - they must be commented out immediately after landing, not just `ensure*`-style CREATION calls.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.74): two blocks from an earlier round's own fix (§18.72's `removeParam('reservation')` and its Navigate-parameter-pass removal) were left live after landing successfully, and blocked the very next unrelated push with `Invalid argument(s): No parameter named "reservation"...` / `Bad state: Expected a "qc3zdc"... parameter pass to remove` - both throw when their target no longer exists, which is guaranteed true on any rerun once the removal has already succeeded once. Generalizes the existing one-shot-cleanup rule: it's not just about what CREATES something, anything that SUBTRACTS something is equally one-shot.
- **The `ensureInsertedInto`/`ensureMovedTo`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` structural editing API is idempotent ONLY when the destination's compiled DSL shape stays constant across reruns - it is UNSAFE for "move pre-existing named nodes into a freshly-authored wrapper" edits, and can silently DELETE content on rerun.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.75), found by reading `_InsertIntoOp`/`_InsertBeforeOp`'s own source before using them: these ops detect "already applied" by comparing the freshly-recompiled DSL widget against whatever node currently occupies the destination, and REPLACE wholesale on any mismatch.
