If the wrapper's DSL source defines its content slot as empty (`children: []`, meant to be filled by a separate `ensureMovedTo` afterward), a rerun sees the live node (now populated) not match the freshly-compiled widget (still empty) and replaces the whole subtree with the empty version - silently discarding whatever was moved in, no error. **For "move existing content into a new container" edits specifically, use a raw one-shot `.children` list splice instead**: compile the new wrapper via `compileDslWidgetForExistingWidgetClass(project, widgetClassName:, widget:, app:)` (a fully public function from `src/dsl/compiler.dart`, unlike the internal `src/ui/actions.dart` helpers - see the entry above on internal SDK helpers not being exported), pull the target `FFNode`s out of their current parent's `.children` (`indexWhere` + `removeAt` - `FFNode.children` is a mutable `PbList<FFNode>` supporting normal `List` operations), splice them into the compiled wrapper's own `.children`, then append the wrapper into its destination. Treat the whole block as one-shot, like every other raw patch in this file - comment it out immediately after it lands.
- **When `generated_code/`'s indentation is ambiguous about real node nesting (e.g. a percent-width `Container` compiles to a multi-level `Row > Expanded > Container` in Flutter that doesn't correspond 1:1 to FFNode depth), use `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --page/--component <name> --outline` (optionally `--selector-key <key>` to scope + `--max-depth <N>`) instead of guessing from generated-code bracket nesting.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.75): reverse-engineering `ReservationdetailsPage`'s real structure from `generated_code/`'s indentation alone gave a wrong mental model (assumed the admin-action sections were nested inside the same scrolling content column as 基本情報/当事者情報); the `--outline` CLI flag gave the real, authoritative FFNode tree in one call and immediately revealed they were actually siblings of the sidebar+content Row instead - the actual root cause of why the section looked visually disconnected.
- **`AppState(...)['field']` (the DSL's `[]` field-access operator) does NOT compile against a JSON-typed app-state field - it only works against a declared struct/Firestore-collection/Postgres-table target.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.76), found by reading `_compileVariable`'s `FieldAccess` case in `compiler.dart` before assuming otherwise: `_inferExpressionType` returns `null` for a plain `JSON` app-state field (like `selectedReservation`), which then hits `UnsupportedError('Field access requires a struct or document target...')`. There is no dedicated JSON-path `DslExpression` at the widget-builder layer either (checked every subclass in `references.dart`). For binding a Text/value widget to a `$.field` path on a JSON app-state field, use the established two-step recipe (first proven in §18.71): declare the widget with a placeholder literal + a `name:` marker, then a raw `app.raw(...)` pass finds it by name and sets `props.text.textValue.variable` to `_appStateJsonFieldVariable(appStateName, appStateKey, jsonPath)`. This is unrelated to `ItemRef()['field']`/`item['field']` inside a `ListView` builder, which DOES work - that goes through a separate, special-cased compiler path (`_compileGeneratorItemFieldAccess`) keyed off the collection/struct's declared item type, not the generic `FieldAccess` path.
- **`page.ensureActions(...)` silently no-ops instead of replacing an EXISTING native (visual-designer-authored) `ShowDialog`/`alertDialog.customDialog` trigger when only the dialog's TARGET component changes - it only reliably works for adding a trigger where none existed, or where the action's outward "shape" also changes.** Confirmed twice now: first for the Staff/Administrator search→info button conversion (pre-existing fix, `_patchDialogTarget` helper), and again 2026-08-05 for `ReservationdetailsPage`'s own search→info button (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.76) - `ensureActions` pushed with no error both times, but `generated_code/` still showed the OLD dialog target (`SearchUserDialogCompWidget()`) unchanged afterward. Root cause: `_EnsureTriggerOp`'s semantic-equality check doesn't treat a different `componentClassNodeKeyRef` as a "difference" for this action type. **Always re-check `generated_code/` for the new widget class name after any `ShowDialog.component(...)` push that targets a button with a PRE-EXISTING native trigger - a clean `run` exit is not proof the retarget landed.** Fix: use the already-built `_patchDialogTarget(node, {newComponentKey})` raw helper instead, which walks `node.triggerActions` directly and repoints `alertDialog.customDialog`'s `componentClassNodeKeyRef.key` in place. Its sibling `_patchDialogSize(node, {width, height})` is usually needed alongside it - retargeting doesn't resize the dialog's WRAPPER Container, which stays at whatever size the OLD (native-authored) dialog needed and will clip a differently-sized new component.
- **A JSON-path-bound Text/Button showing the literal string "null" is a MISSING `FFVariable.defaultValue`, not a widget-content or codegen bug.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.77): `getJsonField(...)` returns Dart `null` when a path doesn't resolve, and `.toString()` on a `dynamic` null returns the literal string `"null"` (it does not throw) - that's exactly what renders. Setting a default makes codegen switch the WHOLE expression shape server-side, from bare `getJsonField(...).toString()` to null-safe `valueOrDefault<String>(getJsonField(...)?.toString(), 'X')` - this is not something to hand-write via DSL string manipulation, it's a proto field. **The field is `FFVariable.defaultValue` (an `FFParameterValue`, e.g. `FFParameterValue(serializedValue: '-')`) - it lives on the `FFVariable` itself, NOT on `FFStringValue`, `FFText`, or `FFJsonPathOperation`** (all three look like plausible homes and were checked first; none carry a live non-deprecated default-value field - `FFStringValue.inputValue` is a `oneof` sibling of `variable`, not a fallback alongside it). Found empirically by diffing a working example (`GuestUserdetailsPage`'s ニックネーム field, which already had a default) against a broken one via `flutterflow ai inspect ... --tree`, not by reading the schema alone - the schema didn't make the right field obvious. For a project-wide sweep of this class of bug, walk every `FFWidgetClass` in `project.widgetClasses.values` (one unified map covering pages AND components) and set `defaultValue` on any `props.text`/`props.button.text` (`FFButton` reuses `FFText`) `textValue.variable` that lacks one AND has a `jsonPathOperation` in its `operations` chain - the condition is exactly "nothing left to do" on a rerun, so it's safe to leave live/idempotent, no comment-out needed.
- **`FFConditionalValue` (FlutterFlow's native "Conditional Value" mechanism) is NOT a reliable way to drive a widget's Color property dynamically - hit two consecutive opaque server-side validator rejections on every attempt and had to abandon it for a custom Dart function instead.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.78). First rejection ("Conditional value configuration is invalid"): the SDK's own unexported `conditionalValueVar` reference helper (`src/helpers/function_call_helpers.dart`) reveals a `returnParameter: FFParameter` field the schema doesn't document as required anywhere. Second rejection, even with `returnParameter` set to `FFBaseDataType.Color` ("Color from string configuration is invalid" after switching to a String-typed conditional wrapped in `colorFromString`): confirmed by pulling a REAL, already-deployed `conditionalValue` from this exact project via `inspect --tree` (a dialog-result message) that `Color` isn't an accepted `returnParameter` scalar type for this mechanism at all, and no real example of `colorFromString` exists anywhere in this project to debug the second failure against. **When you need a widget property driven by multi-branch logic that the DSL widget-builder layer can't express (e.g. `Icon.color`'s `ColorToken` is a `sealed class` with only `NamedColor`/`HexColor`, no expression variant), prefer a small custom Dart function (`addCustomFunction` + `FFFunctionCall(customFunction: id, values: [...])`) over hand-building `FFConditionalValue` - it's a plain, thoroughly-proven mechanism (`CustomFunctionInvocation` is used throughout `compiler.dart`) with none of this proto's delicate, undocumented required-field traps.** If `FFConditionalValue` must be used for something else in the future, always cross-check the exact proto shape against a REAL already-deployed instance in the live project (`inspect --tree`, grep for `"conditionalValue"`) rather than the schema/SDK docs alone - the schema didn't reveal `returnParameter`'s necessity, and the SDK's own reference helper for it isn't even exported.
- **`addCustomFunction` is a one-shot declaration, same family as `app.component`/`app.customAction`/`removeParam` - `CustomCodeDuplicateError` on any rerun once it has landed.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.78). Unlike a page/component's root node key (which the SDK returns from a live push, e.g. §18.76's `ReservationInfoDialogComp`), a custom function's `FFIdentifier` key is assigned INSIDE `addCustomFunction` itself and returned synchronously in-process - so after the one successful push, comment out the declaration and replace the identifier with a literal `FFIdentifier(name:, key:)`, reading the real server-assigned key back via `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --custom-function <name>` (prints it under "Key:").
- **A raw-patch block from an EARLIER, unrelated task can silently overwrite a NEW fix if it targets the same node and sits LATER in `dsl/edit.dart` - this is the same "registration order, last block wins" class of bug §18.71 first hit, and it recurred here with zero warning or error.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.78): an older theme-centralization cleanup block (~line 8583) unconditionally reset `WithdrawalApplicationListPage`'s `Icon_w4srqier` to a fixed `FlutterFlowTheme.of(context).success` on every run; since it sits later in the file than a brand-new conditional-color fix for that same icon, it silently won on every push with no error - only caught by re-checking `generated_code/` after the push instead of trusting a clean `run` exit. **Before landing a raw-patch on a specific node key, grep the whole file for that key first** - if it appears elsewhere, check whether the other occurrence runs later (wins) and needs updating/removing, not just the new block being added.
- **The "silent unbounded-height ListView layout crash" bug class (§18.63, §18.74/75) is now confirmed a THIRD time (§18.79, `KYCReviewListPage`) - and this occurrence revealed an important refinement: when the ListView in question is built via a DSL `ListView(...)` call inside a `page.ensureReplaced(...)`, fix `shrinkWrap` IN THE DSL SOURCE, never as a separate raw patch on the compiled node.** `page.ensureReplaced(...)` has NO semantic-equality no-op path - `_ReplaceOp.apply()` is an unconditional `replaceByKey` on every single run (same fact §18.78 already established for Cast/Staff/Administrator's row templates) - so it rebuilds that whole subtree fresh, with brand-new random keys, on EVERY execution of this script, for ANY reason, not just changes to that specific page. A raw patch targeting a key captured from one `inspect`/`generated_code/` snapshot would be silently wiped out the next time this script runs for an unrelated task, reproducing the exact same bug report later with no obvious connection to what caused it. **Diagnostic checklist for a NEW "blank screen" report on any page with a list**: (1) rule out the reported "page was deleted" framing first, empirically (`flutterflow ai resources`, check the route target chain end-to-end) rather than assuming it - it's rarely literally true; (2) check every `ListView`/`GridView` on the page in `generated_code/` for a missing `shrinkWrap: true` when nested under `SingleChildScrollView` or a `mainAxisSize.min` `Column`; (3) before patching, grep `dsl/edit.dart` for whether that ListView is DSL-authored via `page.ensureReplaced(...)` (fix the `ListView(...)` call's own `shrinkWrap:` parameter directly) vs. a native/one-shot-landed subtree (a raw patch on the compiled node, per §18.63/74/75, is fine there since nothing rebuilds it on every run).
- **For "the styling looks bad, make it match the rest of the site" requests, reuse this project's own existing row-styling helpers (`_userListCardRowContent`/`_fieldPairBox`/`_statusValue`, established for `GuestUserListPage`, matched by Cast/Staff/AdministratorListPage in §18.56) instead of inventing a new look.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.80, `KYCReviewListPage`'s row): `_fieldPairBox` takes `DslWidget` value slots, not just text, so it can host non-text content (an `Image`, in this case) directly rather than needing a bespoke variant - check whether the existing helper's SHAPE actually fits before writing a new one. Since these list rows are DSL-authored via `page.ensureReplaced(...)` (per the entry above), any status dot restyled this way needs its color wired through the SAME name-based `_patchStatusDotByName` + real `functionId` pattern as every other list, not left as another static placeholder color - "match the site's style" includes color-by-status, which is now an established site-wide convention (§18.78), not just spacing/borders.
- **When restyling a widget that already has hand-written `triggerActions` worth preserving (a confirm-gated button, a custom-action call chain, etc.), NEVER reach for `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedInto` on that node - both compile a brand-new node from scratch and silently discard its entire existing action chain with no warning.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.81, `KycReviewDialogComp`'s restyle): its 2 buttons carry the §18.40 confirm-gate fix (tapping "Cancel" must not still fire `adminApproveKYC`) - restyling them via `component.update(selection, (patch) => ...)` instead (a pure property patch - `borderRadius`/`padding`/`color`/etc. - that never touches `triggerActions` at all) preserved that logic completely, confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged in `generated_code/` afterward. `ensureReplaced` is only safe for genuinely INERT nodes (plain `Text`/`Image`/layout containers with no `onTap`/triggers) - always check `--outline`/`--tree` for existing `triggerActions` on a node before deciding whether a restyle can use `ensureReplaced` or must stick to `component.update(...)`/raw property patches.
- **A component/dialog with no explicit `width` anywhere (own root, AND no fixed-size wrapper `Container` at its call site) can render with an unconstrained intrinsic width and overflow the viewport - especially if it contains a `Row` of `Expanded` children, which need a bounded ancestor width to divide.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.81): `KycReviewDialogComp` is opened via a native `showAlignedDialog` call with no wrapping size `Container` (unlike `Info_DialogComp`/`ReservationInfoDialogComp`, both wrapped in a fixed `Container(height:, width:)` at their call site) and its own root `Container` never had a `width` either - it rendered well past the right edge of the screen. When a client reports a modal "overflowing"/"cut off", check for a missing `width` FIRST, on both the component's own root AND whatever wraps it at the call site - not just color/spacing choices.
- **A `Container(borderRadius: N, ...)` value set via the DSL widget-builder CONSTRUCTOR (inside `ensureReplaced`/a fresh widget literal) does not always compile literally - confirmed one real mismatch (`borderRadius: 14` compiled to `16.0`), cause not fully identified.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.82, `KycReviewDialogComp`'s photo frames). By contrast, `component.update(selection, (patch) => patch.borderRadius(N))` (a property PATCH on an existing node, different code path) and `Image(...).borderRadius` both compile literally - confirmed by direct before/after `generated_code/` diffs at several values. No other `Container(...)` constructor call anywhere else in this project used a non-"clean" radius (everything else is 4/8/12/16/20/24/60), so this had never surfaced before and there's no wider pattern to generalize from yet. **Do not trust a DSL-declared `borderRadius:` constructor value as the actual compiled value - always re-check `generated_code/` directly (grep the specific `BorderRadius.circular(...)` line) before reasoning about geometry/spacing that depends on it**, especially before computing a "concentric"/nested-radius fix from it (see next entry - this exact mistake caused a first fix attempt to fail and get correctly called out by the client as still-broken).
- **FlutterFlow's own `VALIDATION_CONCENTRIC_BORDER_RADIUS` check (surfaced in the Builder's "Optimizations & Enhancements" panel as "Concentric Border Radius") does NOT use the textbook `innerRadius = outerRadius - padding` nested-corner formula - empirically, it requires zero content-inset (`padding`) between the two rounded shapes, not a formula-adjusted inner radius.** Confirmed 2026-08-05 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.82) by testing 4 real combinations through `flutterflow ai validate` and reading each run's own `.flutterflow/traces/<run-id>.json` `diagnostics` array: outer=16/inner=10 (the "correct" formula pair) still flags; outer=16/inner=16 (exact match) with padding still at 6 still flags; outer=16/inner=16 with `padding` ALSO patched to 0 clears it. **When this diagnostic (or any other Builder-panel-only warning with no CLI-exposed detail) needs debugging, use `flutterflow ai issue candidates` / `issue show <id>` and the per-run trace's `diagnostics` array (filter by `code`) as a non-visual, authoritative, no-push-needed verification loop** - `flutterflow ai validate` writes a fresh trace every call, so hypotheses can be tested locally in ~20s instead of waiting on a screenshot from the client or guessing blind. If a "padded frame" look needs to survive a fix like this, swap the padding-created inset for a matching-color `border:` ring instead of removing it outright - confirmed this also clears the check while keeping a similar framed visual.
- **A native "Generate Dynamic Children" row's per-widget local state (e.g. a `Checkbox`'s own `_model.xValueMap`, auto-generated just from dropping the widget on the canvas) CANNOT be written from a DIFFERENT widget's action chain - `SetFormField`/`WidgetState` and the lower-level `FFLocalStateUpdate` can only target fields in the widget class's OWN declared `classModel.stateFields` list, and this kind of auto-generated per-widget map is never one of them.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.83, `ReportListPage`'s freeze checkbox): tried to wire a label's tap to toggle an adjacent Checkbox's own per-row value via `SetFormField(checkboxHandle, Not(WidgetState(checkboxHandle, .value)))` - `compileDslApp` rejected it outright ("...trying to set a field that doesn't exist or is dynamic"), confirmed by checking `inspect --page <name>`'s own `classModel.stateFields` output and finding the checkbox's map genuinely absent from it. This is the SAME structural class of limitation as §18.50's `TextField`-in-dynamic-row finding (a per-row value with no externally-addressable identity) - reading a dynamic row's own widget state from elsewhere in the SAME row (e.g. via `WIDGET_STATE`/`nodeKeyRef` in a CONDITION, as the pre-existing freeze-gate does) works fine; WRITING to it from outside the widget's own built-in handler does not, and there is no raw-proto workaround - don't spend time hunting for one.
- **A `SetFormField`/`WidgetState` widget reference by NAME can be ambiguous even when the name looks unique, because `Checkbox(label:)`'s `_labelRow` builder helper (`lib/src/ui/ui.dart`) gives BOTH the checkbox itself AND its auto-generated wrapping wrapper Row the SAME `name`.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.83): `inspect --outline` showed `Checkbox_8aee2fsi` and its parent `Row_r82jwadq` both named `"FreezeReportedUserCheckbox"` - `compileDslApp` rejected the name-string reference ("Widget name ... is ambiguous"). Fix: reference the widget through its typed SDK handle instead (`ff.Pages.<page>.widgets.byKey('<realKey>').single`), which resolves unambiguously by key regardless of name collisions.
- **Locating a specific `FFActionNode` by its own `.key` anywhere inside a trigger chain needs a recursive walker covering BOTH `followUpAction` and `conditionActions` branches (`trueActions[].trueAction` + `falseAction`) - the simpler linear `while (current.hasFollowUpAction())` walkers already used elsewhere in this file (e.g. the `BulkFreeze`/`WithdrawalApplicationListPage` patches) only follow straight chains and silently miss anything nested inside a branch.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.83) needing to reach a freeze action buried inside 2 levels of `conditionActions` on `ReportListPage`'s resolve button. Also worth remembering: `findByKey` only walks the WIDGET tree (`FFNode`) - it cannot find an `FFActionNode` at all, since action-chain keys are a completely separate keyspace from widget node keys. Reusable helper added: `_findActionNodeByKey(FFActionNode node, String key)`.
- **Binding ANY widget property to a native dynamic row's own auto-generated per-widget state map (a `Checkbox`/`Switch`'s `_model.xValueMap[item]`) crashes with "Unexpected null value" on first render if the READING widget is declared BEFORE the SEEDING widget in their shared parent - always add `FFVariable.defaultValue` when reading one of these, don't assume the map is already populated.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.84, a real client-facing crash from this file's own §18.83 work): the map entry is only seeded (`??= false`) by the Checkbox's OWN `value:` expression, the first time THAT SPECIFIC widget builds - Flutter builds a `Column`'s children top-to-bottom, so a SIBLING widget declared earlier (here, the resolve button, declared before the `Row` holding the Checkbox) reads a genuinely-missing map key on the row's very first build, before the Checkbox has ever run. Passing that `null` into a non-nullable custom-function parameter (or any non-nullable binding) throws immediately, for every row, every time - not a rare race, a guaranteed first-paint crash. Same fix as §18.77's project-wide "null shown as text" sweep - `FFVariable.defaultValue` (e.g. `FFParameterValue(serializedValue: 'false')`) - confirming that mechanism is general-purpose (works for any `FFVariable` use, not just text bindings): compiles to `valueOrDefault<bool>(_model.xValueMap[item], false)` at the exact read site. Check widget declaration ORDER inside the shared parent whenever wiring a NEW binding to an EXISTING dynamic row's own state map, not just whether the variable reference itself is correct.
- **`FFVariable.defaultValue` is NOT universally respected by every codegen path - confirmed it is silently IGNORED (inert, no-op) for a native "Generate Dynamic Children" widget's own generator SOURCE variable, unlike text/value bindings and `FUNCTION_CALL` arguments where it works (§18.77, §18.84).** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.85): set `defaultValue` on `Row_l94r8xla.generatorVariable.variable` (the `$.reports` source feeding `List.generate`), it landed on the proto (confirmed via `inspect --tree`), but `generated_code/` compiled to bare `getJsonField(...).toList()` before AND after - byte-identical. Don't assume this mechanism applies everywhere just because it worked for a text binding or a `FUNCTION_CALL` argument - verify the ACTUAL generated code after every use, per-context, especially for a genuinely new kind of binding (a generator source is structurally different from a widget property read).
- **`addCustomFunction(code:)` means the function BODY ONLY, never the full signature - passing a full definition doesn't error, it silently nests your real code as an unreachable inner local function inside the SDK's own auto-generated outer wrapper, and the outer function then always returns `null` (falls through with no `return`).** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.85, `reportResolveButtonLabel` from §18.83): passing `String reportResolveButtonLabel(bool freezeUser) { return ...; }` as `code:` compiled to `String? reportResolveButtonLabel(bool? freezeUser) { String reportResolveButtonLabel(bool freezeUser) { return ...; } }` - 100% syntactically valid Dart (a local function declaration is legal, just dead code), so `compileDslApp` AND `dart analyze` both passed clean, and every call site (`functions.x(...)!`) crashed with "Unexpected null value" on every invocation, deterministically, because the OUTER function always returned null. **A targeted `grep -A N "functionSignature"` after a custom-function push is not sufficient verification - it can match the INNER dead declaration and look like confirmation.** Always read the whole generated function with enough context to see there's a real `return` reachable from the OUTER signature. Already-live functions need `updateCustomFunction(project, name:, code:)` to fix in place - `addCustomFunction` throws `CustomCodeDuplicateError` on an existing name.
- **Custom-function parameters compile as NULLABLE in the generated Dart signature (`bool? x`) regardless of the non-nullable `FFDataTypeV2` passed to `arguments:` in `addCustomFunction`/`updateCustomFunction` - write function bodies defensively (`x ?? false`, `x ?? ''`, etc.), never assume the declared type controls nullability at the call site.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.85): fixing the dead-code bug above (previous bullet) first produced `return freezeUser ? ... : ...;` with `freezeUser` declared as `Boolean` (non-nullable) in `arguments:` - `dart analyze` still failed with "A nullable expression can't be used as a condition" because the actual generated parameter was `bool? freezeUser`. **`compileDslApp`/`flutterflow ai run` reporting success is not sufficient evidence a custom function's Dart body is valid** - it validates the DSL/proto shape, not full type-correctness of the `code:` string against its auto-generated wrapper. Always run `dart analyze` on the specific exported file(s) after ANY custom-function push (add OR update), not just after structural DSL changes.
- **`page.updateParam(name, type: someType.withDefault(x))` does NOT reliably set a page param's `defaultValue` on the live proto - confirmed inert for a native (pre-DSL) page param.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.86, `ReportListPage`'s `filterStatus`): compiled and pushed clean, but `inspect`ing the param afterward showed no `defaultValue` field at all, and the router's own `params.getParam(name, ParamType.String)` call was unchanged. If a page needs a real default value when navigated to without an explicit param, patch the actual `Navigate` action(s) at the call site(s) to pass the value explicitly instead of relying on the param's own default - find the right `Navigate` node by walking the source page's widget tree for `action.navigate.pageNodeKeyRef.key == <target page's Scaffold key>` rather than hunting for the tile/button's own node key by hand.
- **`.deepCopy()`-ing a LIVE node (e.g. `trigger.rootAction`) to build a "reusable copy" of an action chain is only safe for a ONE-TIME transform, never for logic meant to survive a rerun - once pushed, the live node IS your new structure, and the next run's `deepCopy()` captures that instead of the original.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.86): a confirm-gate rebuild that deep-copied `Button_7m1nc727`'s own chain validated and pushed fine the FIRST time, then failed the very next `compileDslApp` with `Unsupported operation: Attempted to change a read-only message (flutterflow.FFAction)` - the live chain was now the confirm-gate wrapper itself (no `.action` set at its own root), and mutating an unset message field hits protobuf's shared frozen default instance. **If an action-chain restructuring needs to be safely rerunnable, rebuild it from known LITERAL values (the project's own established action/arg keys) instead of deep-copying live state** - this is also naturally idempotent (same inputs -> same output every run), unlike a deep-copy-based transform which is inherently one-shot no matter how it looks.
- **Renaming only the DISPLAY name of an `ACTION_OUTPUTS` reference (`outputVariableIdentifier.name`) is not enough to make two copies of the same action independent - codegen resolves the display name via `actionKeyRef.key` GLOBALLY across the widget class, so two producer nodes sharing one `action.key` still alias each other's variable even with different declared names.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.86): two deep-copied branches of a resolve action, renamed only via `outputVariableIdentifier.name`, compiled to one branch ASSIGNING `_model.resolveResult1Unchecked` and then CHECKING `_model.resolveResult1Checked` (the other branch's name) on the very next line. Fix: give each copy's producer node a fresh, unique `action.key` too, and keep the condition's `actionKeyRef.key` in sync with it - not just the identifier's display name.
- **Replacing an existing action chain WHOLESALE (not extending it) can silently orphan any EARLIER raw-patch block in the same file that searches for a stable key inside the OLD chain shape - it will find nothing and throw on the very next script run, not a bug in the older block, a real compatibility break the newer rewrite caused.** Confirmed twice in one day, both in `ReportListPage`'s resolve/freeze button (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.86): a chain-wholesale-replacement broke both the original §18.50 freeze-gate wiring block AND half of §18.85's freeze-result-dialog block, since both searched for keys (`jd6489di_freeze_gate`/`jd6489di_freeze_action`) that only existed inside the pre-rewrite chain shape. **After replacing an action chain wholesale, grep the whole file for every stable key the OLD structure used and check whether any earlier block still depends on finding one of them** - comment out whatever's now-obsolete (confirm its job was fully absorbed into the new structure first) rather than leaving a block that will throw on the next rerun.
- **A native node key referenced by an EARLIER, unrelated raw-patch block can silently stop existing on the live project between one push and the next, with no local change having touched that page at all - always check `flutterflow ai validate` for failures pointing at a DIFFERENT page than the one you're editing before assuming it's your own change.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88): mid-way through unrelated `AffiliateListPage` work, `compileDslApp` started failing on a `ReportListPage` selector (`Text_u3p1f6vg`, a checkbox's own internal label from §18.83) with "found no matches" - confirmed via `inspect --dsl-json` (0 matches) that the key genuinely no longer exists live, despite this session never touching ReportListPage since it last validated successfully. Best guess: it's an implicitly compiler-generated child of a `Checkbox(label:)`, not an independently stable node, so its key isn't guaranteed durable across recompiles the way an explicitly-authored widget's key is. Since `flutterflow ai run` compiles the ENTIRE script regardless of what page you're actually trying to edit, an unrelated stale reference like this blocks EVERY future push until fixed or commented out - don't skip investigating a validate failure just because it names a page you didn't touch.
- **A `List<JSON>`-typed page-local state field (`WIDGET_CLASS_STATE`, via `editPageState`/`FFLocalStateUpdate`) cannot reliably be WRITTEN into from this DSL - same landmine already documented for `List<JSON>` App State fields (search "reservationList" above), now confirmed for page-local state too.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88): never got as far as testing the write itself, because even `listOf(json).withDefault(<Object?>[])` on a fresh field declaration failed outright with `Unsupported operation: Default values are not supported for type ListType(...)`. **Reading** a `List<JSON>` via `ACTION_OUTPUTS` + `generatorVariable` (what every native "Generate Dynamic Children" row already does) is unaffected and fully reliable - only the WRITE path (and, per this entry, even a bare default-value declaration) is the landmine. If a live-updating list is needed, don't try to own a copy of it in state at all; keep reading the original `ACTION_OUTPUTS` source directly and drive per-row overrides through small SCALAR state fields merged in via a custom function instead (see the next bullet).
- **`ACTION_OUTPUTS` → jsonPath narrowed into an `FFLocalStateUpdate` target is unreliable in this SDK even for a plain SCALAR (String) destination, not just lists.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88): writing a `String` page-state field from `_actionOutputJsonFieldVariable(...)` (reading back one field of a just-completed custom action's own result) failed `compileDslApp` with `Field "..." has an update value that is not properly set` three different ways in a row - as one multi-entry `FFLocalStateUpdate`, split into its own single-entry chained action node, and with an explicit `returnParameter` declaring the destination's scalar type. **Fix: don't read the value back through the action output at all if an equivalent value is ALREADY live somewhere else as `WIDGET_STATE`/`LOCAL_STATE`** (e.g. copy a form field's own current value directly, the same source kind FlutterFlow's own native chains already use for that exact widget) - that source kind writes into `FFLocalStateUpdate` cleanly. Reserve `ACTION_OUTPUTS`-into-state-write for cases with no live alternative, and expect to need one of these workarounds if it fails.
- **`ItemRef['field']` (the `item['x']` syntax inside a freshly-authored `ListView(itemBuilder:)`) requires the source to be struct/document/row-typed - it does not work against a plain `List<JSON>` source, even though the exact same `GENERATOR_VARIABLE` proto shape underneath supports raw JSON fine for NATIVE "Generate Dynamic Children" rows.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88): `compileDslApp` rejected `item['nickname']` against a `listOf(json)` source with "Item field access ... requires a known struct, document, or row item type." Workaround: author the item template with harmless literal placeholders (for title/subtitle/onTap values), then rebind every one of them via a `page.mutateNode` raw patch afterward - `itemTemplate.props.<widgetType>.<field>.textValue` to a `GENERATOR_VARIABLE`-sourced `_generatorVariableJsonField(generatorNodeKey: node.key, jsonPath: ...)`, and any `SetState` values by walking the item template's own `triggerActions` chain and matching `update.fieldIdentifier.name`. `page.mutateNode`'s closure only receives `FFNode node`, not `project` - anything needing `_localStateVariable(project, ...)` etc. must be built earlier (while `project` IS in scope, inside an `app.raw` block) and captured in an outer `late final` variable, not constructed inside the `mutateNode` closure itself.
- **Once a widget inserted via `ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` is live, a LATER script run referencing it by `page.findByName(...)` fails `compileDslApp`'s typed-SDK-authoring validation ("Use ff.Pages.x.widgets.byKey(...) ... instead of findByName(...)")** - the compiler starts requiring the typed handle once a named widget genuinely exists. Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88). Combined with `ensureInsertedAfter` having no dedup guard (already documented above), the correct pattern after a structural insert has landed once is: comment out the `ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` call entirely (it already did its job), and switch any later reference to it from `findByName(...)` to `findByKey('<real key>')`, reading the real key from the freshly-regenerated `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/<slug>.dart` after the push that created it.
- **Never wrap an inserted `ListView`/scrollable in `Expanded` when authoring a NEW widget subtree via this DSL - use an explicit-height `Container` instead, even if the ambient constraint chain LOOKS bounded from reading `generated_code/`.** `Expanded` requires its own parent Flex to receive a bounded max-height constraint from somewhere further up the tree; whether that actually holds depends on the FULL chain of Containers/Rows/Columns/ScrollViews above the insertion point, which is easy to misjudge by re-reading generated Dart (this project's pages commonly wrap their whole body in a `SingleChildScrollView`, which hands its child unbounded height along the scroll axis - some intermediate Containers re-bound it, some don't, and it is NOT always obvious which). Confirmed the hard way 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.88 hotfix): `Expanded(child: ListView(...))` inside a newly-inserted picker section compiled and pushed clean (`compileDslApp` does not catch this - it's a runtime Flutter layout error, "RenderFlex children have non-zero flex but incoming height constraints are unbounded"-class), but blanked the entire section on screen for the client. `Container(height: N, child: ListView(...))` is immune to this class of bug BY CONSTRUCTION regardless of ambient context - a Container's own explicit height is its preferred size either way, so prefer it over `Expanded` for any list/scrollable inserted into an EXISTING native page (as opposed to a page built with this DSL from scratch, where the whole layout skeleton - and thus every constraint - was authored by this same script and is fully known).
- **An EXPLICIT fixed `height:` on a container can produce a WORSE overflow in a smaller viewport than in a larger one, even with LESS content - because a fixed height only ever REQUESTS that size, and gets silently clamped to whatever the incoming constraint actually allows.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.92): a picker panel's host containers were resized 340→380px across two earlier fixes for a reported 14px overflow; the NEXT client report, on what looked like a smaller browser viewport, showed a 54px overflow despite the panel now defaulting to a much shorter COLLAPSED state. Bumping the number again would not have helped if the real ceiling (set by ancestors several levels up, including a `MediaQuery.sizeOf(context).height`-driven Container much higher on the page) is externally smaller than any fixed guess. **Prefer clearing the explicit height entirely over picking a bigger number**, so the container sizes to its own content within whatever space is actually available in any viewport - correct by construction. Mechanism: `FFDimensions` has a standard protobuf-generated `clearHeight()` (and `clearWidth()`) - `node.props.container.dimensions.clearHeight()` via `page.mutateNode`, no typed-patch equivalent found (`EditWidgetPatch.size()` can only SET width/height, not un-set a previously-set one).
- **A static (non-variable) `patch.visible(false)` can compile correctly into the live proto (`inspect --tree` shows `visibility.visibleValue.inputValue: false`, confirmed genuinely applied) while codegen still renders the widget completely unconditionally - no `if`/`Visibility` wrapper anywhere in the compiled Dart.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.92), on a plain `Text` node that also had an `ON_TAP` trigger attached via `ensureActions` in an earlier edit - `flutterflow ai codegen status` confirmed the local `generated_code/` snapshot was genuinely fresh, ruling out a stale-snapshot explanation. Net effect if unnoticed: the "hidden" widget renders right alongside whatever replaced it, doubling up on screen. **A clean push and a correct-looking live proto are not proof a static visibility patch actually suppresses rendering - verify the compiled `generated_code/` itself, every time, the same standing discipline already applied to everything else in this project.** When this happens, don't chase the visibility mechanism further - use `page.ensureRemoved(...)` instead (proven-working throughout this file) if the widget is safe to remove outright, which it usually is once whatever replaced it no longer depends on it as a structural anchor.
- **CORRECTION to the previous entry's own mechanism: `page.ensureRemoved(page.findByKey('...'))` is NOT actually safe to leave live once its target has really been removed - `page.findByKey(...)` (the typed page-selector) resolves and throws EAGERLY at call time (`Bad state: Selected-name <Type> "<key>" not found.`), so `ensureRemoved` never even gets a chance to no-op.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.94): a `page.ensureRemoved(page.findByKey('Text_ovc14fsx'))` call that had landed successfully in the §18.92 push blocked the very next unrelated push on this file with exactly that error, contradicting this project's own earlier (incorrect) belief that `ensureRemoved`'s internal `_NoopOp` fallback made it durable to leave live - that fallback only helps if the SELECTOR resolution itself succeeds and returns an empty match set, which `page.findByKey(...)` does not do for a truly-missing key. **Treat `page.ensureRemoved(page.findByKey(...))` as exactly as one-shot as `ensureReplaced`/`app.customAction`/etc. - comment it out immediately once confirmed landed, the same standing discipline as everything else in this file.**
- **`page.ensureReplaced(...)`'s "no dedup guard, comment out after it lands" rule (already documented extensively above) applies even to a page's ORIGINAL CRUD build-out, not just later restyle/hotfix follow-ups - and going undetected for a long time doesn't mean it's safe.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.94): `CocomiseListPage`'s very first `ensureReplaced(...)` call (which originally built `CocomiseShopListView`, back when the page's CRUD was first wired up) had been left live for the rest of this project's history simply because nobody had re-run this script's `CocomiseListPage` section since - it only surfaced when a later, unrelated `shrinkWrap` fix tried to `findByKey` the ListView by its known-live key and got `found no matches`, because this earlier block had ALREADY silently replaced it with a fresh key earlier in the SAME compile pass. **When a raw-patch/`findByKey` fix on an established, long-untouched page fails with "found no matches" despite the key being confirmed correct via a fresh `inspect`/typed-SDK read, grep the whole file for an earlier LIVE `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter` targeting that same page before assuming the key itself is wrong** - the earlier block may be silently re-executing and replacing the node on every run, not just its original one.
- **A component widget class's own `component.root` is often a SYNTHETIC `"isDummyRoot": true` wrapper (confirmed via `inspect --tree`) that codegen ignores entirely for rendering - restyling it (border/radius/shadow/dimensions) has ZERO effect on the compiled widget, whether via typed `component.update(component.root, ...)` OR raw `component.mutateNode(component.root, ...)`.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.95, `CocomiseEditDialogComp`'s modal restyle): both attempts compiled and pushed clean with no error, and the RAW-proto attempt even verifiably wrote the correct decoration values server-side (`inspect --tree` showed them present on `Container_39h35kpe`) - yet `generated_code/` never changed. `component.root` IS reliable for ACTION wiring (`ensureActions(component.root, ...)` works fine, used successfully elsewhere in this file for ON_INIT_STATE) - this is specifically a rendering/decoration blind spot, not a general `component.root` problem. **The node codegen actually renders as the component's visible outer widget is its first REAL child** (`.byPath('ComponentName.children[0]')` in this case) - identify it by cross-checking `inspect --tree`'s `isDummyRoot` flag (skip anything flagged true) and matching an already-known rendered property (e.g. an existing background color already visible in `generated_code/`) against the candidate node's own props before trusting it as the restyle target.
- **`ShowDialog.component(...)`'s underlying `FFCustomDialogAction` positioning fields (`isGlobal`, `followerAnchor`, `targetAnchor`, `avoidOverflow`) are CONFIRMED NOT respected by codegen in this SDK build - no proto-level fix exists, don't re-attempt one.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.96): this dialog action always codegens as `showAlignedDialog(...)` (the `aligned_dialog` pub package, positions relative to the TAPPED WIDGET by default) - `isGlobal: true` is the package's own documented way to switch to screen-relative centering, and a raw-proto patch setting it (plus `followerAnchor`/`avoidOverflow`) was verified via `inspect --tree` to genuinely persist server-side across 2 separate pushes, yet `generated_code/` kept emitting the unset defaults regardless, even after a forced `codegen refresh`. Searched the full `FFCustomDialogAction`/`FFAlertDialogAction`/`FFWidgetClass` schema for an alternative field and found none - this is a real, isolated codegen gap, not a missed field or a caching issue. **The working fix for "this dialog needs to always be centered/positioned differently regardless of what triggered it": add a custom ACTION with `includeContext: true` (FlutterFlow auto-supplies `BuildContext`) that calls Flutter's own `showDialog(...)` directly, wrapped in an explicit `Center(...)` (or whatever positioning is actually needed) - `showDialog`/`DialogRoute` does NOT auto-center on its own (only `Dialog`/`AlertDialog` do, via their own internal `Center`), so the explicit wrap is load-bearing.** The action's code can `import '/components/<name>_widget.dart';` directly (FlutterFlow's own project-relative import convention, resolves against the current package's `lib/` root once the code is actually pushed) and instantiate the SAME component with the SAME params the old `ShowDialog.component(...)` call passed - the swap is purely at the trigger-action level (`ShowDialog.component(...)` → `CallCustomAction.named('yourAction', ...)`), no changes needed inside the dialog component itself. Validate the action's code against real generated types via `.ffai_staging/` BEFORE pushing (use `package:<projectname>/...` imports there, not the `/`-prefixed form, since the staging package is separate from the real project) - the SDK's own shape validators don't catch type errors against generated types like `CocomiseEditDialogCompWidget`.
- **`page.ensureActions(...)`/`component.ensureActions(...)` IS genuinely idempotent (unlike `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`) - confirmed by reading `_EnsureTriggerOp.apply` directly: it compares the target's CURRENT trigger chain against the requested one and only removes+replaces on an actual semantic difference, no-opping cleanly on an exact rerun.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.97). This makes it the right tool both for wiring a trigger the FIRST time AND for later REPLACING an existing trigger's action chain wholesale (e.g. swapping `ShowDialog.component(...)` for a different action) - just call it again with the new `actions:` list, no need to comment anything out afterward, unlike the one-shot structural ops. Don't confuse this with `ensureRemoved(findByKey(...))`, which looks similar but is NOT safe to leave live (see the entry above) - the difference is that `ensureActions`' OWN target lookup doesn't throw when unrelated things change, while `ensureRemoved`'s `findByKey` selector throws eagerly once its specific target is gone.
- **`Actions.conditional(...)`/`Actions.conditionalMulti(...)` (from `lib/src/ui/actions.dart`) operate on raw `FFVariable`/`FFActionNode` and do NOT fit inside a `component.ensureActions(..., actions: [...])`/`page.ensureActions(...)` call, which expects `List<DslAction>`.** The typed, `DslAction`-based conditional for THAT context is `If(condition, {then: [...], orElse: [...]})` (from `dsl/actions.dart`) - already used successfully elsewhere in this file (e.g. the bulk-select checkbox's `If(WidgetValue(), then: [...], orElse: [...])`). Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.97) while gating `DismissDialog()` behind a save/delete action's success: `If(ActionOutput('someBoolOutput'), then: [DismissDialog()], orElse: [Snackbar('...')])` compiles and behaves correctly directly inside an `actions:` list; `Actions.conditional` would need constructing at the raw-proto layer instead (as `_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog`/`_fixPrefecturesSaveButton` elsewhere in this file do, when directly mutating an already-compiled `FFActionNode` tree). **Pick `If(...)` when authoring inside a typed `actions: [...]` list; pick `Actions.conditional(...)` only when hand-building/patching a raw `FFActionNode` tree.**
- **A custom action that returns a plain `bool` (not raw `json`) sidesteps the "FieldAccess requires a struct/document target" restriction entirely for a save/delete-success check** - `If(ActionOutput('boolOutputName'), then:, orElse:)` reads a bool `ActionOutput` directly with no `jsonPathOperation` narrowing needed, unlike checking one field of a raw-`json`-typed action output (which hits that FieldAccess restriction, documented extensively elsewhere in this file). Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.97): when a native/DSL-authored Save or Delete button's action chain unconditionally dismisses the dialog regardless of whether the backend call actually succeeded (a real, easy-to-miss bug class - the backend can reject the request, e.g. a required-field validation error, while the client silently proceeds as if it worked), the cleanest fix is a wrapper custom action that calls the existing backend action, checks `result['success']`, and returns `true`/`false` - not an attempt to jsonPath-narrow the existing raw-json output in place.
- **This backend's `functions/src/index.ts` re-exports every Cloud Function through an explicit named list (`export { adminGetUsers, ..., adminUpdateSystemConfig, ... } from "./admin";`) - a brand-new function written in `admin.ts` is NOT deployable until it's also added to this list, and `firebase deploy --only functions:<name>` fails SILENTLY (reports "Deploy complete!", exit 0) when the name isn't in it, rather than erroring.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.100): a first deploy attempt for a genuinely new function (`adminGetSystemConfig`) reported success but the function never appeared in `gcloud functions list` - the deploy log's own tell was that it never printed a "creating/updating function `<name>`..." line at all, just generic housekeeping (API enablement, packaging) with nothing function-specific. **After writing any brand-new Cloud Function: (1) grep `index.ts`'s export block for its name before deploying, adding it if missing; (2) after deploying, verify the deploy log actually contains a "creating/updating ... function `<name>`" line, and/or confirm via `gcloud functions describe <name> --gen2` (or `gcloud functions list`) - a clean exit code and "Deploy complete!" alone do not prove the function was touched at all.**
- **When a page's `initState()` runs a SINGLE sequential chain of `getJsonField(...)` assignments into NON-nullable model fields with no per-field try/catch, one missing/failed upstream field silently aborts every assignment after it in that same chain, not just the one that threw** - confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.100, `SystemSettingsListPage`): the FIRST `getJsonField(...)` call that hit a missing field (because the whole action-output object was an error fallback, not the expected shape) threw assigning `null` into a non-nullable `bool`, and every subsequent assignment in that same `addPostFrameCallback` body silently never ran (Dart's normal exception-propagation-out-of-a-function behavior, not anything native-FlutterFlow-specific) - leaving OTHER, unrelated page-state fields (some nullable, bound to widgets like `CheckboxListTile(tristate: false)` that assert on null) permanently null and crashing LATER, at build time, producing a second, seemingly-unrelated error. **When a client reports a crash on a page with many sequential JSON-path field assignments in one init callback, don't assume the exact line the stack trace names is the only broken thing - check whether EVERY expected field in the action's output is actually guaranteed present on every return path (success and failure) of the action supplying that data, since one gap early in the sequence can cascade into unrelated-looking downstream crashes.**
- **`app.customAction(...)` (and by extension `app.customFunction`/`app.customWidget`/`app.pubDependency`) left UNCHANGED across many reruns of this script against an already-existing remote entity is empirically confirmed safe - it behaves as a no-op, not a `CustomCodeDuplicateError`, ONLY as long as the payload stays byte-identical to whatever is CURRENTLY live remotely.** Confirmed 2026-08-06 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.101), then CORRECTED the same day (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.102) once the payload actually diverged: `adminGetAuditLogs`'s original `app.customAction(...)` declaration was left untouched across many pushes with zero duplicate-name errors - but the very next push after an `updateCustomAction(...)` call ELSEWHERE in the SAME file changed what was actually live for that same entity, the untouched `app.customAction(...)` declaration (now stale relative to remote) broke immediately with `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action ... with a different payload`. **Re-syncing the stale declaration's `code:` string by hand did NOT fix it either** - a visually-identical retype (confirmed against `flutterflow ai inspect --custom-action <name>`'s own output) still failed the same way; the exact-match bar is stricter than a manual diff can reliably confirm. **The only robust fix: once ANY `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` call is added for an entity, comment out its ORIGINAL `app.customAction`/`app.customFunction` declaration in that SAME push - don't leave it live "because it still matches," because the update call it now needs to coexist with guarantees it eventually won't.** Nothing else in the file needs to change - call sites reference custom actions/functions by plain string name (`CallCustomAction.named(...)`) or a captured `FFIdentifier` handle (for functions used in bindings), never by re-resolving the original declaration, so commenting it out is safe and sufficient; all future edits go through `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` alone (the established, proven-reliable pattern - see `adminUpdateAllPrefectures`'s own precedent elsewhere in this file).
- **`page.ensureInsertedAfter(...)`/`ensureInsertedBefore(...)` requires the passed widget's OWN ROOT node to carry a non-empty `name:` - throws `"ensureInsertedAfter requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name."` otherwise, and this bites even when a deeper descendant already has a name.** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.103, ProcessedEventsListPage's nav-tile insert): `Container` has no `expanded:`-style prop in this DSL (unlike the FF builder UI's own per-widget "expand" toggle) - to match a sibling tile that needs to fill available Row space, the `Container` had to be wrapped in the separate `Expanded(child)` wrapper widget, which shifted the effective "root" from the caller's perspective. The `name:` has to move to whichever widget is now outermost - `Expanded(name: 'MyTile', Container(...))`, not `Expanded(Container(name: 'MyTile', ...))`. **Whenever wrapping an about-to-be-inserted widget in `Expanded`/`Flexible`/another wrapper, double check the `name:` sits on that OUTERMOST wrapper, not on the child that used to be the root before the wrap.**
- **`Text(...)` in this DSL has no `fontSize:`/`fontWeight:` override params - only `style: Styles.<token>` (`headlineMedium/Small`, `titleLarge/Medium/Small`, `bodyLarge/Medium/Small`, `labelMedium/Small`).** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.103): attempting to match a native tile's literal "fontSize 14, fontWeight w600" styling with `Text(..., style: Styles.bodyMedium, fontSize: 14, fontWeight: FontWeight.w600)` doesn't compile (`Text`'s constructor only exposes `style`/`color`/`maxLines`/`textAlign`/`overflow`). Pick the closest matching named theme-style token instead (`Styles.titleSmall` was the closer match for a small-but-bold label here) rather than trying to express a literal size/weight override - this DSL doesn't expose per-widget font overrides at all, styling goes entirely through the theme's named style scale.
- **A raw-patch `app.raw(...)` call NESTED INSIDE an `app.editComponent(...)`/`app.editPage(...)` callback runs BEFORE that callback's own `ensureReplaced`/other queued mutations are actually applied - even though it appears textually AFTER them, inside the same callback body.** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.106), by reading the SDK source directly (`edit.dart`'s `editComponent`/`editPage`): the callback passed to `editComponent`/`editPage` only QUEUES operations onto an internal `EditWidgetEditor` (the SDK's own doc comment: "the closure only records intent... selectors resolve later... during compilation") - the actual mutation is deferred to ONE `raw((project) => _applyEditWidgetClassEdit(project, editor))` call that `editComponent`/`editPage` itself registers AFTER the callback returns. A NESTED `app.raw(...)` call (e.g. a name-based color patch meant to run right after a `component.ensureReplaced(...)` a few lines above it in the same callback) registers ITS OWN operation immediately, at the point it's called during that callback's execution - which is BEFORE `editComponent`'s own post-callback registration, so it ends up earlier in the overall operation queue and finds nothing yet. Hit as `Bad state: Toggle "hokkaidoActiveToggle" not found on "Prefectures_DialogComp"` - the replacement Container genuinely didn't exist yet when the name lookup ran. **Fix: any raw patch that depends on an `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/etc. result from the SAME `editComponent`/`editPage` call must be a separate, subsequent TOP-LEVEL `app.raw(...)` call, positioned textually AFTER the entire `editComponent(...)`/`editPage(...)` call closes - never nested inside its callback.** This generalizes `_patchStatusDotByName`'s own doc comment (already documented for `editPage` specifically) to `editComponent` too - same underlying mechanism, first confirmed there this session.
- **To ADD a step onto a SHARED trigger (one widget's ON_INIT_STATE/etc. driving several unrelated tabs/sections at once) without risking the other sections' own logic, use `compileDslActionSequenceForExistingWidgetClass(project, widgetClassName:, targetNodeKey:, triggerType:, actions:, app: app)` to compile a standalone `FFActionNode` and manually SPLICE it in - never `page.ensureActions(target, triggerType:, actions: [...])` for this case.** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.108): `ensureActions` REPLACES a target's entire trigger chain wholesale (confirmed by reading `_EnsureTriggerOp.apply` - it diffs and replaces, never appends) - fine for a trigger that belongs to ONE isolated widget, but dangerous for a page-ROOT `ON_INIT_STATE` shared by multiple tabs/sections, where reconstructing the FULL existing chain by hand (to preserve every other section's own load logic) risks a mistake silently breaking something unrelated. `compileDslActionSequenceForExistingWidgetClass` is an SDK-exposed helper (confirmed by reading its implementation) that resolves the SAME trigger-compile environment `ensureActions` uses internally and calls the SAME `compileActionSequence`, but just RETURNS the compiled node instead of attaching/replacing anything - lets you author the NEW steps with the full typed DSL (`CallCustomAction.named(...)`, `SetState(...)`, etc., including `ActionOutput(...)` cross-references between the new steps) with zero raw-proto hand-construction. Splice pattern: find the target's existing trigger, `deepCopy()` its current `rootAction`, walk the NEWLY COMPILED chain to its own last node (`while (node.hasFollowUpAction()) node = node.followUpAction;`), attach the deep-copied old root there as that last node's `followUpAction`, then set `trigger.rootAction` to the new chain's head - every existing step still runs, byte-for-byte unchanged, just after the new step instead of before. Same splice TECHNIQUE already proven earlier in this file for a single row's own isolated `ON_TAP` (`ReservationListPage`'s `selectedReservation` splice) - `compileDslActionSequenceForExistingWidgetClass` is what makes it safe to reach for on a SHARED page-root trigger too, since it removes the need to reconstruct anything by hand. **One-shot, exactly like `ensureReplaced`/`app.state`/etc. - comment out immediately once confirmed landed** (forgetting this once produced `Name "..." already in use` / `Action ... has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget` on the very next push - a second splice tried to stack another copy of the same step onto the already-modified chain).
- **`ShowDialog.component(...)` only accepts the DSL's own `ComponentHandle` (issued by `app.component(...)`) - a native/pre-existing component's typed-SDK handle (`ff.Components.xxx`, type `XxxComponentHandle` from the `flutterflow_ai` barrel) is a DIFFERENT, incompatible type and fails at `dart analyze` time, not at push time.** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.108): `ShowDialog.component(ff.Components.prefecturesDialogComp)` (`Prefectures_DialogComp` is native, never declared via `app.component(...)` in this DSL) failed with `argument type 'PrefecturesDialogCompComponentHandle' can't be assigned to parameter type 'ComponentHandle?'` - caught locally by `dart analyze` before ever reaching a push, cheap to catch but easy to not think to check first. Distinct from the ALREADY-documented `ShowDialog.component` positioning bug (`isGlobal`/`avoidOverflow`/`followerAnchor` not respected by codegen, §18.96) - that one affects components THAT DO have a real `ComponentHandle` (e.g. ones declared via `app.component(...)` in this DSL) and is a codegen/runtime issue; this one is a compile-time type mismatch that blocks native/pre-existing components specifically, before the positioning question is even reachable. **Same fix as §18.96 either way: bypass `ShowDialog.component` entirely with a plain `includeContext: true` custom action that calls Flutter's own `showDialog(...)` directly** - for a native component, read its ORIGINAL native trigger's generated code first (not guessed) to replicate the exact same dialog shell (`Dialog`/`GestureDetector`/sizing), then import the component widget directly (`import '/<path>/<name>_widget.dart';`) and instantiate it inside the custom action's own `showDialog` builder.
- **Before wholesale-replacing a container via `ensureReplaced`, walk its FULL descendant tree (via `flutterflow ai inspect`/the typed SDK file) for any OTHER functionally-important node nested inside it - a sibling-looking button/control can be a genuine descendant, not a sibling, and gets silently deleted along with the container with no compile-time warning.** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.108): replacing `Container_1251djq5` (a list panel) was planned as "safe - the 提供エリア追加 button is a separate sibling node, not touched by this replace," based on an incomplete read of the tree; it was actually nested three levels inside that same container (`Column_sbw5lptl.children[1]`), and got deleted the moment the container was replaced. Removing a widget is never a `dart analyze`/`compileDslApp` error - the only way to catch this is to actually verify `generated_code/` for the specific feature after the push, per this project's own standing "verify in generated_code/, don't assume a clean push means done" discipline - grep for the removed feature's own visible text/label, not just "did the push succeed."
- **`Chart` widgets (`FFChart`/`FFPieChart`/etc.) have NO typed DSL patch surface at all - confirmed via `flutterflow ai docs ui` having zero "chart" entries - so any style property on an existing chart (colors, radius, donut hole, etc.) must go through the raw `mutateNode` escape hatch, with the exact proto shape read off the LIVE node via `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --selector-key <key>` WITHOUT `--dsl-json` (that flag only returns key/type/path for Chart nodes, not the real prop tree).** Confirmed 2026-08-07 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.112, dashboard user-type pie chart): the plain (non-`--dsl-json`) inspect output showed the full `props.chart.pieChart.listPieChartColors` tree; cross-checked against the generated proto source (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/schema/gen/flutterflow.pb.dart`) to get the exact Dart types (`node.props.chart.pieChart.listPieChartColors` is `repeated FFValue`, each entry `FFValue(inputValue: FFParameterValue(color: FFColor(value: <Int64 ARGB>)))` - a 3-level wrap, not the simpler 1-level `FFColorValue(inputValue: FFColor(...))` shape used for ordinary widget `colorValue` properties like `Divider`/`Icon`). **`FFPieChart` also has a deprecated field trap specific to charts**: `legacyListPieChartColors` (field 6, `repeated FFColor`, `[deprecated = true]`) sits right next to the modern `listPieChartColors` (field 8, `repeated FFValue`) - same off-limits-deprecated-field discipline CLAUDE.md documents for `FFConditionActions`, just not obvious until you actually read `FFPieChart`'s own message definition. `Int64` (required by `FFColor.value`) isn't re-exported by `flutterflow_ai.dart`'s barrel - import `package:fixnum/fixnum.dart` directly (already resolves, since it's a transitive dependency of `protobuf` present in `pubspec.lock`).
- **`"generatorVariable": {}` (or any proto submessage) appearing in a raw `inspect` JSON dump does NOT mean that field is populated - EVERY `FFNode` carries this field, empty by default, whether or not that node is an actual "Generate Dynamic Children" source.** Confirmed the expensive way, 2026-08-07/08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.115): a page's real list generator (`Column_tbwaswzc`, confirmed via directly inspecting IT and finding a genuinely populated `generatorVariable.identifier`/`variable`) was misidentified as a DIFFERENT, deeply-nested Column several levels lower in the tree, because THAT wrong Column's own children happened to show `"generatorVariable": {}` in their own operations dump - an empty placeholder, not evidence of anything. The wrong node was actually nested INSIDE a single list item's own row template. A widget inserted there compiled and pushed clean, and the typed SDK showed it existing - but it never rendered at all (its immediate parent was a single-child `Padding(child: Builder(...))` with nowhere to put a second child, silently dropped by codegen with no error anywhere) and, had it rendered, would have repeated once per list item instead of once for the page. **Before trusting any node as "the generator," check that node's OWN `generatorVariable` field specifically has a non-empty `identifier`/`variable` - never infer it from a CHILD having something with the same field name.** Same standing lesson as the rest of this file's "verify in `generated_code/`, don't just trust a clean push" discipline - a clean push is not proof a structural insert landed somewhere that actually renders.
- **`ActionOutput('name')` (the typed DSL expression) only resolves inside the SAME action/trigger chain that alias belongs to - it cannot be used as a free-standing argument to a widget PROPERTY (e.g. `visible:`) elsewhere on the page, even though nothing in its type signature suggests that restriction.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.115): using `ActionOutput('bannersResult')` (a real, already-existing native trigger's output name) as an argument to `CustomFunction(...)` inside a Text widget's `visible:` failed at LOCAL `compileDslApp` - not even a push attempt - with `Bad state: Action output alias "bannersResult" is not available in this action chain.` Reading `compiler.dart`'s own `ActionOutput()` case confirmed why: it looks up `env.actionOutputs[alias]`, a map populated only while compiling THAT SPECIFIC trigger's own action sequence - a fallback to `env.pageNodeKey` elsewhere in the same case (for `nodeKeyRef`) makes it look like page-wide resolution might be supported, but the alias lookup itself is not page-scoped. To reference an already-completed action's own output from an ARBITRARY widget property elsewhere on the page (not inside the producing trigger), the raw-proto `FFActionOutputVariable(actionKeyRef:, outputVariableIdentifier:)` shape is required instead (already proven safe for this exact use elsewhere in this file, e.g. dialog-param bindings) - the typed `ActionOutput(...)` expression is not a substitute.
- **`listHasValueOperation` (the proto operation for "is this list non-empty," `FFVariableOperation`) requires its INPUT to be a value with a STATIC "this is a List" type - a value reached via `jsonPathOperation` off an untyped `json`-returning custom action does not qualify, even though it genuinely holds a list at runtime.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.115): worked perfectly when applied to a field on an App-State list (`List<JSON>` or `List<DataStruct<X>>`, both fine) or a struct-typed action's own declared list sub-field (`ReservationExtrasResult.extensions`/`.rewards`) - but failed server-side validation (`Operation requires a list parameter`) the moment the base variable's type came from narrowing a raw-`json`-returning action's output via `jsonPathOperation` instead. If a custom action was declared with a proper `returns:` struct (list-typed fields included), this operation works fine on those fields; if it returns bare `json`, this operation cannot be used on anything reached by narrowing it, and there is no per-DSL workaround found - either type the action's return properly, or compute the check in a custom Dart function that accepts the field as `dynamic`/`json` (no static list-type requirement on function parameters).
- **`WIDGET_CLASS_STATE`-sourced `LOCAL_STATE` variables (page/component-instance-scoped state, as opposed to `APP_STATE` which is a single global instance) need an explicit `nodeKeyRef` pointing at that widget class's own root node - `APP_STATE`-sourced ones do not.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.115): a raw-constructed `FFVariable(source: LOCAL_STATE, baseVariable: FFBaseVariable(localState: FFLocalStateVariable(fieldIdentifier:, stateVariableType: WIDGET_CLASS_STATE)))` with no `nodeKeyRef` failed server-side validation (`Widget class state field not found`) on every use, while the byte-for-byte same construction with `stateVariableType: APP_STATE` (and still no `nodeKeyRef`) worked fine - confirmed by cross-checking an already-live equivalent binding (a ListView's own `generatorVariable.variable` for a `WIDGET_CLASS_STATE` field) via `flutterflow ai inspect`, which showed a `nodeKeyRef: {"key": "Scaffold_..."}` the broken construction was missing. Makes semantic sense in hindsight (APP_STATE has no ambiguity about which "instance" to resolve; WIDGET_CLASS_STATE does) but is easy to miss since APP_STATE's own working precedent has no `nodeKeyRef` at all, inviting the assumption that LOCAL_STATE variables never need one.
- **A shared NATIVE custom action's per-argument opaque KEYS cannot be reliably reconstructed from any `inspect` output - `--custom-action <name>` only lists parameter NAMES (no keys at all), and a live call site's raw `argumentValues.arguments` map shows keys only paired with VALUES (no name), with JSON key order NOT reliably matching the Dart function's declared parameter order (confirmed empirically: a value known to belong to the 9th declared parameter appeared in the 4th JSON position).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.116, `adminGetUsers`): this makes the otherwise-proven "extend a shared custom action's parameter list, reusing each existing parameter's real key" pattern (documented elsewhere in this file, e.g. `adminGetReservations`'s `guestId` addition) UNSAFE to attempt on a wholly NATIVE action from scratch - that pattern is only safe when every existing key is already known because THIS workspace originally declared the action via the DSL. **When a shared native action's behavior needs to change but its argument list can't be safely extended, prefer changing its CODE ONLY (no signature/argument-list touch at all) and, if the new behavior must not reach every existing caller, defensively counter-act it in the specific unwanted caller(s) instead of trying to make the change opt-in via a new parameter.** Concretely: rather than adding an unsafe `sortOnlineFirst` parameter to `adminGetUsers` (used by 6+ call sites), the sort was made unconditional inside `adminGetUsers` itself (code-only `updateCustomAction`, zero risk), and the ONE caller whose behavior had to stay unchanged (`adminGetKycUsersList`, a review queue unrelated to the request) got its own small counter-sort undoing the reorder for just that caller - both changes fully within actions this workspace already controls, no reverse-engineered keys anywhere.
- **When a shared custom action calls ANOTHER custom action internally (an "action chain," e.g. `adminGetUserListItems`/`adminGetKycUsersList` both calling `adminGetUsers`), a behavior change to the INNER action's return value/order silently propagates to every caller of the OUTER action too - before changing a widely-reused action's output, grep `generated_code/lib/custom_code/actions/*.dart` for every OTHER action that calls it internally, not just the page-level widgets that call it directly.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.116): changing `adminGetUsers`'s own returned order affects not just its 2 direct page callers but also `adminGetUserListItems` (2 more pages) and `adminGetKycUsersList` (a page outside the requested scope) transitively - the full caller graph is only visible by grepping the actual Dart call sites, not by reasoning about which PAGES visibly use a given action.
- **`EditWidgetPatch`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensureInsertedAfter` selectors resolve via a STATIC INDEX-based path (`....children[N]...`) captured once at DSL-authoring time - NOT a key-anchored path - so a structural insert/remove earlier in the SAME `editComponent`/`editPage` call silently shifts the live index of every LATER sibling (and any descendant of a later sibling), stranding that sibling's static selector onto whatever else now occupies its old numeric slot, with no error at the point of staleness.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.117), by reading `WidgetRef._fromNode`/`_applyEditWidgetClassEdit` directly: paths are built as `'$path.children[$genericIndex]'` while walking `node.children` in order, and EVERY operation in an `editComponent`/`editPage` call re-resolves its (captured-once, pre-edit) static path against a FRESHLY-rebuilt live snapshot immediately before it runs - so an earlier insert's index shift is real and persists for the rest of that call. Hit as `Bad state: Typography patches are not supported for Icon.` - an unnamed-then-fixed icon insert shifted a sibling `Text` node from index 0 to 1, and the very next patch (still holding the stale index-0 path) landed on the icon instead. **Fix: within one `editComponent`/`editPage` call, put every pure property `update(...)` patch FIRST (none of them change structure, so their static paths never go stale), and put every structural insert/remove LAST, ordered by DESCENDING original tree position** (highest original index first) - an insert only ever shifts siblings AFTER its own position, so processing latest-position anchors first keeps every insert's own anchor valid when it runs.
- **A component's actual RENDERED size is not necessarily governed by the widget class's own ROOT node - walk one level into the tree and confirm which node's `dimensions` the outermost VISIBLE `Container(width:, height:)` in `generated_code/` actually matches before trusting a root-level size patch.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.117, `BannerFormDialogComp`): the root (`Container_mdgc6xkb`) is a thin outer wrapper; the real sized container is its own single child (`Container_y0leh3or`), which carries its own independent `dimensions`. A `patch.size(...)` on the root alone compiled clean, pushed clean, and was even confirmed present in the live proto via `inspect` - but `generated_code/` kept rendering the OLD size, since codegen reads the CHILD's dimensions for the visible box, not the root's. Patching only the root while leaving the child at its old size would additionally have left an invisible empty band past the end of the content, since the wrapper carries its own background decoration. **Patch every node in that wrapper chain together, not just the outermost one** - and re-verify the actual rendered `width:`/`height:` in `generated_code/` after the push, don't trust a clean `inspect` read of the root alone.
- **For a BRAND NEW custom function bound into a FRESHLY-authored widget tree (via `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/etc.), skip the raw `addCustomFunction` + follow-up-raw-patch dance entirely - the fully-typed `app.customFunction(name, args:, returns:, code:)` + `CustomFunction(handle, args: {...})` path works directly, with the invocation usable as an ordinary widget content argument (e.g. `Text(CustomFunction(fn, args: {...}), ...)`) and zero raw proto.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.118): every custom-function binding in this file before this point went through `addCustomFunction`(raw)/`updateCustomFunction` + a SEPARATE `app.raw` patch onto an already-existing native node - necessary THERE only because those functions had to bind onto pre-existing FlutterFlow-built nodes across a later push (needing a hardcoded key to survive reruns, per this file's own top-of-file workaround comment), not because typed function invocation itself requires raw patching. When the target node is being freshly constructed in the SAME push (via `ensureReplaced` or similar), `app.customFunction(...)`'s returned `CustomFunctionHandle` plugs straight into `CustomFunction(handle, args: {'x': someExpression})` inline at construction time, compiling to the correct `FFVariable(source: FUNCTION_CALL, ...)` shape with no follow-up patch step at all - confirmed against `references/action_block_showcase_dsl.dart`'s own `formatFavorites`/`CustomFunction(...)` pair before writing any code. Also confirmed `listOf(structHandle)` (already proven as a custom ACTION `returns:` type in this file) works identically as a custom FUNCTION `args:` type - the generated Dart signature still comes out nullable regardless of the declared type (`List<XStruct>? x`), consistent with this project's already-documented "custom-function parameters compile as nullable" rule, so defensively write `x ?? const <XStruct>[]` before iterating.
