- **The DSL's `Wrap` widget only accepts a static `children:` list - no `source:`/`itemBuilder:` generator support (unlike `ListView`/`GridView`, confirmed by reading `widgets.dart` directly).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.118): considered for a 47-item service-area summary (only showing the ~handful of ACTIVE prefectures as tightly-packed pills) but rejected on this basis. **CORRECTION, same day (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.119):** the reasoning that immediately followed this in the original version of this entry - "there is no client-side filter step between a generator's `source:` and a real state field" - was WRONG and has since been disproven in the same file: `ListView`/`GridView`'s `source:` parameter is `normalizeExpression(source)`, i.e. an ordinary `DslExpression`, and accepts a `CustomFunction(fn, args: {...})` invocation directly, exactly like `Text`'s content parameter does (see the entry above) - a custom function CAN filter/reorder/transform a list before a `ListView`/`GridView` ever sees it, live, re-evaluated every rebuild, with zero extra state fields. Proven working in §18.119's `canonicalServiceAreas`/`extraServiceAreas` split (`ListView(source: CustomFunction(canonicalServiceAreasFn, args: {'areas': State('serviceAreaListStruct')}), ...)`). **The `Wrap`-has-no-generator limitation is real and still stands** - a tightly-packed, variable-width chip flow genuinely isn't buildable this way - but if a `ListView`/`GridView`-shaped result is acceptable, filtering via a custom function bound directly to `source:` is the correct, proven tool; don't reach for a separate synced state field (extra surface, extra drift risk) when a `CustomFunction` expression will do.
- **A shared row-template BUILDER FUNCTION (e.g. `DslWidget _xRow(ItemRef item) {...}`) reused as the `itemBuilder` for TWO OR MORE independent `ListView`/`GridView` instances on the same page compiles a SEPARATE, INDEPENDENT copy of that template's subtree per list - any hardcoded name/`outputAs` string inside it collides across copies.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.119, `_serviceAreaRow` reused for a "canonical 11" list and a new "追加エリア extras" list on the same page): two concrete failure modes from the SAME root cause. (1) A hardcoded action `outputAs: 'serviceAreaToggleResult'` inside the row's `onTap` chain made BOTH lists' row templates declare an action output with the identical name - rejected outright by `compileDslApp`'s validation (`Action in Container has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget`), caught on the first push attempt. (2) A hardcoded `statusName` passed to `_statusValue(...)` (used for a later `_patchStatusDotByName(..., statusName:, ...)` raw color patch) would have left the SECOND list's status dots permanently stuck on their placeholder color - `_patchStatusDotByName`'s underlying `_findNodeByName` is a first-match helper with no duplicate-name detection, so it silently patches only the FIRST of the two same-named nodes it finds, no error anywhere. **Fix for both: thread a per-call-site-unique identifier (e.g. a `statusName`/`sectionId` parameter already required for one of the two reasons) through the shared builder, and derive EVERY name that must be unique per instance - `outputAs`, `name:`, anything a later raw patch searches for by string - from that same identifier, rather than hardcoding any of them as literals inside the shared function.**
- **`app.struct(...)`/`app.customAction(...)`/`app.customFunction(...)` are `ensure*`-style (create-if-missing) calls, not upsert - editing an EXISTING entity's payload directly in its own `app.struct/customAction(...)` call fails outright once that entity is already live, even though the exact same call shape works fine the first time.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.120): extending `WorkPostItem`'s field list directly in its `app.struct(...)` call, and editing `adminGetWorkPosts`'s `code:` body directly in its `app.customAction(...)` call, both failed identically - `Invalid argument(s): ensure[DataStruct/CustomAction] found an existing [data struct/custom action] named "X" with a different payload. \`ensure*\` helpers are create-if-missing only.` For a custom action/function, the fix is already documented elsewhere in this file: `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` via `app.raw(...)`, original declaration commented out. For a DATA STRUCT specifically, the parallel `updateDataStruct`/`updateDataStructField`/`addDataStructField` helpers exist in the SDK source (`data_schema_helpers.dart`) but are **NOT exported from the public `flutterflow_ai.dart` barrel** - confirmed the hard way: local `dart analyze` resolved them fine (a broader path-dependency than the real published package), but `flutterflow ai run`'s own compile - the one that actually matters, since it's what gets pushed - failed with `Method not found` for all of them. **To add a field to an EXISTING data struct: keep the `app.struct(...)` call's payload byte-for-byte identical to the current remote shape (so `ensureDataStruct` still matches), and add the new field(s) separately via a raw proto walk** - `project.backend.dataSchemaConfig.dataStructs` (a plain `PbList<FFDataStruct>`, confirmed exported) to find the struct by `identifier.name`, then `.fields.add(FFParameter(identifier: FFIdentifier(name:, key:), dataType: FFDataTypeV2(...), description:))` directly - all of `FFParameter`/`FFIdentifier`/`FFDataTypeV2`/`FFSubType` ARE exported (proto message types, not helper functions). Self-guard with an "already has this field" check (`fields.any((f) => f.identifier.name == '...')`) so the block is safe to leave live, since there's no `ensure`-style variant available. **For a LIST-of-struct field specifically**: unlike custom function/action arguments (which wrap list types via the DSL's own `listOf(DslType)`), a data-struct field's "is this a list" is the separate `FFParameter.isList` bool - `dataType` stays the bare ELEMENT type (`FFDataTypeV2(scalarType: FFBaseDataType.DataStruct, subType: FFSubType(dataStructIdentifier: <other struct's identifier>))`), never a `listType`-wrapped one.
- **`Actions.conditional(...)`/`Actions.chain(...)` - CLAUDE.md's own general-purpose documented pattern for building typed conditional action chains - are NOT exported from this SDK build's public `flutterflow_ai.dart` barrel.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.120): `dart analyze` reported `Undefined name 'Actions'` outright on first use. The real, exported equivalent is **`If(condition, {then: [...], orElse: [...]})`** (`actions.dart`) - same capability (build a real `FFConditionActions`-shaped conditional, not the deprecated-field trap), different name, and `then`/`orElse` take a plain `List<DslAction>` directly with no `Actions.chain([...])` wrapper needed at all.
- **`ActionOutput(name)['field']` field access only works when that action's `returns:` type is a genuine struct or document - it does NOT work on `returnType: json` (a generic/opaque scalar in this DSL's type system), even though indexing into a NAMED STRUCT's fields this exact way is the normal, documented pattern elsewhere.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.120): `ActionOutput('createWorkPostResult')['success']`/`['error']`, on an action declared `returns: json`, failed `compileDslApp` with `Unsupported operation: Field access requires a struct or document target, got ScalarType(DslScalarKind.json, default: null)`. **When a `json`-returning action's result needs to be inspected (success/error branching, reading a specific field), either give the action a properly-typed struct `returns:` instead of bare `json` so field access works, or - the lower-risk, zero-new-surface option - skip inspecting it entirely and treat the result as opaque**, matching how every `json`-returning action-completion handler already in this file does it (a straight, unconditional refetch-and-continue with no success/failure branching at all).
- **After deploying a BRAND NEW Cloud Function (not updating an existing one), verify it actually exists via `firebase functions:list --project <id>` - a clean `firebase deploy` exit code and a per-function "Successful update operation" line only prove what deploy's OWN discovery step found, not that every function you intended to deploy was actually in that discovered set.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.120): this backend's `index.ts` exports functions via an EXPLICIT named list from `./admin` (`export { adminGetUsers, ..., adminGetWorkPosts, adminCloseWorkPost } from "./admin";`), NOT a wildcard `export *`. A deploy targeting 2 brand-new functions plus 1 existing one reported success with a "Successful update operation" line for the EXISTING one only - zero mention, not even an error, of the two new ones, because `firebase deploy`'s discovery step reads `index.ts`, and neither new function had been added to that list yet (both existed fine in `admin.ts` itself). Caught by not trusting the clean deploy log at face value and cross-checking `functions:list` afterward. **Whenever adding a brand new Cloud Function in this backend, add it to `index.ts`'s export list in the SAME change as writing the function itself** - it's easy to write the function, `tsc`-check clean (which also doesn't catch this, since `admin.ts` compiles fine on its own regardless of what `index.ts` re-exports), and deploy without ever registering it.
- **`_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog(confirmAction, ...)` only RESTRUCTURES an action node that already IS a `ConfirmDialog` - it does not insert one. Calling it on a plain (non-dialog) root action to "add" a confirm gate compiles clean client-side but is rejected server-side with `Conditional based on a confirm dialog must immediately follow the confirm dialog action.`** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.131, SystemSettingsListPage's 追加エリア row delete-confirm): when the action you want to gate has no ConfirmDialog anywhere in its live compiled chain yet, hand-build one first - `FFActionNode(key:, action: FFAction(alertDialog: FFAlertDialogAction(confirmDialog: FFConfirmDialogAction(title:, message:, confirmText:, dismissText:))), followUpAction: <the action to guard>)` (proven shape, copy from an existing raw-built confirm dialog in this file, e.g. the freeze/unfreeze or resolve-report confirms) - THEN pass that new node into `_gateActionOnConfirmationDialog(...)`, and finally point the trigger's own `rootAction` at it (`trigger.rootAction = confirmDialogNode`). Passing the action-to-be-guarded directly is the wrong call shape even though the error message doesn't say so explicitly.
- **Before trusting that a DSL block in `dsl/edit.dart` referencing a target widget/panel is "the" live construction site, confirm it isn't one of several ALREADY-superseded, commented-out `ensureReplaced` blocks for the same node - this file accumulates multiple historical attempts at the same panel across sessions, and only the LAST one that actually landed (before being commented out per this file's own one-shot discipline) reflects what's live on the server.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.131): `SystemSettingsListPage`'s service-area extras list had 4 separate historical `ExtraServiceAreaListView`-building blocks in the file (§18.107 through §18.128), every one of them commented out - a mid-session edit was made to the OLDEST of the four (adding `confirmBeforeToggle: true`) under the mistaken assumption it was live, which had zero effect since it never compiles. `dart analyze`'s own `unused_element`/`unused_local_variable` warnings are a fast, free tell: a shared row-builder function (or a `CustomFunction`/`CustomAction` handle) flagged as unused means every call site referencing it is dead code, so if you're about to edit "the" call site, first check it isn't the one warning points at as unreferenced. When in doubt, a raw `.writeToJson()` (or `inspect --tree`) dump of the LIVE node is the ground truth - dead DSL text is not.
- **`ColorToken` (the type every `EditWidgetPatch` color/border/shadow/gradient param takes - `color()`, `border({color:})`, `Shadow({color:})`, `GradientStop`) is a `sealed class` with two subclasses, `NamedColor` (theme tokens: `Colors.primary` etc.) and `HexColor` (`Colors.hex(...)`) - it is NOT restricted to named theme tokens, even though the parameter type name and every existing call site in this file up to 2026-08-08 only ever used named tokens.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.133), by reading `widgets.dart` directly rather than assuming from precedent: `Colors.hex(0xFF...)` patches cleanly through `page.update(...)`'s ordinary typed patch surface for borders, shadows, and gradients on an EXISTING widget - no need to reach for `mutateNode`/raw proto just because a custom (non-token) accent color is wanted on a property patch. Also confirmed while on the same task: `EditWidgetPatch` has real typed methods for `gradient(Gradient value)` (`Gradient.linear([GradientStop(color, position), ...], angle:)`, positional constructor args, not named) and `pieChartConfig({donutHoleRadius, sectionSpacing, startAngle, sectionLabel})` - **check the SDK source (`edit.dart`'s `EditWidgetPatch` class) for a typed method before assuming a raw `mutateNode` patch is required**, the way §18.112 genuinely needed it for `listPieChartColors` (no typed surface exists for that one specifically) - the two chart properties look similarly "exotic" but aren't actually raw-only.
- **To relocate an existing subtree to a genuinely different parent in the SAME push, prefer `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` (at the new position) + `ensureRemoved` (at the old position) over a raw-proto reparent move when the new widget is being freshly authored, not literally the same node.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.134, AdminDashboardPage's legend relocation): a raw move (the `stackNode.children.removeAt(...)` / `outerColumn.children.add(...)` technique from §18.130) only works when moving a node whose KEY YOU ALREADY KNOW - it can't be combined with authoring a brand-new replacement widget in the same operation, because `ensureReplaced`/fresh construction only gets its real key AFTER the compiler assigns one at push time, and nothing in the same script can reference a key that doesn't exist yet. Insert-at-new-position + remove-at-old-position sidesteps this entirely, since both operations target keys that are already known before the script runs, and reads as two simple, independent, individually-idempotent operations rather than one fragile move. Reach for the raw-proto move ONLY when relocating the literal same node with no content changes (§18.130's actual case - moving an existing Save button, unchanged, to a new parent).
- **When a widget needs to size to the FULL width of its actual container rather than the intrinsic width of whatever it happens to be nested beside, check what it's currently sharing a `Row`/`Column` with - a non-`Expanded`/`Flexible` child only ever gets as much space as its own content needs, not a share of its parent's available space.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.134): a legend meant to read as a full-width horizontal strip stayed visually cramped even after being restyled, because it was still nested inside the SAME header `Row` as an icon badge - swapping its internal layout direction alone would not have fixed the width, since the `Row` was still sizing that whole side to its own narrow intrinsic content. Relocating it to be a direct sibling further down the enclosing `Column` (no longer sharing a `Row` with anything else) is what actually unlocked the full card width - a layout-direction fix and a width-constraint fix are not the same problem, and it's worth checking which one (or both) a "make this wider/horizontal" report actually needs before touching only the more obvious one.
- **A `Wrap` whose children's combined natural width sits very close to its available width can visually overlap/mis-lay-out siblings in this app's real device/browser rendering, even when its own compiled `generated_code/` shows a completely unremarkable, correctly-configured `Wrap` (right `direction`, `spacing`, `runSpacing`, no stray `Stack`/`Positioned`, only one copy of the subtree in the file).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.135, AdminDashboardPage's legend row): 5 chips whose total natural width was near the borderline of the actual available card width rendered with the 3rd chip visually overlapping the 2nd, in a `Wrap` nested inside a no-explicit-width `Container` → `Column` → no-explicit-width `Container` (itself `Expanded`) ancestor chain - a static read of the generated Dart gave no indication anything was wrong. **Don't spend long chasing the exact Flutter layout mechanism when a `Wrap` is this close to its width limit - switch to a layout that CANNOT exhibit the failure regardless of cause**, e.g. explicit fixed `Row`s (grouped so each row's own content is comfortably under half the available width) instead of one `Wrap` relying on a borderline auto-reflow calculation. Treat "`Wrap` near its width limit" as a real risk factor to design around up front, not just a theoretical edge case - this project has now hit it in practice.
- **`EditWidgetPatch.size(...)` does NOT support every widget type - `_applySizePatch` only covers `Container`/`Button`/`Image`/`Icon`/`IconButton`; calling it on a `Chart` node throws `Bad state: Size patches are not supported for Chart.` even though the Chart node genuinely carries its own `dimensions` field (confirmed via a raw `inspect --tree` dump).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.136). Workaround: `page.mutateNode(chartSelection, (node) { node.props.chart.dimensions.width = FFDim(pixelsValue: FFDoubleValue(inputValue: w)); node.props.chart.dimensions.height = FFDim(pixelsValue: FFDoubleValue(inputValue: h)); })` - the exact `FFDim(pixelsValue: FFDoubleValue(inputValue: ...))` shape the SDK's OWN internal converters (`_converters.dart`/`ui.dart`) use for every other widget's size patch, read directly from the SDK source rather than guessed. `FFDim`/`FFDoubleValue` are exported proto message types, usable the same way `FFColor`/`FFValue`/`FFParameterValue` already are in this file. Bundle this into the SAME `page.mutateNode` call already touching that Chart node for other raw fields (colors, radius) rather than a separate top-level `app.raw(...)` - `mutateNode` queues alongside `page.update`/`page.ensureReplaced` in the same editor and avoids this file's own documented "a raw call can run before an `editPage` block's queued mutations" ordering hazard.
- **When a client reports "this doesn't match the size/style of its sibling," check the SIBLING'S actual current values too, not just the reported element - a size mismatch is exactly as likely to be `A` that changed as `B` that never got updated to match, and picking the wrong one to calibrate against produces a wrong fix.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.136): AdminDashboardPage's donut card had gone height-unbound in an EARLIER fix (§18.132's `clearHeight()`) while its sibling bar-chart card had never been touched and was still fixed at its original 250×350 - re-deriving the sibling's own real internal size BUDGET from its compiled `generated_code` (not just its outer declared dimensions) is what made it possible to shrink the reported card's content to fit the same box safely, rather than just copying a number and re-triggering the exact overflow bug that made it unbound in the first place.
- **A concrete reference image supersedes a prior verbal styling direction outright, even when they conflict - don't try to reconcile "make it vibrant/cute chip badges" (an earlier ask) with "match this reference's plain swatch-list legend" (a later ask, with an actual image) by blending both.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.137): the client's own reference image for AdminDashboardPage's legend used a plain swatch+neutral-text list, structurally the opposite of the colored-chip-badge design built across §18.134-136 in response to earlier "flashy and cute" requests. The newest, most concrete instruction wins wholesale - built a fresh helper (`_dashboardLegendItem`) rather than trying to retrofit color/contrast logic from the superseded `_dashboardLegendChip` helper into the new minimal style. When a task's own reference material scopes narrower than the literal image (e.g. the reference's full standalone page chrome vs. this app's own small embedded card), adopt the STYLE ELEMENTS the reference actually demonstrates (legend format, chart type, palette family) rather than replicating the reference's own container/page-level layout verbatim into a structurally different context.
- **A pie/donut chart's on-chart label TEXT STYLE (`sectionLabelStyle`, an `FFText` message - the same general text-style proto used for ordinary `Text` widgets) has no typed `pieChartConfig(...)` surface - that method only covers `donutHoleRadius`/`sectionSpacing`/`startAngle`/`sectionLabel` (the label TYPE: none/value/percent), not its typography.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.137). To resize (or otherwise restyle) chart section labels, mutate the EXISTING `sectionLabelStyle` field in place via `page.mutateNode`: `node.props.chart.pieChart.sectionLabelStyle.fontSizeValue = FFDoubleValue(inputValue: ...)` - keeps the field's existing `themeStyle` base (e.g. `HEADLINE_SMALL`) while overriding just the size, the same "theme base + override" shape already used for ordinary `Text` node styling elsewhere in this file. Default label size is the theme's `headlineSmall` (24px in this project) - almost certainly too large for a small chart with several slices; budget for shrinking it whenever `sectionLabel` is turned on on a compact chart.
- **`patch.pieChartConfig(..., sectionLabel: PieSectionLabel.percent)` (or `.value`) - specifically the FIRST TIME a chart's `sectionLabel` is turned on from its default `NONE` - can reassign FRESH KEYS to the Chart node AND its immediate wrapper/Row, unlike every other property patch in this file (`page.update`/`page.mutateNode` normally preserve keys; only `ensureReplaced` is documented as one-shot-key-reassigning).** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.138): `Chart_gho0plh9`/`Container_6j9ihppj`/`Row_3qhtc5xf` (AdminDashboardPage's pie chart, touched by property patches only across §18.112-137, keys stable the whole time) all got fresh keys (`Chart_yzxjtds6`/`Container_w6coshx1`/`Row_6n1cs1c3`) in the exact push that first set `sectionLabel: percent` - `donutHoleRadius`/`sectionSpacing` changes alone (§18.112/136) never did this. Symptom: a LATER push (even one touching totally different code) fails with `findByPath(...) found no matches` or `byKey("...") expected exactly one widget, found 0` for a key that has worked in every prior push. Fix: after ANY push that changes a chart's `sectionLabel` for the first time, re-verify that chart's own key AND its wrapper's key via `flutterflow ai inspect --selector-key <old-key> --tree` (a `Selector key not found` response confirms the reassignment) before authoring the next task against it - don't trust the last-known typed SDK snapshot. Also confirmed the SAME push silently discarded explicit dimension values a `page.mutateNode` had JUST set on that same node moments earlier (came back as unexplained defaults, never authored anywhere) - treat any `page.mutateNode` bundled in the SAME `editPage` call as a `pieChartConfig` `sectionLabel`-enabling patch as unreliable; if both are needed, verify the mutateNode's own values survived (via `inspect --selector-key`) rather than assuming a later push's dimensions are still whatever was last authored.
- **Full end-to-end backend verification technique (mint token → exchange for ID token → call the callable's HTTPS endpoint directly), already documented above for isolating "no data" bugs, doubles as the fastest way to answer "is this chart/widget genuinely dynamic or just showing static/mock data."** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.138): rather than trust that a chart bound to `_model.xxx` fields is dynamic, traced the full chain - page's own `getJsonField(...)` on-load binding → the custom action that calls it → the deployed Cloud Function's actual source (real Firestore aggregation, not a mock) → a direct authenticated call to the LIVE deployed function, confirming it returns genuinely fresh, real-time counts. A model's own hardcoded field default (e.g. `List<int> userTypeValues = [450, 280, 45];` in the page model file) is NOT evidence of static/mock data - it's only the pre-load placeholder shown before the async `getJsonField` assignment resolves; check the on-page-load handler, not the model's declared default, to determine what's actually rendered post-load.
- **Setting a text-typed `FFVariable.defaultValue`/`initialText` fallback to an EMPTY STRING (`''`) is not the same as "no fallback configured" from the compiler's perspective - it silently DROPS the generated `valueOrDefault<String>(...)` null-safety wrapper entirely, producing a bare `getJsonField(...).toString()` that renders the literal text `"null"` for any genuinely-missing value, instead of an empty field.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.139, BannerFormDialogComp's title/URL/advertiser fields): proto3 scalar string fields don't distinguish "explicitly set to the zero value" from "never set," so `FFParameterValue(serializedValue: '')` and an absent `defaultValue` compile identically - and identically WRONG, since the compiler's own codegen decision for which shape to emit (bare `.toString()` vs. the `valueOrDefault` wrapper) keys off whether a MEANINGFUL default is present. Compiled and pushed clean with no error either way - only caught by re-reading `generated_code/` after the push, not by trusting a clean compile. **Never use `''` as a text field's "no value" default when the field's downstream validation needs to distinguish "genuinely empty" from "has the placeholder" - either keep a real non-empty placeholder (e.g. this project's own `'-'` convention) and adjust the CONSUMING validation logic to also reject that placeholder, or don't touch `defaultValue` at all.**
- **Removing the DSL code that set a value in an earlier push does NOT revert that value - the live server state from the earlier (already-executed) push persists until something EXPLICITLY sets it back.** Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.139): after the empty-string-default mistake above landed live, the very next push simply omitted that mutation entirely (reasoning "I won't touch it, so it stays as it originally was") - re-checking `generated_code/` afterward showed the SAME broken bare-`.toString()` shape, unchanged, because the flawed value was already permanently written to the server in the prior push. A genuine revert requires an explicit push that sets the field back to its ORIGINAL value - "stop authoring the mutation" only prevents FUTURE damage, it does not undo damage already done. Always re-verify the actual current live state (`generated_code/` or `inspect --selector-key`) before assuming "I stopped touching X" is equivalent to "X is back to normal."
- **A NATIVE FlutterFlow `uploadData` action's resulting file-URL model field (e.g. `_model.uploadedFileUrl_<actionSuffix>`) has NO exposed path through EITHER of this SDK's two normal `FFVariable` sourcing mechanisms for use in an `FFActionCondition`** - not `LOCAL_STATE`/`WIDGET_CLASS_STATE` (`_localStateVariable` fails with `State field "..." not found`, confirmed the field is absent from `classModel.stateFields` even though it's genuinely referenced in generated Dart), and not `ACTION_OUTPUTS` either (the native `uploadData` action type has no `outputVariableName` field at all - confirmed via a live `inspect --selector-key` dump of the button's own trigger, unlike a `customAction` call or most other action types, which always declare one). Confirmed 2026-08-08 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.139) while attempting to gate a Save button on "was an image actually uploaded" - had to abandon a typed-condition approach for this specific field entirely and scope the fix down rather than force an unverifiable raw proto construction. If a future task genuinely needs to condition on an upload's result, expect to need either a DIFFERENT mechanism entirely (e.g. wiring the upload action's own follow-up chain directly, rather than reading its result from an unrelated later trigger) or to accept this as a real, disclosed gap - don't guess a proto shape for this specific field without first finding a working precedent to copy, the way every other successful raw-proto construction in this file did.
- **Commenting out an entire `app.editPage`/`app.editComponent` block because ONE of its operations has a stale key can silently discard OTHER, still-valid fixes bundled inside that same block - always read the WHOLE block being commented out before doing it, not just the operation that's actually failing.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.142): §18.136's block contained both a card-size restoration (`Container_lbohc44g` → fixed 250x350, matching the sibling bar-chart card) AND other chart edits whose keys later went stale for an unrelated reason. When that whole block was commented out in §18.137 to fix the stale-key operations, the size restoration went down with it as unnoticed collateral damage - re-exposing an EARLIER `clearHeight()` call (from §18.132, textually before it, superseded-but-never-removed) that had been silently masked by last-write-wins script ordering the whole time. The regression wasn't caught for 2 more pushes, only surfacing when the client explicitly reported the card no longer matched its sibling. **Before commenting out a whole block for a stale-key error, check every operation inside it - if any of them are still doing real, independent work (not just superseded-but-harmless idempotent patches), either only comment out the specific stale operations, or explicitly re-author the still-needed ones as their own new standalone block** so they can't be taken down again by a future, unrelated edit to the same file region.
- **Every `Chart` widget in this project (bar AND line) defaults to `AxisLabelInfo.showLabels = false` for BOTH axes (confirmed in `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/flutter_flow_charts.dart`'s own `AxisLabelInfo` class) - `xLabels`/data being correctly bound and `reservedSize` being set do NOT make axis titles actually render; a separate, easy-to-miss boolean has to be turned on too, and this app's charts were never doing that.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.143, AdminDashboardPage's bar chart, `Chart_qbs84ej9`): months were correctly bound (`xLabels: _model.monthLabels`, real data) and 28px was reserved for them, but nothing ever rendered in that space because `showLabels` was never explicitly set. No typed patch surface exists for this - fix via raw `mutateNode`: `node.props.chart.barChart.axisInfo.axisLabels.bottom.showLabels = true;` (and `.left` for the Y axis) - `FFChartAxisInfo_AxisLabel` (field 2 in `flutterflow.pb.dart`). **Any OTHER chart in this project inherited from the same native/legacy baseline is worth checking for this exact same gap before assuming its axis labels are just intentionally hidden.**
- **`showLabels: true` alone is NOT sufficient to make a chart's axis titles actually render - `AxisLabelInfo.labelInterval` (passed straight into fl_chart's own `SideTitles.interval`) also has to make sense for the axis's actual VALUE RANGE, or fl_chart's tick-generation will produce zero usable positions to draw a title at, even with the "show" flag correctly on.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.144, the SAME `Chart_qbs84ej9` as the entry above): a pre-existing, never-authored `labelInterval: 10` on the bottom axis - a bar chart indexed by category POSITION (0-4 for 5 months) - is larger than the entire axis range, so fl_chart's tick stepping (0, 10, 20...) only ever has a candidate near position 0 and never revisits 1-4; the Y axis had the identical `10` misconfigured the opposite way (sales figures span into the tens of thousands, so `interval: 10` asks for thousands of ticks, which fl_chart likely declines to render at all). **When axis labels still don't appear after confirming `showLabels: true`, check `labelInterval` next** - and prefer `clearLabelInterval()` (confirmed to exist on `FFChartAxisInfo_AxisLabel`) over guessing a new hardcoded number: fl_chart auto-computes a sensible interval from the actual data range when `interval` is left `null`, correct by construction regardless of how the chart's own data scale changes later, unlike a fixed number that will just recreate this exact bug at a different data scale.
- **When a client reports "still not fixed" after a verified-correct server-side change, don't default to "clear your cache" twice in a row - ask a TARGETED diagnostic question that can actually distinguish "your view is stale" from "the fix has a real remaining bug," using another recently-changed, independently-verifiable element on the SAME page as a control.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.144): asking whether the sibling pie chart's own separately-pushed, already-confirmed size increase (§18.142) was visible - the client confirmed yes - immediately ruled out a general stale-Test-Mode-session explanation (which would have affected an unrelated chart on the same page identically) and correctly redirected the investigation toward a bar-chart-specific bug, which is exactly what was there (`labelInterval`, the entry above). A second "have you tried clearing your cache" would have wasted another full round-trip on an already-falsified theory.
- **A COMPONENT's own root node (the outermost handle in `ff.Components.xxx.widgets` - typically the SAME key exposed as the component handle's own `.key`) can be a synthetic `isDummyRoot: true` boundary wrapper with NO corresponding Flutter output at all - patching its decoration compiles clean, pushes clean, and is genuinely written to the live proto (confirmed via `inspect --tree`), but produces zero visible change even after a forced `codegen refresh`.** Confirmed twice independently in this project - first at an earlier sidebar-height fix (this file's own history, `dsl/edit.dart` ~line 10664), then rediscovered fresh in §18.146 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md) when a gradient patch on `Main_Menu_Comp`'s root (`Container_ro6df76s`) had no effect. **Before patching a component's root-level Container for anything visual (color/gradient/border/shadow/size), inspect ONE LEVEL IN (`ff.Components.xxx.widgets.byKey(<root>.children[0]'s key)` / a live `inspect --tree` on the root) and compare `dimensions`/`boxDecoration` against what `generated_code/` actually shows for the outermost VISIBLE `Container(...)` - if the root's own props are empty/default while a child one level down carries the real size and `themeColor`, that child is the real target, not the root.** This is the SAME general "the child governs what's actually rendered, not the wrapper" class of bug as the `BannerFormDialogComp` modal-shell entry above (§18.117-era) - components' own synthetic root wrapper is simply the most extreme case of it, since the wrapper isn't just visually overridden by its child, it emits no Flutter code whatsoever.
- **When redesigning a whole page/component's visual style in one pass (as opposed to one card/widget), read the CURRENT decoration of every section straight from `generated_code/` before writing any patch, not from memory of earlier sessions' fixes to nearby elements - and re-verify every patch group afterward with WHITESPACE-NORMALIZED string matching, not naive single-line `grep`.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.145/146, the dashboard-wide redesign): a first-pass `grep` for `BorderRadius.circular(20.0)`/`Colors.white`/etc. across the freshly-regenerated page file returned suspiciously low or zero counts for patches already independently confirmed correct by direct reads - purely because Dart's own 80-column formatter wraps long expressions like `BorderRadius.circular(\n  20.0)` or `color: Colors\n  .white` across multiple lines, and `Colors.white` (no space) never matches the wrapped `Colors\n  .white` (which collapses to `Colors .white`, WITH a space, under a naive `\s+ -> ' '` normalization). The reliable check is `re.sub(r'\s+', '', text)` (strip ALL whitespace, not just collapse it) before substring-counting - false negatives from this exact formatting artifact cost real investigation time before being traced to a non-issue.
- **A widget's on-page-load entrance animation (`EditWidgetPatch.animations([WidgetAnimation(...)])`, `widgets.dart`'s `WidgetAnimation`/`AnimationEffect` sealed-class family - `FadeEffect`/`SlideEffect`/`ScaleEffect`/`RotateEffect`/`ShakeEffect`/`ShimmerEffect`/`BlurEffect`/`TintEffect`/`SaturateEffect`/`TiltEffect`/`FlipEffect`) is a real, unrestricted typed patch (no widget-type gate, unlike `size`/`gradient`) - but its STAGGER DELAY must be set on each individual `AnimationEffect` (`FadeEffect(delayMs:)`, `SlideEffect(delayMs:)`, etc.), never on the `WidgetAnimation` wrapper itself, even though `WidgetAnimation` exposes its own top-level `delayMs` parameter that looks like the obvious place for it.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.145/146 revision, staggering 15 dashboard cards' entrance animations 60ms apart): setting a distinct, clearly non-zero `WidgetAnimation(delayMs: ...)` per card (0/60/120/.../840ms) compiled clean and pushed clean, but every one of the 15 generated `AnimationInfo` entries in `generated_code/` came back with `delay: 0.0.ms` on both effects regardless - `WidgetAnimation.delayMs` writes to a real proto field (`FFAnimation.delay`, confirmed via `edit.dart`'s `_buildFFAnimation`), but codegen does not read it for the on-page-load case. Moving the same delay value onto `FadeEffect(delayMs:)`/`SlideEffect(delayMs:)` instead (which write to each effect's own `FFEffect.delay`) fixed it - reconfirmed all 15 distinct delay values present in regenerated code on the next push. **Treat `WidgetAnimation`-level `delayMs`/`durationMs` as unverified/likely-inert for on-page-load animations until proven otherwise on a different trigger type - set timing on the per-effect fields instead, they're what's actually confirmed wired through codegen.**
- **There is no hover trigger anywhere in this project's exposed styling/animation surface for `Container` widgets - a "flash/glow on mouse hover" request cannot be built the way an on-page-load or on-tap animation can, for a plain Container.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.145/146 second revision), checked from multiple angles before concluding it's a real gap, not a missed API: (1) `AnimationTrigger` (`widgets.dart`) only has `onPageLoad`/`onActionTrigger` - no hover variant; (2) `FFActionTriggerType` (`flutterflow.pbenum.dart`), the full proto trigger enum covering 100+ tap/drag/swipe/semantics variants, has no `ON_HOVER`/mouse-enter equivalent either; (3) the DSL's `MouseRegion` widget (`widgets.dart`) only exposes `cursor:` styling, no `onEnter`/`onExit` callback hooks at all; (4) `FFContainer`'s own proto message (`flutterflow.pb.dart`) has zero hover-related fields. **A real native hover-color mechanism DOES exist** (`hasHoverStyle`/`hoverColorValue`/`hoverIconColorValue`/`hoverBorderColorValue`) but is scoped to `FFButton`/`FFIconButton` ONLY, not `FFContainer` - confirmed by reading each message definition directly rather than assuming the fields found on one widget type generalize to another. **If a future task needs genuine hover feedback on an existing Container-based element** (a card, a custom-styled tile, etc.), the honest options are: (a) it's simply not achievable via a property patch on that Container - flag this to the client rather than silently skipping or faking it with an unrelated trigger: (b) convert the specific widget to `Button`/`IconButton` to gain the hover fields - a real structural type change, risky if the widget already has other triggers/children that don't map cleanly onto Button's semantics (verify nothing breaks, especially any existing `ON_TAP` action, before proposing this); (c) custom Flutter code wrapping the widget in a `MouseRegion(onEnter:, onExit:)` driving local state - genuine engineering effort, and if the wrapped widget already has its own trigger (e.g. `ON_TAP`), extra care is needed so the wrapper doesn't intercept/break existing gesture handling. None of these are a quick patch - surface the tradeoff to the client (via `AskUserQuestion` or equivalent) rather than picking one unilaterally when "maintain all existing features" is an explicit constraint.
- **A "RenderFlex overflowed" report on a card/container whose own width is a hardcoded fallback (e.g. wrapped in `Expanded` but still carrying its own explicit `width:`) can be structural, not numeric - check whether the offending content's immediate parent is a bare `Row`/`Column` (which enforces "child must fit or throw") versus something more tolerant like `Align`/`Stack`, before assuming a slightly-too-large pixel value is the whole story.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.147, AdminDashboardPage's bar chart): the bar card's chart sat inside a bare `Row(children: [Container(width: 350, ...)])` inside a 250px-declared card - a classic overflow setup, since a Row's non-flexible child MUST fit within the Row's own available width or Flutter throws the visible yellow-black overflow banner. The SIBLING pie chart had an almost identical numeric mismatch (340-wide chart inside the same 250px-declared card) but never triggered a visible error, because ITS chart sits inside an `Align(alignment: center, child: ...)` instead of a bare Row child - `Align` silently centers/tolerates an oversized child rather than enforcing a hard fit-or-throw contract. **When comparing two structurally-similar elements where only one overflows, diff their actual PARENT WIDGET TYPES, not just their pixel dimensions** - the fix that follows from this diagnosis is to shrink the offending content to fit the space its own parent GUARANTEES (not the space it might opportunistically get from an `Expanded` upstream), rather than inserting a new tolerant wrapper widget, which is a larger structural change for the same result.
- **When a client asks to "match page X's style" across many other pages, DO NOT assume every page shares one shell component - some parts may already be shared (free, zero-work) and others may be independently copy-pasted per page (needing a real patch per page), and only reading the actual generated structure across a sample of pages reveals which is which.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.148/149, the 33-page gold rollout): `Main_Menu_Comp` (the sidebar) is a genuinely SHARED component instantiated by 34 of 35 pages, so restyling it once (already done for the dashboard) had already propagated everywhere - zero further work needed. The header bar LOOKS like it should be the same kind of shared element but isn't - every page independently authors an identical-looking "two-tier shell" Row from scratch with its own fresh keys, so matching its style elsewhere genuinely requires a patch per page, unlike the sidebar. **Before scoping a multi-page consistency task, check for each visually-shared element whether it's backed by one real shared component (patch once) or copy-pasted per page (patch N times)** - grep `generated_code/` for the component's own widget class name (e.g. `MainMenuCompWidget(`) across every page; if it appears N times pointing at the SAME single source file, it's shared; if every page has its own independently-keyed but structurally-identical subtree instead, it isn't.
- **To safely propagate a proven-good widget-key-discovery pattern from ONE already-correctly-styled page to many structurally-similar sibling pages, diff the sibling pages' typed SDK files against the reference page's own known-good keys by RELATIVE PATH SUFFIX (not exact key value, which is randomly generated per page) - and expect at least one structural variant to exist, not a uniform 100% match.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.148/149): a Python script computed each needed element's path suffix relative to `AdminDashboardPage`'s own already-known-correct header keys, then looked up the identical relative suffix (after stripping the page's own class-name prefix) in each of 32 other pages' typed SDK files. 28 pages matched perfectly on the first attempt; 4 pages matched only after calibrating a SECOND reference pattern from one of the mismatches (they had one fewer nesting level - only one header Container instead of two); 1 page had a manually-rebuilt, structurally incompatible shell (its own DSL comment literally named it `"ProcessedEventsPageShell"`) requiring a fully manual special case. **This 28/4/1 split is the expected shape of this kind of propagation - budget for at least one deviating cluster and one true outlier, and spot-verify a sample of the automated matches against the actual source (not just "the script found something non-null") before trusting the pattern at full scale.** For an anchor point that might shift depth between pages (e.g. a page title that sits directly under one container on the root page but one level deeper, alongside an extra "back" button, on every non-root page), anchor on a stable semantic marker instead of a fixed path depth - e.g. locate the node with `componentName == "Main_Menu_Comp"`, then depth-first-search ITS sibling subtree for the first `Text` node, rather than assuming a fixed number of `.children[N]` hops always lands on the title.
- **`ProjectPageHandle<TParams, TState>` (and `ProjectComponentHandle<TParams, TState>`) can be accepted as a function parameter with its generic type arguments OMITTED, letting one shared helper function apply the identical patch logic across many differently-typed `ff.Pages.*`/`ff.Components.*` handles instead of duplicating the same `app.editPage(...)`/`app.editComponent(...)` block once per page.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.148/149): every page's typed handle has a DIFFERENT concrete type (`ProjectPageHandle<UserManagementPageParams, UserManagementPageState>` vs. `ProjectPageHandle<BannerListPageParams, BannerListPageState>`, etc.), which looks like it would block writing one shared function across all of them - but `ProjectPageHandle.widgets` (the only member these patch helpers actually need) does not depend on either generic parameter (confirmed by reading `project_sdk.dart` directly), so a function signature like `void _applyPatch(App app, ProjectPageHandle pageHandle, {...})` (bare, no `<...>`) compiles and works correctly regardless of which concrete page is passed in. Worth reaching for whenever the SAME patch shape needs to be applied to more than a handful of pages/components - far less error-prone than hand-duplicating a multi-step `app.editPage` block dozens of times, and any generated per-page call sites become a single line each (just the page handle + its own discovered keys as named arguments).
- **When a DSL rollout touches more pages than fit in `generated_code/`'s flat legacy `pages/` folder layout, the regenerated snapshot for most pages lives under per-feature-group directories instead (e.g. `generated_code/lib/user_management/`, `generated_code/lib/banner/`) - always resolve the actual file path via `generated_code/.flutterflow/export_manifest.json`'s `primary_files`, never assume a `generated_code/lib/pages/<slug>/` path exists.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.148/149): only 3 of 35 pages/components (the ones actually touched by DSL pushes so far this session) have a folder under `generated_code/lib/pages/` at all - the other 32 pages' own generated Dart lives under feature-group folders that don't obviously correlate with the page's own file name. **Also confirmed the manifest's entity `name` field does not always match a naive PascalCase conversion of the page's snake_case file name** - e.g. `kycreview_management_page.dart` maps to manifest entity name `"KYCReviewManagementPage"` (mixed-case "KYC" acronym), not the mechanically-derived `"KycreviewManagementPage"` - look up entities by scanning the manifest's actual `name` list rather than assuming a fixed casing transform when verifying a page you didn't just push.
- **A never-commented-out one-shot `ensureReplaced` doesn't just risk being silently discarded (the already-documented failure mode above) - it can sit ACTIVE and keep re-running on every single subsequent push indefinitely, reassigning fresh keys to its ENTIRE replaced subtree each time, quietly invalidating every other DSL call in the file that references any key inside that subtree, no matter how unrelated that other call's own purpose is.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.150): `ProcessedEventsListPage`'s own shell-rebuild `ensureReplaced` (authored 2026-08-07) carried a comment explicitly saying "comment this out once confirmed landed, same discipline as every other `ensureReplaced` call in this file" - but the comment was never acted on. It kept re-running on every push since, and because `ensureReplaced` has no dedup guard, each run reassigned a BRAND NEW key to literally every node in that page's tree. Two consequences, both silent until this investigation: (1) an unrelated `_insertEmptyStateAfter` call for this same page's empty-list message, authored later and referencing a key captured from a specific past push, never actually landed from the day it was written - it kept referencing a key that was already stale by the time ITS OWN push ran, since the rebuild (textually earlier in the file, so it always executes first within the same script run) had already reassigned everything by then; (2) a LATER, entirely unrelated task (§18.150's own header-recolor work, which had correctly captured this page's keys during the immediately-prior push) failed outright on its very next push with `byKey(...) expected exactly one widget, found 0`, because the SAME rebuild ran again and reassigned the keys out from under it - despite §18.150 never touching this page's structure at all. **Whenever a hardcoded key reference to any page/component fails with "expected exactly one widget, found 0" and you're confident the key was correct as of the last push, grep the whole file for OTHER `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/etc. calls targeting the SAME page that might still be active** - the actual bug may be nowhere near the failing line, in a completely different, much-earlier section of the script that silently reshuffles the ground out from under it on every run. The fix is exactly what the original one-shot discipline already prescribes: comment out the rebuild once its `generated_code/` result is confirmed correct, then re-capture and fix every OTHER reference to that page's keys in the same push (there may be more than one, and they can be old and easy to forget about).
- **The never-commented-out `ensureReplaced` failure mode above is now confirmed SYSTEMIC across this file, not an isolated one-off - a single reactive fix (comment out the one that just broke your current push) is not enough; do a full project-wide sweep whenever you hit even one instance.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.152): fixing `AuditLogListPage`'s active `ensureReplaced` (which broke the CURRENT push) still left **7 more** active instances undiscovered elsewhere in the file - `GuestUserdetailsPage` (x2, `ReservationHistoryList`/`PaymentHistoryList`), `KYCReviewListPage`, `CastUserListPage`, `StaffUserListPage`, `AdministratorListPage`, `CocomiseListPage` - each capable of independently breaking some FUTURE unrelated push the same way, on its own schedule. Found via a Python script scanning the whole file for every `ensureReplaced` occurrence and tracking `/* */` comment nesting depth to classify each as active vs. already-frozen; verify each candidate was already successfully landed (`generated_code/` has the expected `itemBuilder`/structure) before freezing it, so you don't accidentally disable a rebuild that hasn't landed yet. **Whenever you fix one instance of this bug reactively, immediately run the same whole-file sweep before moving on** - do not assume the one you just found is the only one; treat "zero active `ensureReplaced` calls remain" as a checkable invariant (rerun the same nesting-depth scan against the final file) rather than a one-off cleanup. Also confirmed a key can drift AGAIN one more time even in the SAME session between "capture the key" and "the freeze takes effect" if the rebuild runs one more time in between - after freezing, re-verify any key captured before the freeze against live state rather than assuming the freeze made it retroactively stable.
- **This §266 systemic-sweep rule has now confirmed-fired in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (the guest app) too, not just the admin dashboard it was originally written up in — treat it as a general SDK-level risk on every project sharing this DSL, not a lesson scoped to wherever it happened to be found first.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (that project's own `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §28's freeze addendum): catching one unfrozen `ensureReplaced` on `reservation_confirmed.dart` (caught via the trace-level `NO_OP` check showing a spurious `Modified pages: 1` instead of the expected no-op) and running the same nesting-depth sweep this rule prescribes found **11 more** active-but-landed instances across `reservation_form.dart` (6 dropdown reconstructions) and `reservation_detail.dart` (6 action-row reconstructions) — same pattern, same root cause (finding one instance and only fixing that one, instead of sweeping immediately), same fix. Worth explicitly re-running this sweep on ANY project using `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` extensively, the first time you catch even one unfrozen instance in that project's history, rather than assuming a fresh project starts clean of this pattern just because it's young.
- **The local typed SDK (`lib/flutterflow_project/pages/*.dart` / `components/*.dart`) is not reliable ground truth for a page whose structure may have drifted (e.g. from the `ensureReplaced` bug above) - not even immediately after running `flutterflow ai refresh-context`.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.152): the typed SDK files for `AuditLogListPage`/`AdministratorListPage`/`CocomiseListPage`/`KYCReviewListPage` were all missing the very keys needed, and stayed unchanged (same mtime, same stale content) even after an explicit `refresh-context` call - the command appears to have its own caching/diffing behavior that doesn't always force a full rewrite. `flutterflow ai inspect --page <Name> --outline --max-depth <N>` (a live ASCII tree with `# selector: findByKey(...)` annotations) was the only source that reflected genuinely current server state. **When a page has (or ever had) an active structural rebuild in its history and a key-based patch is failing, don't trust the typed SDK file even right after a refresh - go straight to a live `inspect --outline` instead.** Conversely, don't over-correct into distrusting the typed SDK everywhere: `GuestUserListPage`'s own file was 5 days stale by mtime yet still structurally correct, because only property patches (which never reassign keys) had ever touched that specific page - staleness of the FILE only implies staleness of the KEYS for a page with an `ensureReplaced`/similar rebuild in its history, not universally.
- **`dart analyze`'s `unused_element` check only flags the OUTERMOST unreferenced symbol in a dead call chain, never anything transitively dead beneath it - deleting a flagged function can newly orphan its own callees, which only surface on a FRESH analyze run afterward.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.155, a ~1,500-line dead-code sweep): the original `dart analyze` run flagged 26 unreferenced functions, but `_userListCardRowContent`/`_fieldPairBox`/`_statusValue`/`_kvRow`/`_serviceAreaItemStruct` were NOT among them - each was still "referenced," but only by another function (`_castRow`/`_userListCardRow`/`_serviceAreaRow`) that was ITSELF one of the 26 flagged as dead. The analyzer only checks "is symbol X referenced anywhere in the file's live/parseable code," not "is there a reachable path from an actual entry point to X" - a dead function's own body still counts as a live textual reference to whatever it calls, even though nothing ever calls the dead function itself. **After deleting any batch of `unused_element`-flagged declarations, re-run `dart analyze` and repeat until it reports zero NEW `unused_element` warnings** - a single pass against the original warning list will silently leave orphaned callees behind.
- **A local variable flagged `unused_local_variable` is NOT automatically safe to delete along with its declaring statement - check what the right-hand side actually does first.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.155): spot-checking a batch of these warnings found two genuinely different cases. Pure, side-effect-free value construction (`FFIdentifier(name:, key:)`, `ComponentHandle(ComponentDeclaration(...))`) is safe to delete whole-cloth if truly unused downstream - nothing else depends on the statement having run. But `final xFn = app.customFunction(...)` (or `addCustomFunction`/`addCustomAction`/any other declarative SDK call with a real server-side effect) is NOT the same shape even though the lint warning looks identical - the STATEMENT is what matters (it declares/redeclares the function on every push), not whether the local variable holding its return value gets read again afterward. Deleting the whole statement in that second case is a functional change (stops keeping that custom function in sync with the DSL source), not a cleanup. **When in doubt, check the RHS's type/behavior per warning rather than batch-deleting every `unused_local_variable` hit the same way `unused_element` hits get handled.**
- **`generated_code/assets/` (the local Flutter snapshot's own asset folders) never mirrors real binary media for ANY asset, working or not - it's a source-only export, not proof an image is or isn't actually bundled.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.158 re-investigation): `find generated_code/assets -type f` shows the SAME `favicon.png` duplicated as the ONLY file across every single subfolder (`images/`, `fonts/`, `pdfs/`, `videos/`, `rive_animations/`, `audios/`, `jsons/`) - a structural placeholder, not real content. Even long-established, definitely-working assets (`icoccha_logo.png`, `icoccha_logo_admin.png`) are equally absent from this local folder despite being referenced correctly in the same generated Dart source and rendering fine live. **Never use "is the file physically present in `generated_code/assets/`" as evidence for or against a rendering bug** - verify real asset availability by asking the client to check the builder's own asset picker/library instead, or by reading live proto state via `inspect --tree`, neither of which this local export can substitute for.
- **Switching an `Image` widget's `type` (e.g. `FF_IMAGE_TYPE_NETWORK` → `FF_IMAGE_TYPE_ASSET`, which also changes the underlying rendered Flutter widget class - `Image.network` vs. `SvgPicture.asset`) can cause FlutterFlow's backend to reassign that node a FRESH KEY on a later save/normalize pass, not just update its properties in place - the same class of stale-key hazard already documented above for chart `sectionLabel` toggles and `ensureReplaced` reruns, now confirmed for `Image.type` changes too.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.158 re-investigation): `AdminDashboardPage`'s logo `Image` node, hardcoded as `Image_yvpq8q38` in the DSL and successfully matched at push time, had become `Image_7v01vsv2` by the next live `inspect --outline` - no DSL edit referenced the new key anywhere; FlutterFlow's own backend renamed it. **After any push that changes an Image's `type`, re-verify that node's key via a live `inspect --outline`/`--tree` before authoring the next task against it** - don't trust a hardcoded key or the last-known typed SDK snapshot to still be valid.
- **A DSL push that adds a genuinely NEW pub dependency to the project (e.g. `flutter_svg`, auto-added by codegen the first time an `Image` is typed as `FF_IMAGE_TYPE_ASSET` with an `.svg` path) will not take effect in an already-open Test Mode session, even after the client clears cache or refreshes the page - Flutter's hot-reload/hot-restart mechanism (what keeps a live preview session fast) cannot link in a package that wasn't part of that process's original compile; picking up a new `pubspec.yaml` entry requires a full stop-and-relaunch of the run, not a page-level refresh.** Confirmed 2026-08-09 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §18.158 re-investigation): after matching the client's own proven-working screenshot configuration exactly (verified byte-for-byte via live `inspect --tree` against server state, `flutter_svg: 2.1.0` correctly present in `pubspec.yaml`/`pubspec.lock`, asset confirmed genuinely uploaded via the builder's own asset picker) the image still didn't render - the one remaining variable was that the client had been iterating in a single long-running Test Mode session across the whole multi-round saga. **Whenever a fix's own `generated_code/pubspec.yaml` gains a dependency it didn't have before the fix, tell the client explicitly to fully stop and start a fresh Test Mode session (not just refresh/clear cache) before concluding the fix itself is broken** - this is a different, easy-to-miss failure class from every other "still not showing" bug in this file, which were all genuinely wrong proto/DSL state, not a stale running process.

### New quirks found in this project

- **A custom action added via the brownfield `addCustomAction(project, ...)` helper (inside `app.raw(...)`) is invisible to a page built via the declarative `app.ensurePage(...)` in the SAME script run — `compileDslApp` fails with `No custom action named "..." found in the project`, even though both were declared correctly in the same push.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §9, the onboarding chain build): declarative entities (pages, enums, structs, custom actions declared via `app.customAction`) appear to compile in one pass, with `app.raw`-queued mutations (including `addCustomAction`) applied only afterward — so a raw-declared action doesn't exist yet when an in-the-same-run page's `CallCustomAction.named(...)` reference is validated. **Fix: use the declarative `app.customAction(...)` instead of `addCustomAction`/`app.raw` for a brand-new custom action referenced by a brand-new page in the same push** — `app.customAction`'s own doc comment confirms an identical rerun is a no-op (only a changed payload throws), so it's safe enough for an edit flow despite the general brownfield guidance elsewhere preferring `find*`/`add*` helpers. Still one-shot enough to comment out once confirmed landed, same standing discipline as every other declarative call in this file.
- **`app.editPage(...)`'s own page argument requires the typed `ff.Pages.*` handle, not a raw string page name, once this workspace's DSL has touched the project at all — same for `WidgetState(...)`'s widget-reference argument when pointing at a PRE-EXISTING native widget.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §9): `compileDslApp` rejected `app.editPage('SignupPage', ...)` with `Use ff.Pages.signupPage instead of a raw page name`, and `WidgetState('MobileNnmberField', ...)` with `Use ff.Pages.phoneVarification.widgets.byKey(...).single instead`. **`Navigate(...)`'s own page-target argument is explicitly exempt** — confirmed by reading `_resolvePageName`'s source directly (`String value => value` is a fully sanctioned, separate code path), and confirmed empirically: dozens of `Navigate('PageName')` string calls in the same push compiled clean with zero complaint. Also exempt: a widget freshly inserted in the SAME push (no typed handle exists for it yet — a raw string/name reference there is correct, not a workaround, consistent with the already-known "same-run-inserted widget has no typed handle" rule).
- **To retarget a `Navigate` action buried inside an existing native multi-step trigger chain, without reproducing (and risking silently dropping) any of the chain's other real logic**, use `page.mutateNode(selection, (node) { ... })` to reach `node.triggerActions.first.rootAction` (assuming one trigger type on the node — check `node.triggerActions` for more if there could be several), then walk it recursively through BOTH `followUpAction` and `conditionActions`' true/false branches (a linear walk alone misses anything nested in a conditional — the same landmine already documented for other action-chain walkers in this file) looking for `node.hasAction() && node.action.hasNavigate() && node.action.navigate.pageNodeKeyRef.key == oldTargetKey`, reassigning just that one field in place. Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §9) fixing SignupPage's own post-signup routing (was hardcoded to Home, bypassing the entire verification chain) — verified via the regenerated `generated_code/`: the button's `context.pushNamedAuth(...)` target changed to the new page while the password-match check, `createAccountWithEmail` call, Firestore write, and success Snackbar all stayed byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **No native typed-DSL action exists for calling a Cloud Function directly** (confirmed by reading `actions.dart` in full — no `Callable`/`CloudFunction`-style class). The established pattern for this project (matching its own pre-existing `callCreatePaymentIntent`/`confirmStripePayment` custom actions) is a custom action wrapping `FirebaseFunctions.instanceFor(region:).httpsCallable(name).call({...})` — write a new one per callable needed, following that exact shape.
- **`app.ensurePage(...)`'s own idempotency is narrower than it looks — once the page exists, its `body:`/`state:` content becomes PERMANENTLY INERT on every future run (confirmed by reading `compiler.dart`'s `_compilePages` directly: pages already matched by name are filtered out and skipped entirely before compilation), yet the typed-SDK-authoring VALIDATION pass still checks that inert body's own `State(...)`/`SetState(...)`/`Navigate(...)` references against current server state.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §9/§10): a `BasicInfoRegistration` page built via `ensurePage(...)` with raw string state references compiled fine on the FIRST push (page didn't exist yet); the very next push — which didn't even touch this page, just added unrelated KYC wiring — failed with `Use ff.Pages.basicInfoRegistration.state.accountType instead of State("accountType")`, even though that `ensurePage` call's body would never be re-applied either way. **Fix: once an `ensurePage(...)`-created page is confirmed live, comment out the ENTIRE call** (not just fix the reference style) — same one-shot discipline as every other declarative call in this file; any FUTURE change to that page's own content must go through `app.editPage(ff.Pages.x, ...)` instead, since the `ensurePage` call can never apply an update again regardless of what its body says.
- **`FFActionCondition.variable`'s "completely bare, zero operations" restriction (documented above for `Equals`/`Not`-wrapped conditions) also rejects a plain nullable-String `ActionOutput` used directly as a boolean condition — not just explicit comparison operators.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §10): `If(ActionOutput('uploadResult'), ...)`, where `uploadResult` is a custom action's `String?` return (a file-upload URL, `null` on failure), failed identically to the `Equals`/`Not` case (`Conditional execution for action is improperly set`) — the implicit type-coercion from nullable-String to boolean is itself an "operation" this restriction blocks, even with no explicit comparison written in the DSL source. **Same fix as the general case: compute the boolean via a small preceding custom action** (here, a tiny reusable `isNonEmptyString(value)` returning `bool`) **and branch on ITS bare `ActionOutput(...)` instead** — never branch on a non-bool-typed `ActionOutput` directly, regardless of how "truthy" its intended meaning is.
- **`SwitchListTile` is not a valid `ON_TOGGLE_ON`/`ON_TOGGLE_OFF` target in this SDK — only `Switch`/`Checkbox` widget types are.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §12): `compileDslApp` rejected `page.ensureActions(switchListTileNode, ON_TOGGLE_ON, ...)` outright with `Bad state: ON_TOGGLE_ON requires a Switch or Checkbox target, got SwitchListTile` — a clean, specific error, not the generic "conditional execution" class seen elsewhere. **Fix: replace the native `SwitchListTile` with a DSL-authored `Checkbox`** via `ensureReplaced` (same boolean semantic, `value:`/`onChanged:` bound directly at construction — no separate ON_TOGGLE_ON/OFF wiring needed at all, simpler than the toggle-trigger approach this DSL apparently reserves for genuine `Switch`/`Checkbox` node types only).
- **List-typed page-local state fields cannot take a `.withDefault(...)` at all in this SDK build — not just `List<JSON>` (already documented above), `List<String>` hits the identical rejection.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §14): `state: {'myReservationsList': listOf(string).withDefault(<String>[])}` failed `compileDslApp` with `Unsupported operation: Default values are not supported for type ListType(...)` — generalizes the earlier List<JSON> finding to list types broadly, not just JSON element types. **Fix: declare the field with no default at all** (`listOf(string)`, full stop) — fine in practice for a field an `onLoad` action populates immediately, which covers the common case (a page-load data fetch feeding a list display).
- **A 3rd level of nested `If(...)` inside one trigger chain can fail `compileDslApp` (`Conditional execution for action is improperly set.`, reported against ALL THREE levels, not just the new one) even when every individual condition is the already-proven-safe "completely bare `ActionOutput(...)`" shape — the restriction isn't only about what's INSIDE one condition, nesting depth itself is also a factor.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §11): adding a 3rd nested `If` to `KycSubmitButton`'s already-working 2-level chain (to fork cast-vs-guest navigation after a successful submit) broke all three conditions in that chain, not just the new one — `flutterflow ai trace latest` (not the plain error list, which didn't name the widget) was needed to confirm all three violations pointed at the same button. **Fix: don't stack a 3rd level onto a working chain — split the decision across two separate triggers/pages instead**, e.g. move the deeper fork to a DIFFERENT page's own (fresh, 2-level) `ON_INIT_STATE` rather than nesting further into an already-multi-level chain. Unconfirmed whether the real ceiling is exactly 2 levels or something else entirely (sample size of one) — treat "keep nesting shallow, split deep decisions across triggers when a 3rd level would otherwise be needed" as the working rule until a future session has reason to test the boundary more precisely.
- **`app.customFunction(...)`'s `code:` parameter must be BODY-ONLY (statements like `return ...;`, a bare `switch`, etc.) — NOT a repeated function signature the way `app.customAction(...)`'s `code:` requires. Writing a full `<ReturnType> <name>(<args>) { ... }` wrapper inside `code:` compiles WITHOUT any error, but silently produces a function that always returns `null`.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §22): `dart analyze`/`flutterflow ai test`/`flutterflow ai run` all passed clean for 3 customFunction declarations (`reservationListItemLabel`/`reservationListItemResId`/`reservationListItemCastId`) that each mistakenly included a full signature inside `code:` — the SDK wraps whatever `code:` contains in ITS OWN correctly-typed outer function, so a `code:` that already contains a complete function produces a local function declaration NESTED inside an identically-named outer one, with no call/return connecting them: `String? name(args) { String name(args) { ...real logic... } }`. Dart allows a nullable-returning function to fall off the end with an implicit `return null;`, so this compiles silently and just always returns null — no analyzer warning, no compile error, no push failure, nothing but a silently-broken feature. **Confirmed via two independent checks, not just reasoning about Dart semantics**: (1) ran the exact broken shape through `dart run` standalone twice (before and after the fix) and empirically verified null-vs-real-value; (2) every `references/*.dart` example of `app.customFunction(...)` — `formatFavoriteSummary`, `summarizeLineItem`, `statusLabel`, `formatRecommendationHeadline`, `formatFavoritesCount` — uses body-only `code:`, confirming this is the universal convention, and a genuinely pre-existing native function (`calculateExtensionPrice`, confirmed via `grep` to have never been touched by DSL) has the correct flat shape for comparison. **Practical impact was severe, not cosmetic**: this broke `ReservationListPage`'s displayed text AND its row-tap navigation's `resId` param since the page first shipped (§14) — every downstream action gated on that `resId` (approve/decline/meetup/complete/review/cancel) silently no-op'd the whole time, undetected because the WIRING (call sites, correct function names) looked completely correct in `generated_code/` and nobody had checked the called function's own body-level behavior. **Fix: `updateCustomFunction(project, name:, code:)` with body-only code** (exists and works exactly like `updateCustomAction`, confirmed by reading `custom_code_helpers.dart` directly) — freeze the original `app.customFunction(...)` declaration in the same push (same landmine-avoidance rule as every other `update*` call in this file). Since `updateCustomFunction` returns `void` (no handle to capture), any later `CustomFunction(handle, ...)` reference needs a locally-constructed `CustomFunctionHandle(name:, args:, returnType:)` stub instead — confirmed safe by reading the class (`types.dart`): a plain, non-proto-tied value object matched only by name. **Whenever authoring or reviewing an `app.customFunction(...)` call, check that `code:` does NOT start with the function's own name/signature — if it does, that's this exact bug, not a stylistic choice.**
- **`flutterflow ai run`'s pipeline has (at least) two distinct validation stages — `compileDslApp` and a separate `preflight` — and `preflight` can fail on a page your OWN push never touched, blocking the push entirely even though `compileDslApp` itself passed clean.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §18): a push containing only Dart-source-hygiene changes (removing 2 unused local variables, correcting a comment — zero effect on the compiled proto) failed 3 times in a row with `[FAIL] preflight ... Preflight failed: MacchaChats. — List has a backend query but no child widget binds to list item data` — `MacchaChats` is a native page never referenced anywhere in `dsl/edit.dart`. Cross-checked `.flutterflow/runs.jsonl`: the immediately PRIOR push (same session, ~50 minutes earlier) shows `validationFailedTask: null` (succeeded) — so this specific `preflight` check either wasn't enforced yet at that point or didn't previously flag this pre-existing condition on `MacchaChats`. **Practical implications:** (1) `[OK] compileDslApp` in the CLI output is NOT sufficient evidence a push actually landed — always check the final line for `Pushed project ... (commit ...)` or inspect `.flutterflow/workspace.json`'s `lastRun.pushed`/`validationFailedTask` fields before reporting a push as successful; piping through `tail -N` can hide an earlier `[FAIL] preflight` line above a long list of non-fatal advisory warnings that follow it, making a failed run look like a pile of warnings on an otherwise-successful push. (2) A `preflight` failure can be entirely unrelated to what your own script changed — before investigating your own diff, check `.flutterflow/runs.jsonl` for whether the immediately-prior push (same project, unrelated content) also would have hit the same check, to distinguish "my change broke this" from "this was always broken and something now enforces/catches it." (3) Once `preflight` starts failing on an unrelated page, it blocks ALL subsequent pushes to the project, not just the one that first surfaced it — treat a `preflight` failure discovered mid-task as project-wide and urgent even if it's outside your current task's scope, and say so explicitly rather than silently retrying the same push.
- **`firebase deploy --only storage` failing with `Firebase Storage has not been set up on project ... Go to ... and click 'Get Started'` does NOT actually require the Console UI — the Firebase Storage Management API (`firebasestorage.googleapis.com` v1beta) has a direct `POST v1beta/projects/{project}/defaultBucket` endpoint (`firebasestorage.projects.defaultBucket.create`) whose own description is literally "Creates a Spark tier-eligible Cloud Storage bucket and links it to your Firebase project" — the exact same effect as clicking "Get Started."** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §17): the `firebase` CLI's own `deploy --only storage` never implements this provisioning call itself (it only manages rules for an ALREADY-linked bucket), which is why it always points at the Console instead of just doing it — a CLI limitation, not an actual API restriction. Confirmed the endpoint by fetching the LIVE discovery doc (`curl https://firebasestorage.googleapis.com/\$discovery/rest?version=v1beta`) rather than guessing at REST paths. **Working recipe, no service-account key needed if the local `gcloud` session already has project access** (`gcloud auth list` / `gcloud projects list` to check): `TOKEN=$(gcloud auth print-access-token)`, then `curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"location": "<region>"}' https://firebasestorage.googleapis.com/v1beta/projects/<project-id>/defaultBucket` — pick `<region>` to match the project's existing Firestore/Cloud Functions region (co-location, avoids cross-region egress/latency), confirmed via `gcloud firestore databases describe` and the functions source's own `setGlobalOptions({region: ...})`, not guessed or defaulted to a US region. **Treat this as a genuinely permanent, real-infrastructure action** (bucket location can't be changed after creation without deleting and recreating it, and it creates a real — if Spark-tier-free — billable GCP resource) — confirm the target region with the user before calling `create`, same standing discipline as any other hard-to-reverse action this project's rules already call for. Once the bucket exists, `firebase deploy --only storage` starts working normally for rules deploys, no different from any other already-provisioned project. **Confirmed idempotent** (re-verified 2026-08-10 during a follow-up review): calling `create` again against an already-provisioned project returns the same `DefaultBucket` result rather than erroring, matching the API's own doc ("If the default bucket already exists, this method will re-link it") — safe to retry if a call times out or the response is ambiguous.
