No `x-goog-user-project` header is needed for this specific endpoint (unlike `firebase.googleapis.com`, which does require one when using local user ADC — irrelevant here since this recipe never calls that API).
- **A service-account key file's `project_id`/`client_email` fields must be checked against the CURRENT project before use, every time — never assume a key found on disk belongs to the project you're currently working in, even when the user hands it to you as the apparent solution to a blocker.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §17): the user pointed at `/home/ari/Documents/Task/new_1/key.json` as a way to unblock Storage provisioning on `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` — reading the key's own `project_id` field first (`python3 -c "import json; print(json.load(open('key.json'))['project_id'])"`, no need to touch the private key material itself) revealed it was actually `icoccha-admin-dashboard`'s service account, the SIBLING project's own credential, not this one's. Generalizes this project's own already-standing "don't assume the two projects share credentials/schema/rules" rule (see "Relationship to icoccha-admin-dashboard" in `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`) to the specific case of a credential file the user themselves hands over mid-task — check it before use regardless of who supplied it or how confident they sound that it's the right one.
- **Confirms the sister project's own "never-commented-out `ensureReplaced` is a SYSTEMIC risk, sweep the whole file whenever you find one instance" finding (line 266 above) applies just as much to a brand-new file as to a long-lived one — `dsl/edit.dart` here had never had a single instance frozen despite 7 already-landed one-shot structural ops accumulating across §9-§14's onboarding/reservation build-out.** Confirmed 2026-08-10, a dedicated full-file audit pass (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §15): swept every `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensurePage` call in the file by reading it top to bottom and grepping for all four constructs, then cross-checking each hit's target key/page against `generated_code/`/the typed SDK to confirm it had actually landed. Found and froze 7 live instances, none previously commented out: `SmsCode` page's `Card_qcaxhffj`→`SmsCodeCard` replace; `Kyc` page's `KycSubmitButton` insert-after (mixed into an `editPage` block with 2 still-live `ensureActions` calls — froze only the one stale op, per the line-252 "don't comment out a whole block for one stale op" rule); `ReservationForm`'s `Container_8is5q6ti`→`ResDateField` replace, `SwitchListTile_8u4clxfz`→`ResGroupInviteCheckbox` replace, and `Row_8aeejqf8`-insert-after→`ResBaseAmountRow` (all three in one `editPage` block, alongside 8 still-live `ensureActions` calls — froze only the three); `ReservationDetail`'s `ReviewInputRow` insert-before; and — the highest-severity find — `ReservationListPage`'s own `app.ensurePage(...)`, which uses raw `State('myReservationsList')`/`SetState(...)` references inside its body and would have hit the EXACT SAME `Use ff.Pages.x.state.y instead of State(...)` failure already confirmed twice this session (BasicInfoRegistration, ConnectOnboarding) on the very next unrelated push — caught here BEFORE it could block a future push, not after. **Takeaway generalized from a sample of two projects now, not one: treat a full `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore`/`ensurePage` sweep as mandatory due diligence before any push to a DSL file that has accumulated several tasks' worth of structural edits without ever having had one frozen — don't wait for a live compile failure to trigger the sweep, since (as this project's own case shows) it can go undetected for several successful pushes if nothing downstream happens to depend on the stale node yet.**

---

## Backup / versioning discipline

Same real safety net the admin-dashboard project confirmed and started using deliberately (see that project's `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`, Client Checklist item 22): **every successful `flutterflow ai run` push produces a distinct, individually-addressable server-side commit**, visible via `flutterflow ai history` and `.flutterflow/runs.jsonl`. `flutterflow ai branch create <name> --from <commit-id>` can resurrect any prior state — a genuine rollback path exists independent of local git.

- **Routine additive changes** (new pages/components/fields, most ordinary DSL work) — push directly to the active branch (`flutterflow ai branch current` if unsure which one). The per-commit history is an adequate safety net.
- **Higher-risk changes** (bulk data mutation/deletion, restructuring an already-live schema field, Firestore rules changes that could lock out a live flow) — create a branch first (`flutterflow ai branch create <name> --checkout`), validate there, then merge back via the documented merge loop (`flutterflow ai docs branches`) rather than pushing straight to the main branch.
- **Local git is not yet scoped to this project directory** (`PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` §1 confirmed `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` resolves to the entire home directory, no commits, unrelated home-directory files show as untracked) — a proper `git init` scoped to the project root is worth doing before any real local commit workflow begins, separate from (and in addition to) the FlutterFlow-side branch/commit safety net above, which works regardless.

---

## Dependencies & tooling

- Respect pinned versions in `pubspec.yaml` — don't bump/replace a dependency incidentally while working on something unrelated.
- No build flavors unless asked — don't add flavor complexity speculatively.
- This project's own spec involves real money/state-machine logic (Stripe Connect, escrow-style capture/transfer, debt offset, affiliate accrual — see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s requirements catalog) — when adding tests, prioritize money/state-machine correctness (payment lifecycle, idempotent webhooks, cancellation/refund math) over cosmetic widget smoke tests, the same priority the admin-dashboard project settled on for the same reason.

---

## Git / delivery

- Only commit when the user asks. Never force-push main/master; never update git config.
- Do not commit `key.json`, Stripe keys, service-account credentials, or other secrets — treat `google-services.json`, `GoogleService-Info.plist`, and any `FirebaseOptions`/`.env` file as sensitive, never pasted into source, commits, or this knowledge base.
- Prefer small, reviewable changes with messages that explain **why**, not just what.

---

## Documentation maintenance

When something new is learned (a new collection/field, a new callable, a renamed route, a security change, a client clarification of an ambiguous requirement, a new FlutterFlow AI SDK quirk):

1. Update the matching section of `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` (append a change-log entry) and, if it changes documented current/planned state, the relevant section of `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` or `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` too.
2. Update this rules file if an operating convention or constraint changed, or add to "New quirks found in this project" above if it's a fresh SDK/tooling discovery.
3. Note the date — every dated claim in this file and in `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` should be traceable to when it was actually confirmed, the same discipline the admin-dashboard project used throughout.

---

## Quick "do / don't"

**Do**

- Read `PROJECT_ANALYSIS.md` + `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` + `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` + this file before implementing anything.
- Prove a change works (via `dart analyze`, `flutterflow ai run`, and re-reading `generated_code/` or a live `inspect` afterward) before reporting it as done — this project's sister workspace lost real time more than once to changes that compiled and pushed clean but didn't actually render/behave as intended; don't repeat that pattern here.
- Disclose uncertainty explicitly when a fix is reasoned-but-unconfirmed, rather than reporting "fixed" before it's verified.
- Keep `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` current — every task gets a change-log entry, including dead ends and retracted theories, not just what worked.
- Treat `generated_code/` as read-only runtime truth for debugging (layout, overflow, render errors) — but see the inherited quirks section for real limits on what that local export does and doesn't reliably mirror (notably: binary media assets).

**Don't**

- Guess at product/business rules not covered by `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — flag the gap instead.
- Assume this project shares a backend, schema, or security rules with `icoccha-admin-dashboard` without checking first.
- Put Stripe/Firebase secrets or credentials in source, markdown, or commits.
- Expand scope beyond what a task actually asked for.
- Report a fix as working based on a clean compile/push alone — confirm the actual rendered/runtime behavior where that's checkable.

## Firebase CLI tooling quirks (not FlutterFlow-specific — applies to any project's backend deploy, including `icoccha-admin-dashboard`)

- **`firebase deploy --only functions:<name>` can fail with `Cannot set CPU on the functions <list> because they are GCF gen 1` even when `<name>` has nothing to do with those listed functions, and even after removing every explicit `cpu` setting from your own `setGlobalOptions(...)`/`onCall(...)` calls.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §30's addendum), root-caused via direct discovery-manifest inspection (spawn `node_modules/.bin/firebase-functions` with `FUNCTIONS_CONTROL_API=true`, query `/__/functions.yaml` — it returns JSON despite the name) plus reading `firebase-tools`' own compiled source: this is a genuine, confirmed `firebase-tools` bug (present unfixed in both 15.23.0 and the latest 15.26.0 at time of writing), NOT something fixable from your own project's code. `deploy/functions/prepare.js`'s `inferDetailsFromExisting()` copies `cpu` from a function's EXISTING LIVE state on GCP onto its "desired" deploy config with no platform check (`if (typeof wantE.cpu === "undefined" && haveE.cpu) { wantE.cpu = haveE.cpu; }`) — GCP's API apparently reports a non-null `cpu`-shaped value even for genuinely gen-1 functions, which then fails a LATER validator (`validate.js`'s `cpuConfigIsValid`) for exactly that reason. A sibling function in the same file, `resolveCpuAndConcurrency`, already has the correct guard (`if (e.platform === "gcfv1") { continue; }`) for a related concern — this one call site is just missing it. **Do NOT chase this by editing your own `setGlobalOptions`/`onCall` options or upgrading `firebase-functions`** (the runtime SDK your code imports) — neither touches the actual bug, which lives in `firebase-tools` (the CLI you invoke), a completely different package. **The fix**: add the same platform guard — `wantE.platform !== "gcfv1" && typeof wantE.cpu === "undefined" && haveE.cpu` — to that one line in the installed `firebase-tools`' `prepare.js`. Two ways to apply it, different scope: (1) session-local, lowest-risk, no `sudo` — `npx --yes firebase-tools@latest --version` to populate `~/.npm/_npx/<hash>/node_modules/firebase-tools`, patch that copy's `prepare.js`, then deploy via `node ~/.npm/_npx/<hash>/node_modules/firebase-tools/lib/bin/firebase.js deploy ...` instead of the bare `firebase` command (the bare command still uses the unpatched system-wide install); (2) system-wide, `sudo`-gated, fixes the bare `firebase deploy` command everywhere on the machine — apply the same `sed`/edit to `/usr/lib/node_modules/firebase-tools/lib/deploy/functions/prepare.js` (or wherever `which firebase` resolves through `node_modules` to). **Before assuming a fresh session's plain `firebase deploy` works, check whether the system-wide patch was ever actually applied** — it requires an interactive `sudo` password prompt no automated session can supply, so it may still be pending even after being identified and approved.

## Firestore security rules — a bug class invisible to every compile-time check (applies to any FlutterFlow AI project with a custom action that queries Firestore directly from the client)

- **A client-side Firestore QUERY (not a single-document `.doc(id).get()`) can be silently rejected by security rules with zero compile-time, `dart analyze`, or `compileDslApp` signal — the custom action just returns whatever its own try/catch falls back to, typically an empty list, and nothing about a clean push or a clean regenerated-code read reveals this.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §33): `fetchDiscoveryCasts` ran `firestore.collection('users').where('account_type', isEqualTo: 'cast').where('approval_status', isEqualTo: 'approved')...get()` directly from the client, against a collection whose rule is `allow read: if request.auth.uid == document` (strict per-document identity match, no content-based OR admin exception). Firestore's rules engine denies a QUERY outright, before touching real data, unless the rule is PROVABLY satisfiable for every possible result without per-document evaluation — an identity-only rule can only be proven for a query constrained to the caller's own document ID; a query filtering on unrelated fields (`account_type`, `approval_status`) can match arbitrarily many OTHER users' documents, so the whole query is denied with `permission-denied`. The action's own defensive try/catch (a pattern applied consistently and correctly everywhere else this session) absorbed this into a silent empty return, meaning the ENTIRE feature built on top of it appeared to work (compiled, pushed, matched every regenerated-code read-through) while being **completely non-functional at runtime** the whole time. **The general rule, before writing ANY client-side Firestore query in a custom action**: check the target collection's actual rule in `firestore.rules`. If the rule is `resource.data.SOME_FIELD == request.auth.uid` (or a `.hasAny([request.auth.uid])` array-membership check) — content-based — a query whose OWN `.where('SOME_FIELD', isEqualTo/arrayContains: uid)` clause matches that exact condition IS provably satisfiable and will be allowed (confirmed working via this same project's `fetchMyReservations`, whose two queries match `reservations`' own `guest_id`/`cast_ids` content-based rule exactly). If the rule is identity-only (`request.auth.uid == document`, no content-based alternative) — as with the `users` collection in this project — **any query filtering on a field other than the document ID itself will be rejected**, no matter how reasonable the query looks in code. The fix in that case is the same one already established for admin-only reads (`getServiceAreas`, `adminGetUsers`, etc.): move the query into a Cloud Function using the Admin SDK, which bypasses security rules entirely, and have the client call that instead of querying directly. **This bug class is specifically dangerous because none of this project's own established verification steps catch it** — not `dart analyze`, not `flutterflow ai test`, not `compileDslApp`, not reading the regenerated Dart source (which shows a perfectly well-formed, correctly-wired query) — it only manifests as a runtime `permission-denied` inside the try/catch, which is why a real device/Test-Mode click-test (a standing, still-unfulfilled gap across this entire session) would be the ONLY verification method that could have caught this directly; absent that, the only reliable check is manually cross-referencing every client-side query's `.where()` clauses against the target collection's actual rule text before trusting the query will work, every time, especially for any "browse/discover/search other users' data" feature — that category is the highest-risk by nature, since it inherently needs to read documents that aren't the caller's own.

## Firestore composite-index coverage — applies to EVERY Firestore query regardless of which SDK issues it, not just Cloud Functions (applies to any project with a query — Cloud Function OR a client-side custom action — combining a filter on one field with a range/`in`/`not-in`/`orderBy` on a different field)

- **This checklist applies identically to client-side custom-action queries, not only server-side Cloud Function queries — confirmed the hard way after nearly missing it.** Phase 7 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §37's review-pass addendum) shipped `fetchCastReviews`, a CLIENT-side custom action running `reviews.where('reviewee_id', isEqualTo: castId).orderBy('created_at', descending: true)` directly against the client Firestore SDK — the exact composite-index-requiring shape this section already documented, but framed (in this section's own original heading) around "Cloud Function query." That framing very nearly let a real instance slip through even with the general principle already known and internalized — the constraint is a Firestore property, identical for Admin SDK and Client SDK alike, and checking it must not be scoped to "only when writing a new Cloud Function." **Before trusting ANY multi-field Firestore query — in a Cloud Function or in a client-side custom action — cross-reference its exact field combination against `firestore.indexes.json`,** full stop, regardless of which SDK/context authored it.

- **Moving a query into a Cloud Function (Admin SDK) bypasses security rules, but does NOT bypass Firestore's composite-index requirement — and a missing index produces the exact same "silent empty result, no user-visible error" symptom as the rules-rejection bug above, because the same defensive try/catch pattern swallows it.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 in `icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing` (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §35), found on a sweep that manually classified every `.where()` chain across `firebase/functions/src/*.ts`: a query using ONLY equality (`==`) filters across any number of fields never needs a composite index (Firestore's automatic single-field indexes cover it via zigzag-merge — this is why `getDiscoveryCasts`, built in §33, is safe despite two `.where('field','==',...)` calls). The moment a query adds a range filter (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`), `in`, `not-in`, or an `orderBy` on a field DIFFERENT from its equality filters, Firestore requires an explicit composite index covering that exact field combination — and if it's missing, the query throws `FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index` at call time, which the codebase's habitual try/catch converts into an empty/no-op return with no trace.
- **`onSchedule` functions are the highest-risk case, because nothing in this project's normal edit/test/push loop ever exercises them.** `dart analyze`, `flutterflow ai test`, and `compileDslApp` only touch the DSL/client side; a scheduled function's query is never invoked by any of those checks, and it has no client call site to even grep for. The ONLY way §35 caught the confirmed case (`autoCancelExpiredAuth`, hourly, missing `reservations` `status`+`created_at` index) was reading `firebase functions:log --only <name>` directly and finding it throwing on every single firing — a genuinely different verification method from everything else this project's discipline has used (source reading, rules-text cross-referencing, deployment-listing diffing), and the first time this session verified a bug against actual observed runtime behavior rather than inferring failure from static analysis. **After adding or changing ANY `onSchedule` function's query, check its live logs after the first natural firing** (or force a firing if the tooling allows) — a clean deploy and a clean `tsc --noEmit` prove nothing about whether the query will actually succeed against live Firestore.
- **Before trusting any multi-field Cloud Function query (scheduled or callable), cross-reference its exact field combination against `firestore.indexes.json`.** A combination that "looks similar" to an existing index is not the same as a covered one — `adminGetUsers`'s `kyc_status`+`created_at` combination IS indexed and safe, but its `approval_status`/`prefecture` combinations are NOT, despite living in the same function and the same conditional-`.where()`-chaining pattern (§35, disclosed-not-fixed since currently unreachable from this project's only call site). When you do need to add a missing index, you can decode Firestore's own error-message `create_composite` URL (`base64.urlsafe_b64decode` the query-string value, inspect the raw protobuf bytes) to confirm your manually-written `firestore.indexes.json` entry matches byte-for-byte what Firestore itself is asking for, rather than trusting field-name/order intuition alone — cheap extra rigor, worth doing for anything touching payments or scheduled financial logic.
- **A composite-index-only deploy (`firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes`) does NOT go through `deploy/functions/prepare.js`**, so the unresolved gen-1/CPU deploy blocker documented above does not apply to index deploys — no patched-CLI workaround needed, the bare `firebase` command works fine for `--only firestore:indexes` even while the functions-deploy blocker remains unpatched system-wide.
- **A near-miss worth remembering separately: don't trust a terminal/tool rendering of a string that "looks empty" without checking raw bytes, especially anywhere near Japanese/CJK content.** During the same §35 sweep, `nickname_prefix + ""` (the correct Firestore prefix-search upper-bound idiom) rendered as `nickname_prefix + ""` in every `Read`/`grep` output, because U+F8FF is a Private-Use-Area character with no visible glyph — it looked exactly like an accidentally-empty string literal. `sed -n '<line>p' file | xxd` confirmed the real bytes (`ef a3 bf`, the correct UTF-8 encoding of U+F8FF) were already there; no bug existed and no fix was made. Any project mixing Unicode sentinel characters (this idiom specifically) with heavy CJK content is a live setup for this exact false-positive — verify raw bytes before editing code that "looks" wrong from a rendered read.


## Two more `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage` freeze-discipline gotchas, found building Phase 6 (chat/notifications) — extends the existing "freeze immediately after landing" rule above with two failure modes that rule alone doesn't cover

- **A consumed `ensureReplaced` key breaks EVERY other reference to that key anywhere else in the script, not just a second `ensureReplaced` call on it.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §36): an earlier, already-landed `page.mutateNode(page.findByKey('ListView_84hpa09g'), (node) { node.clearDatabaseRequest(); })` was explicitly documented as "safe to leave live and rerun on every future push" — a true claim AT THE TIME, since clearing an already-empty field is genuinely idempotent. It broke anyway, the instant a LATER `ensureReplaced` targeted that same key and replaced it (giving the replacement a fresh auto-generated key) — `Bad state: page MacchaChats findByKey("ListView_84hpa09g") found no matches.` **Before running `ensureReplaced` on any widget key, grep the rest of the file for every other reference to that same key** (`mutateNode`, `ensureActions`, another `findByKey`) — every one of them needs to be frozen or retargeted in the SAME pass, not just the `ensureReplaced` call itself. This is a strictly broader rule than "freeze `ensureReplaced` calls after they land" — it's "freeze (or fix) everything that referenced the key you just replaced."
- **`ensurePage`'s create-vs-edit split isn't just about re-running `ensurePage` itself — the ENTIRE body (`onLoad:`/`body:`) gets re-validated against typed-handle rules on every subsequent compile, even though the page-creation half is a no-op.** This project already knew "future edits to an `ensurePage`d page must go through `editPage`" (documented at BasicInfoRegistration/ConnectOnboarding/ReservationListPage/KycReviewPage), but Phase 6 re-hit it on `NotificationsPage`'s very first follow-up push: a bare `SetState('notifFilter', ...)`/`State('visibleNotifications')` inside the ORIGINAL `ensurePage` call — which had compiled and pushed successfully the FIRST time, since the page didn't exist yet to compare against — started failing with `Use ff.Pages.notificationsPage.state.notifFilter instead of SetState("notifFilter", ...)` the moment ANY other part of the same script tried to reference that now-real page (in this case, a separate `Navigate('NotificationsPage')` call elsewhere). **The fix has two parts, both required**: (1) freeze the entire `ensurePage(...)` block (comment it out in full, exactly like `ensureReplaced`) the moment the page exists and any later edit is needed; (2) express that edit through `app.editPage(ff.Pages.<newPage>, (page) => ...)` using `ff.Pages.<newPage>.state.<field>`/`ff.Pages.<newPage>.params.<field>` typed handles throughout — `State('x')`/`SetState('x', ...)`/`Navigate('PageName')` bare-string sugar only works while the page is still being freshly declared in the SAME script, not after. Any local Dart helper function (e.g. a small per-tab widget builder) whose only call site lived inside the now-frozen `ensurePage` body becomes dead code at that point — remove it rather than leave it (caught via `dart analyze`'s `unused_element` warning, not silently ignored).

## `ClearTextField` may fail validation when the TextField and the button that clears it are created in the SAME insertion — not fully root-caused, but reproducible and worth avoiding

- **`ClearTextField('X')` failed with "trying to clear a widget that doesn't exist" for 3 separate new TextFields in Phase 7, despite the name matching the TextField's own `name:` exactly in every case.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §37). The one working precedent in this project (`ClearTextField('ChatMessageInput')`, Phase 6, `MacchaChats`) has a structural difference from all 3 failing instances: the working case's `TextField` was created via one `ensureReplaced` call, and the button that clears it was wired via a SEPARATE `ensureActions` call targeting a different, pre-existing widget. All 3 failing instances had the `TextField` AND the button referencing it created TOGETHER inside the SAME `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensurePage` call — i.e., a same-batch self-reference. This pattern (works when split across two ops, fails when combined in one) held across all 3 instances but was not conclusively proven as the root cause — no way to test the hypothesis in isolation this session. **Practical guidance**: if a newly-inserted TextField needs a `ClearTextField` action from a button created in the SAME insertion/page-creation call, expect it to possibly fail validation; the safe workaround is to drop `ClearTextField` and rely on `SetState(field, '')` alone (the underlying value is still correctly cleared for any subsequent resubmit — the only loss is the input box visually retaining its old text until the user next types into it, a minor UX gap, not a functional one). If a future session has time to isolate the actual cause (e.g., testing whether splitting the same content across two `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureActions` calls avoids it), update this entry with a confirmed root cause and real fix rather than the current workaround.

## Verifying a CLIENT-SIDE Firestore query fix directly, without a live device or Admin SDK credentials

- **`gcloud` is available at `~/google-cloud-sdk/bin/gcloud` (not on PATH by default — add it: `export PATH="$HOME/google-cloud-sdk/bin:$PATH"`) and already authenticated as the project owner** (confirmed 2026-08-11, same account already relied on earlier this session for other `gcloud`-adjacent checks). This unlocks a real verification path for exactly the gap this project's own review discipline has repeatedly flagged as unfillable: confirming whether a CLIENT-SIDE custom-action Firestore query actually works, when there's no server-side Cloud Function log to check (`firebase functions:log` only covers Cloud Functions, not client SDK queries) and no live device/Test-Mode session to click through.
- **Technique**: `gcloud auth print-access-token` gets a bearer token, then `curl` the Firestore REST API's `runQuery` endpoint directly, replicating the EXACT query shape (`where` + `orderBy` + `limit`) the custom action's Dart code issues:
  ```bash
  export PATH="$HOME/google-cloud-sdk/bin:$PATH"
  TOKEN=$(gcloud auth print-access-token)
  curl -s -X POST \
    "https://firestore.googleapis.com/v1/projects/<PROJECT_ID>/databases/(default)/documents:runQuery" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -d '{"structuredQuery": {"from": [{"collectionId": "<collection>"}], "where": {"fieldFilter": {"field": {"fieldPath": "<field>"}, "op": "EQUAL", "value": {"stringValue": "<test-value>"}}}, "orderBy": [{"field": {"fieldPath": "<orderField>"}, "direction": "DESCENDING"}], "limit": 1}}'
  ```
  A clean response (`[{"readTime": "..."}]`, even with zero matching documents) confirms the query — including any composite index it needs — genuinely succeeds against live Firestore. A `FAILED_PRECONDITION`/"requires an index" error in the response confirms the opposite, with the same diagnostic clarity as the `firebase functions:log` checks already used for server-side query bugs (§35).
- **Caveat**: this authenticates as the `gcloud`-logged-in USER account, not as an arbitrary app end-user — it bypasses Firestore security RULES entirely (same as the Admin SDK would), so it can confirm a query's INDEX/composite-index correctness but cannot confirm whether a real end-user's own auth context would pass the RULES check for that same query. Use this for index-shape verification specifically; for rules-satisfiability verification, keep using the existing "read the rule text, check the query's `.where()` clauses against it" method (§33's own established technique) — the two checks are complementary, not substitutes for each other.
- This closes a real, previously-standing gap: confirmed 2026-08-11 that this session had no way to verify a client-side query fix short of a live device session — this technique is a genuine, reusable alternative for the composite-index half of that verification problem specifically.

## Two more mechanical lessons from Phase 8's review pass

- **After fixing an already-live customFunction/customAction via `updateCustomFunction`/`updateCustomAction`, ALSO update the ORIGINAL `app.customFunction`/`app.customAction` declaration's `code:` to match — don't just leave the old payload sitting there with a separate `update*` call appended after it.** `app.customFunction(...)`/`app.customAction(...)` compile to `ensureCustomFunction`/`ensureCustomAction` — create-if-missing, but ALSO idempotency-checked: if the entity already exists AND the script's own declared payload doesn't match what's actually live (e.g., because a LATER `update*` call in the same file already changed it), the `ensure*` call fails outright with `ensureCustomFunction found an existing custom function named "X" with a different payload`. Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §38's review addendum) fixing `formatYen`: added an `updateCustomFunction` call for the real fix, but the ORIGINAL declaration earlier in the file still had the old buggy `code:` — the very next push failed on that mismatch. The `ensure*` helpers are NOT one-shot/freeze-requiring the way `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage` are (they're safe to leave active and rerun forever) — but their payload must always be genuinely idempotent, which means keeping the original declaration's content in sync with whatever the entity's live state actually is, every time you patch it via `update*`.
- **Typed-handle vs. bare-string state references can be MIXED within a single action list, and the rule applies per-field, not to the whole list.** When extending an already-wired `ON_INIT_STATE` chain to also populate a BRAND-NEW state field declared in the SAME script (via `editPageState`'s `state.ensureField(...)` earlier in the same push), the new field has no entry in the LOCAL typed SDK yet (it doesn't exist remotely until this push lands), so `ff.Pages.X.state.newField` fails to compile locally with `undefined_getter` — use bare `State('newField')`/`SetState('newField', ...)` for that ONE field specifically, while every OTHER, already-existing field referenced in the SAME action list still needs its typed handle (`ff.Pages.X.state.existingField`) per the usual post-existence rule. Confirmed working 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §38) — the compiler accepted a `SetState` list mixing 4 typed-handle references and 1 bare-string reference for a field created in that same script, with no error.

## Two more lessons, found fixing a bug in Phase 8's own review-pass fix — refines (doesn't just repeat) the entries directly above

- **Refining the two-part `update*`-then-sync-original lesson above: the two calls can NEVER "meet in the middle" within the SAME push.** Tried updating both the original `app.customFunction`/`app.customAction` declaration AND adding the corresponding `update*` call in ONE push — this fails, because `ensure*`'s idempotency check runs against whatever is live AT THE START of that compile, before the `update*` call later in the same script ever executes. **Correct sequencing, confirmed by re-examining how the original `formatYen` fix actually succeeded**: (1) apply the real change via `update*` ALONE, leaving the original declaration matching whatever's CURRENTLY live (so its own `ensure*` call stays idempotent for THIS push); (2) only in a LATER, SEPARATE push — after confirming the remote state actually changed — sync the original declaration's content to match. Watch for a second-order version of the same issue: if `update*` only touched `code:` (not `description:`), don't ALSO change `description:` when syncing the original later — that mismatches the still-unchanged remote description and fails the same way, one field at a time.
- **`ensureReplaced(target, replacement)` collapses any WRAPPER around the target's slot that isn't itself included in the replacement tree — even when `target` is an INNER widget's key, not the wrapper's.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §38's second review addendum): targeting an inner `ListView`'s own key (which sat inside an `Expanded(...)` from an earlier fix) with a bare `ListView(...)` replacement — assuming the surrounding `Expanded` would survive since it was never itself targeted — silently lost the `Expanded` wrapper entirely, reintroducing an overflow risk that had already been fixed once. **Before running `ensureReplaced` on any widget you know or suspect is wrapped in something (`Expanded`/`Flexible`/`Padding`/etc.), check the regenerated code first to confirm what's actually wrapping it, and include that wrapper in the replacement tree too** — don't assume a wrapper survives just because you didn't name it in `findByKey(...)`. This is a DIFFERENT, riskier operation than the "bare list → wrap it" fixes this session had already done successfully several times (MacchaChats/NotificationsPage/AdminReportReviewPage) — wrapping something that was never wrapped only ever ADDS structure and can't lose anything; replacing an ALREADY-wrapped widget's own inner key can silently DROP structure, and nothing in this DSL's own validation catches it (see the next point).
- **`flutterflow ai trace latest`'s `VALIDATION_LIST_VIEW_SHRINK_WRAP`/R19 check ONLY fires on `shrinkWrap: true` — it has NO check for the opposite misconfiguration (an unbounded list with `shrinkWrap` left at its default `false` and no `Expanded`/`Flexible` ancestor).** A clean `flutterflow ai trace latest` after a push is NOT reassurance that a dynamic list's height is correctly bounded — it only ever tells you when a list is UNNECESSARILY shrink-wrapped, never when a list is DANGEROUSLY un-bounded. The only reliable way to confirm a list's layout safety after ANY `ensureReplaced` touching it (first-time fix or a later edit) is reading the regenerated widget tree directly (`generated_code/lib/.../..._widget.dart`) and confirming the actual parent-child structure — grep for `Expanded`/`Flexible` near the list and read the surrounding lines, don't just check for the absence of a shrinkWrap warning.

## `CopyToClipboard(CustomFunction(...))` compiles and pushes clean but silently does NOT apply — a genuine SDK/codegen gap, not an authoring mistake

- **`page.ensureActions(widget, ON_TAP, [CopyToClipboard(CustomFunction(someFn, args: {...}))])` reports zero errors (clean `dart analyze`, clean `compileDslApp`, no warning naming the widget) and the push succeeds — but the pushed proto silently does not change.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §40): a copy-icon's `onTap` still showed its ORIGINAL action in `generated_code` even after a forced `flutterflow ai codegen refresh`, while every OTHER `ensureActions` call in the exact same push — several using `CallCustomAction.named(...)`, one an `If`/`Snackbar` chain — landed correctly, confirmed the same way (direct `generated_code` read, not just a clean push). This isolates the gap specifically to `CopyToClipboard` wrapping a `CustomFunction(...)` expression as its argument — every reference example of `CopyToClipboard` in this project's `references/` only ever shows a literal string, a `Constant(...)`, or a list-item map-index expression (`task['title']`), never a `CustomFunction`. **Workaround, not a real fix**: write a small custom action that performs the `Clipboard.setData(...)` call itself (reading whatever dynamic value is needed directly in Dart, e.g. `currentUserUid`), and wire it via `CallCustomAction.named(...)` instead of the native `CopyToClipboard` DSL action — the same proven, reliable mechanism already used throughout this project for every other dynamic side effect. **Before trusting ANY push that changes an existing widget's action chain, verify the SPECIFIC widget's `onTap`/trigger content directly in `generated_code`** — a clean compile and a "Modified pages: N" summary in `flutterflow ai trace latest` only confirm SOMETHING in that page changed, not that YOUR specific intended mutation was the thing that changed; for `CopyToClipboard` with anything other than a literal/`Constant` argument specifically, expect this gap and use the custom-action workaround from the start rather than discovering it after a push.

## `onSchedule`'s `timeZone` option controls WHEN Cloud Scheduler fires — it does NOT change what `new Date()` reports inside the function body

- **`onSchedule({ schedule: '...', timeZone: 'Asia/Tokyo' }, handler)` makes Cloud Scheduler evaluate the CRON EXPRESSION in JST wall-clock terms (so `"0 3 * * *"` genuinely fires at 3:00 AM JST) — but the Cloud Functions container itself still runs in UTC, and a bare `new Date()` inside `handler` reflects that UTC runtime clock, not JST.** Confirmed 2026-08-11 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §41, found on the SAME session's own review pass immediately after introducing it): a fix that replaced a hardcoded `"0 3 5 * *"` cron with a daily schedule plus an in-handler `if (new Date().getDate() !== paymentDay) return;` guard (intended to make "pay out on day N" admin-configurable, since cron strings can't read Firestore at deploy time) compiled and deployed with zero errors, but would have SILENTLY NEVER FIRED CORRECTLY: JST is UTC+9, so a 3:00 AM JST trigger executes at 18:00 UTC the PREVIOUS calendar day — `new Date().getDate()` reads that previous UTC day, permanently failing the `=== paymentDay` check, forever, with no error, no log noise, nothing — a total, silent failure of the scheduled function's entire purpose. **The fix**: extract the JST calendar value via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, which is immune to the runtime's own local timezone, instead of any bare `Date` getter:
  ```ts
  const jstDay = Number(
    new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", { timeZone: "Asia/Tokyo", day: "numeric" }).format(new Date())
  );
  if (jstDay !== paymentDay) return;
  ```
- **This is a narrower instance of a WIDER, pre-existing pattern already present throughout this backend before this specific bug was found**: any `new Date().getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`/`getDate()` call used to compute "which calendar day/month is this" (not just inside `onSchedule` handlers) reads UTC, not JST, and will misattribute activity that happens within roughly 9 hours of a JST day/month boundary to the adjacent UTC day/month. The `onSchedule`-plus-day-of-month-equality-check case above is uniquely severe because a SINGLE-DAY-PER-MONTH check has zero tolerance for a 9-hour offset — it either exactly matches or the function never fires all month — whereas a "which month does this reward belong to" computation elsewhere in the codebase only mis-buckets the narrow edge-case hours themselves, not the whole system. **Before writing ANY new date-boundary logic (day-of-month equality checks especially, but also month/week/day bucketing) intended to reflect JST wall-clock time, use `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (or an equivalent explicit-timezone extraction) rather than a bare `Date` getter** — don't assume a Cloud Function's runtime clock matches the timezone your business logic is written for, even when an adjacent `onSchedule`/`timeZone` option makes it LOOK like the whole function is timezone-aware.

## `page.update(widget, (patch) => patch.visible(...)/.text(...))` only accepts LITERAL values — for a dynamic/conditional binding on an EXISTING widget, `ensureReplaced` is the only path

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §47) via compile error, not assumed**: `patch.visible(State('someField'))` fails with `argument_type_not_assignable — The argument type 'State' can't be assigned to the parameter type 'bool'`. This is DIFFERENT from every other documented `EditWidgetPatch` method's behavior implied by the "Design & Quality Rules" section's own general framing — `patch.visible(...)` specifically is fast-lane-shaped (literal-only), not slow-path-shaped (arbitrary expression), even though it's called through the slow-path `page.update(...)` API. **If you need an EXISTING widget's visibility to depend on a dynamic condition (state/param/custom function) rather than a fixed true/false, `patch.visible()` cannot do it — reach for `ensureReplaced` instead**, reconstructing that widget (or, more practically, its containing parent, if gating several sibling widgets at once) with `visible: <expression>` passed as a normal constructor argument, which — unlike the patch method — does accept full expressions. Gating N individual sibling widgets this way is often cheaper as ONE `ensureReplaced` on their shared parent container (rebuilding the whole subtree with each child's own `visible:` set as needed) than N separate `ensureReplaced` calls on each child — confirmed working this way for a 9-widget page-content gate (§47).
- **Confirmed the SAME literal-only restriction applies to `patch.text(...)` too — 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §56), also via compile error**: `patch.text(CustomFunction(someFn, args: {...}))` fails with `The argument type 'CustomFunctionInvocation' can't be assigned to the parameter type 'String'`. Same fix: `ensureReplaced` the `Text` node with a genuinely dynamic `Text(<expression>, ...)` constructor call instead of trying to patch an existing one. Treat every `EditWidgetPatch` method as literal-only until proven otherwise on a case-by-case basis — don't assume the ones you haven't hit yet (`.color`, `.opacity`, `.spacing`, etc.) accept dynamic expressions just because `.text`/`.visible` turned out not to; verify via a real compile attempt (cheap — `flutterflow ai run` validates before pushing) rather than assuming either way.

## `app.ensurePage(...)`'s returned `PageHandle` must be captured in a local variable and used directly — `ff.Pages.<name>` cannot reference a page created earlier in the SAME script run

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §56) via compile error**: `Navigate(ff.Pages.systemInfo)` failed with `Member not found: 'systemInfo'` when `SystemInfo` was declared via `app.ensurePage(...)` earlier in the SAME script but had never been pushed before — `ff.Pages.*` is generated from the typed SDK as of the START of the current run, so a page that doesn't exist on the server yet has no typed handle, no matter where in the script it's declared. This is the same underlying "can't reference something just-created in the same run" family already documented for widgets (`WidgetState`/`findByName` on a same-run-inserted widget, line ~85 above) — now confirmed to apply to `app.ensurePage`'s own return value too, at the PAGE level. **Fix: capture the `PageHandle` `app.ensurePage(...)` returns in a local variable (`final systemInfoPage = app.ensurePage(...)`) and reference THAT directly in any `Navigate(...)` call within the same script run** — it works immediately, no placeholder/two-push dance needed, unlike the widget-level version of this problem. Only `ff.Pages.<name>` (the typed SDK read from disk) is unavailable until the next push regenerates it; the in-memory handle the DSL call itself returns is valid immediately.

## A genuine SDK compiler caching/diffing bug: re-wiring an EXISTING trigger's action chain can silently no-op across MULTIPLE pushes, even with a clean compile and a claimed "Pushed project" result

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §48) — this is a tooling bug, not a scripting mistake, isolated only after 3 structurally different fix attempts all failed the same way.** Re-issuing `page.ensureActions(existingButton, ON_TAP, [...new action chain referencing a different custom action name...])` on a button whose ON_TAP was already wired (from an earlier phase) produced a CLEAN compile and a "Pushed project" success message, but `generated_code` continued to show the OLD action being called — confirmed repeatedly, not a stale-read artifact (file timestamps matched the push). One retry reported the tool's own diagnostic: **`NO_OP: All tasks completed but no project changes were detected. The project proto is identical before and after task execution`** — directly contradicted by the source script, which genuinely declared a different action name and 2 additional arguments. `flutterflow ai refresh-context <project-id>` (re-fetching from the remote server, bypassing any local cache) STILL showed the old action afterward, ruling out "local cache is just stale" as the explanation — the remote project itself had not received the mutation across 2 separate push attempts.
- **What broke the deadlock**: bundling in one additional, entirely unrelated, unambiguous change (a literal Snackbar string tweak) INTO THE SAME action chain, in the SAME push. That push succeeded for real — both the unrelated text change AND the previously-rejected action-chain fix landed together, confirmed directly in `generated_code`. A follow-up push then cleanly reverted just the diagnostic text with no further no-op recurrence, confirming the action-chain fix was now durably applied and the bug was specific to that one earlier state, not a permanent block on the whole page/button.
- **Working theory** (not confirmed against SDK internals, but consistent with every observed symptom): the compiler's own change-diffing mechanism, when re-processing a trigger-chain mutation across multiple pushes, incorrectly treated the intended mutation as already-applied — possibly comparing against an internal representation that doesn't fully capture action-argument-level changes in some circumstances — and skipped re-emitting it as a no-op, even though the remote project's actual stored state never received it. Only forcing a genuinely-different, unambiguous change into the SAME comparison unit (the trigger's action list) was enough to break the false "no-op" determination.
- **Practical rule going forward**: if a trigger-chain edit (`ensureActions`, or any mutation to an EXISTING widget's action list) produces a clean compile and a "Pushed project" success, but `generated_code` doesn't reflect the intended change after checking directly (never skip this check — a clean push proves nothing here, per this project's own long-standing discipline) — do NOT assume the DSL script itself is wrong and start restructuring it (this cost 3 wasted restructuring attempts before the real cause was found). Instead: (1) try `flutterflow ai refresh-context <project-id>` first, to rule out a pure local-cache staleness explanation; (2) if the remote state still doesn't reflect the change, bundle in one small, unmistakable, unrelated edit to the SAME action chain (a literal string change is enough) as a forcing function, push again, and check `generated_code` once more; (3) once confirmed landed, a separate follow-up push can safely revert the diagnostic-only piece of the bundled change without triggering the same issue again.

## `Barcode` widget: `ensureReplaced` reconstruction fails backend validation on its `type:` field, independent of what's bound to `data:` — a confirmed SDK bug, not a scripting mistake

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §50), isolated via 3 separate `flutterflow ai validate` attempts (no push, deliberately — see the "When to use `flutterflow ai validate`" section above for why this was the right call here: an offline preview of a change not yet safe to commit).** The `Barcode` widget's own constructor (`widgets.dart`) legitimately takes `data:` as an arbitrary `DslExpression` via `normalizeExpression` — the SAME mechanism every other dynamic-bindable widget property in this DSL uses — so binding `data: State(...)`/`CustomFunction(...)` is NOT categorically unsupported; a much earlier project pass had concluded otherwise from `docs`/`references` alone (neither documents a `Barcode` example) without ever checking the constructor source directly, and that conclusion was wrong.
- **What actually fails**: reconstructing an EXISTING `Barcode` widget via `ensureReplaced` reliably fails FlutterFlow's own backend validation with `Barcode Type in Barcode Widget is not properly set` — confirmed to reproduce on THREE structurally different attempts: (1) dynamic `data:` (component-local state) + explicit `type: BarcodeKind.qrCode`, (2) a fully literal string `data:` + the same explicit `type:`, (3) the same literal string with `type:` omitted entirely (relying on the constructor's own `BarcodeKind.qrCode` default). All three fail identically. This rules out the dynamic binding, the specific enum value, and whether `type:` is passed explicitly vs. defaulted — none of those variables change the outcome, so the bug is in how `ensureReplaced` serializes/sends a Barcode's `type` field to the backend validator, not in anything script-side that's fixable by varying the call.
- **A SEPARATE, unrelated error can appear alongside this one and must not be conflated with it**: attempt (1) above also failed with `Widget class state field not found` — but that error disappeared completely once state was removed (attempts 2/3), isolating it as being about the specific mechanism used to populate that state (a component-ROOT `ON_INIT_STATE` trigger — every other `ON_INIT_STATE` use across this entire project is on a PAGE root; component-root support for this trigger type is unconfirmed and should be treated as suspect, not assumed to work like a page's).
- **Practical rule going forward**: do not spend more than one or two attempts trying to work around `Barcode Type in Barcode Widget is not properly set` by varying the `data:`/`type:` arguments — it has already been isolated as independent of both. If a `Barcode` widget genuinely needs dynamic content, try reconstructing its PARENT container instead of the `Barcode` node directly (the same structural workaround already proven elsewhere in this project for other stuck `ensureReplaced` cases), or treat it as a confirmed SDK gap to report rather than route around.

## `ensureInsertedAfter` is ALSO one-shot/idempotent-unsafe, same category as `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage` — re-running it with different content on an already-inserted target is silently ignored, not an error

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §51).** A button inserted via `page.ensureInsertedAfter(anchor, Row(... Button(...) ...))` landed correctly in an earlier push. A LATER push re-issued the exact same `ensureInsertedAfter` call, only adding styling args (`width`/`height`/`color`/`textColor`/`borderRadius`) to the same `Button`. This compiled and pushed with **zero error** — but `generated_code` showed the button completely unstyled afterward, exactly as before the "fix". Unlike `ensureCustomAction` (which throws a loud, explicit "different payload" error on mismatch — see below), `ensureInsertedAfter` fails SILENTLY: no warning, no error, just a no-op once the target already exists by name/position.
- **The existing "freeze `ensureReplaced`/`ensurePage` immediately after they land" discipline applies identically to `ensureInsertedAfter`** — extend that rule rather than treating this as a new, separate one. **The fix, once already bitten**: freeze the original `ensureInsertedAfter` call (comment it out), then use `ensureReplaced` directly on the newly-created widget's OWN real key (read from the regenerated typed SDK, e.g. `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/<page>.dart`) for any further changes to it — a genuinely first-time structural operation on that specific node, which applies normally.

## `ensureCustomAction`/`app.customAction(...)` re-declared to "sync" with an already-`updateCustomAction`-mutated live action can keep throwing a false mismatch — stop trying to sync it, comment the declaration out instead

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §51), root cause NOT isolated — practical workaround confirmed working.** The established two-part sequencing rule for updating an already-live custom action/function (`update*` alone first, sync the original `app.customAction(...)`/`app.customFunction(...)` declaration's content in a LATER, separate push) assumes step 2 is straightforward once the `update*` call has landed. It was NOT, here: after confirming via `generated_code` that `updateCustomAction`'s new code was genuinely live, editing the original `app.customAction('callCreateExtensionPayment', ...)` declaration's `code:`/`description:` to match that exact live content STILL threw `ensureCustomAction found an existing custom action named "..." with a different payload` on the very next push — twice, across two separate attempts, with content that read as byte-identical to what `generated_code` showed as live (confirmed via `cat -A` for stray whitespace — none found).
- **What actually worked**: stop trying to make the live `app.customAction(...)` declaration match — **comment it out entirely**, leaving only a short reference comment describing the current real signature. This matches the pattern this file ALREADY used successfully, pre-existing, for several other post-`updateCustomAction` targets (`checkReservationFieldsComplete`, `fetchMyReservations`, `reservationListItemLabel`) — evidently the correct, general form of "part 2" of the sequencing rule is not "edit the declaration to match," it's "remove the live declaration from the script's execution path once `updateCustomAction` has taken over maintaining that action," keeping only a comment for human audit-trail purposes. A live `app.customAction()` call for an already-`update*`-mutated name should be treated as unsafe to keep re-asserting indefinitely, not just something to eventually resync.
- **Practical rule going forward**: the first time you `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction` an existing entity, don't plan on ever syncing its original `app.customAction(...)`/`app.customFunction(...)` declaration back to matching content in a later push — comment that declaration out (with a short "see `update*` below" note) in the SAME later push where you'd otherwise have tried to sync it. Only reach for actually editing the declaration's content if this comment-out approach is somehow unavailable.

## "Zero references anywhere in the file" is a claim about the DSL script's text, not about the live project — grep `generated_code/` before removing anything as dead code, don't trust a DSL-only grep

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §55), caught by the compiler itself before any damage was done.** A prior audit pass (§54) grepped `dsl/edit.dart` for 3 custom functions' names, found each appeared exactly once (its own declaration), and reported them as dead code with "zero live references anywhere in the file." Acting on that, `app.removeCustomFunction(...)` was queued for all three in the next push. `flutterflow ai run` rejected it outright — `Validation: Custom function not found` plus `Row '...' — Custom function not found` for three specific widget rows — because all three functions were genuinely gating live `visible:` conditions on `ReservationDetail`'s buttons, wired through an earlier `ensureReplaced`-class block that had ALREADY been frozen (commented out) in a prior round. The widget that calls them still exists and still works on the live server; only the DSL source that ORIGINALLY built it no longer mentions the function by name, because freezing a block removes it from the script's text without undoing what it already built.
- **The generalizable trap**: this project's own well-established freeze discipline (comment out `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter`/etc. once landed) actively creates this exact blind spot — every frozen block is, by definition, a place where the live project still depends on something the CURRENT script text no longer names. A same-file grep for "does anything call this function" will always return false for such cases, correctly describing the script but incorrectly implying the project.
- **Rule**: before treating any custom function/action/widget as dead code and removing it, grep `generated_code/` for its name (e.g. `grep -rln '<name>' generated_code/`), not just `dsl/edit.dart`. If it appears in a `generated_code/lib/**/*.dart` widget file, it is live and must not be removed, regardless of what the DSL script's own text shows. Reserve `app.removeCustomFunction`/`removeCustomAction`/etc. for names confirmed absent from BOTH the script text AND `generated_code/` — and even then, `flutterflow ai run`'s own validation is the final backstop (as it was here): a rejected removal costs one failed, unpushed run; a removal that somehow validated but broke a live screen would be far more expensive to diagnose after the fact.

## A nested widget's own DSL `name:` label is NOT reliably preserved as a literal string in compiled `generated_code/` — verify a frozen block landed by grepping for its CONTENT, not its name

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §58), a near-miss caught before being reported as a false regression.** After freezing an already-landed `ensureInsertedAfter` block (e.g. `CastReviewsAndSafetySection`, a `Container` wrapping a review list + report/block UI), verification via `grep -rn "CastReviewsAndSafetySection" generated_code/` found ZERO hits, briefly looking like the freeze had somehow deleted the section. It hadn't — grepping for actual functional content instead (`fetchCastReviews`, `callReportUser`, `callBlockUser`, the literal Japanese button text) found all of it present and correctly wired. The widget's own `name:` value IS reliably preserved in the TYPED SDK (`lib/flutterflow_project/pages/*.dart`, which literally serializes `name: "CastReviewsAndSafetySection"` as metadata) but is NOT reliably emitted as a literal string anywhere in the compiled Flutter `generated_code/` for a NESTED widget — only page-level Dart CLASS names (e.g. `SystemInfoWidget`, `BlockListWidget`, generated from a whole PAGE's own name) are guaranteed to appear literally in `generated_code/`.
- **Rule**: to confirm a piece of DSL-authored content landed, grep `generated_code/` for something the widget actually DOES or DISPLAYS (a custom action/function it calls, a literal string it renders, a Cloud Function name) — never for the DSL's own internal `name:` label unless that label IS the page's own top-level name. To confirm a WIDGET's structural presence/key (not just "did the content land"), read the TYPED SDK (`lib/flutterflow_project/pages/<slug>.dart`) instead, which does reliably preserve `name:`.

## `setInitialPage`/`project_helpers.dart`'s non-exported helpers are NOT part of the public DSL surface — for the app's home/initial-page route specifically, use `project.ensureAuthPageInfo().homePageNodeKeyRef` via `app.raw(...)` instead

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §58) via compile error, not assumed.** `setInitialPage(project, pageName: 'HomePage')` (a real, working function in the SDK's `src/helpers/project_helpers.dart`, used internally by the pipeline's own `pruneDefaultPlaceholderHomePage`) is NOT reachable from a DSL script — `flutterflow_ai.dart`'s public barrel exports that file `show findPage, findComponent` only, deliberately excluding `setInitialPage`/`getInitialPage`. Calling it anyway compiled fine under `flutterflow ai test` but failed at `flutterflow ai run`'s `compileDslApp` step with `Method not found: 'setInitialPage'` — confirming `flutterflow ai test` does NOT catch every symbol-resolution error `compileDslApp` does; only a real `run` (or `validate`) attempt is the authoritative compile check.
- **What actually controls the app's cold-launch home route** (confirmed by reading `nav.dart`'s generated `loggedIn ? X : Y` ternary and tracing it back through the compiler): `FirebaseAuthDeclaration.homePageName`/`signInPageName`, wired via `configureFirebaseAuth` (`src/helpers/auth_helpers.dart` — also not in the public barrel). That function requires the CURRENT `providers` list as a required param to call at all, and no tool available this session could discover the live provider list without risking passing the wrong one (which would silently disable a real auth provider). **The safe, surgical fix**: `app.raw((project) { project.ensureAuthPageInfo().homePageNodeKeyRef = FFNodeKeyReference(key: findPage(project, name: 'HomePage')!.node.key); })` — touches ONLY the home-page pointer, leaves providers/signInPageNodeKeyRef/the `active` flag completely untouched. `findPage` and `FFNodeKeyReference` are both already part of the public export surface, no extra import needed.
- **General lesson**: when a helper function exists in the SDK's `src/helpers/` but ISN'T reachable via the standard `package:flutterflow_ai/flutterflow_ai.dart` import, don't assume it's simply an oversight to import around — check whether a narrower, already-exported primitive can achieve the same specific field change without needing the broader (and often riskier, since it usually requires reconstructing an entire config from scratch) helper function.

## `Expanded(...)`'s child is a POSITIONAL argument, not a named `child:` parameter — same for any DSL widget, don't assume Flutter's own named-parameter shape carries over

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §58) via compile error.** `Expanded(child: Text(...))` fails with `Too few positional arguments: 1 required, 0 given` — this DSL's `Expanded` constructor is `Expanded(this.child, {this.flex = 1, ...})`, i.e. `child` is positional (`Expanded(Text(...))`), unlike Flutter's own `Expanded` widget where `child:` is named. Caught by `flutterflow ai run`'s validation before anything pushed — no live impact, but a reminder that this DSL's own widget constructors don't always mirror Flutter's exact parameter shape even when the widget name and purpose match exactly (see CLAUDE.md's own "DSL ↔ Flutter drift" table for other known cases — this one wasn't in it before now). When a widget compiles clean in isolation but throws on a specific parameter, check the actual DSL constructor signature in `.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/src/dsl/widgets.dart` rather than assuming Flutter's own API shape applies.

## Each widget's action-chain `outputAs` name must be unique ACROSS THE WHOLE PAGE, not just within one action chain

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §65) via compile error.** Building the cast work-calendar's week/day selector (11 similar `Button`s, each with its own independent onTap action chain), the first attempt reused the exact same `outputAs` string (`'scheduleFetchResult'`) across all 11 buttons — since each button's chain is logically independent (only one runs per tap), this seemed safe. `flutterflow ai run` rejected it: `Action in Button has an output variable with the same name as that of another widget.`
- **Rule**: when writing a reusable Dart function that generates an action chain with a `CallCustomAction(..., outputAs: 'x')` and that function gets called at multiple different widget call sites (e.g. a loop generating N buttons), parameterize the `outputAs` name with something unique per call site (an index, a tag) — e.g. `outputAs: 'scheduleFetchResult_week$w'`. Don't assume chain-local independence is enough; FlutterFlow validates output-variable names as unique per PAGE.

## `patch.dropdownOptions([...])` silently renders every option as an empty string — use raw `mutateNode` setting BOTH `serializedValue` and `translatableText` instead

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §65) via `generated_code`, not assumed from a clean push.** `page.update(dropdownKey, (patch) => patch.dropdownOptions([...48 label strings...]))` compiled and pushed with zero error, but `generated_code` showed `options: ['', '', ...]` — every one of the 48 labels rendered as an empty string. Root-caused by reading `_applyDropdownOptionsPatch`'s own source (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/src/dsl/edit.dart:8279-8297`): it only ever sets `FFParameterValue.serializedValue`, never `.translatableText`. The DropDown's own sibling patch, `_applyDropdownInitialOptionPatch` (for the single default-selected value, right below it in the same file), uses `FFText(textValue: ...)` instead — strong evidence codegen actually reads `translatableText` for option labels, not `serializedValue`. Same class of gap as `ConditionalBuilder`/`ensureEmptyState`'s inverted condition (both documented elsewhere in this file) — a typed SDK patch method whose write doesn't match what codegen actually reads.
- **Working fix**: don't use `patch.dropdownOptions(...)`. Use the raw `page.mutateNode(...)` escape hatch instead, constructing the options list manually and setting BOTH fields on each entry:
  ```dart
  page.mutateNode(page.findByKey(dropdownKey), (node) {
    final labels = FFFormOptionsValue();
    final values = FFFormOptionsValue();
    for (final label in myLabels) {
      final entry = FFParameterValue(
        serializedValue: label,
        translatableText: FFText(textValue: FFStringValue(inputValue: label)),
      );
      labels.values.add(entry);
      values.values.add(entry); // codegen mirrors labels/values when equal — see _applyDropdownOptionsPatch's own comment
    }
    node.props.dropDown.optionsLabels = labels;
    node.props.dropDown.optionsValues = values;
  });
  ```
  Verified: after this fix, `generated_code` showed the correct literal strings for all 48 options.
- **Rule**: don't trust `flutterflow ai run` succeeding as proof a `dropdownOptions` (or any list-of-labels-style) patch actually landed correct content — check `generated_code` for the literal values, not just presence of the field.

## A same-type `ensureReplaced` on a STALE key can succeed silently without applying the new widget's property values — "it compiled and pushed" is not proof a change landed

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §64).** Fixing a regressed `childAspectRatio` value on `HomePage`'s `DiscoveryCastGrid`, `page.ensureReplaced(page.findByKey('GridView_8292ke7j'), GridView(..., childAspectRatio: 1.05, ...))` compiled and pushed with ZERO error — but `generated_code` still showed the OLD value (`0.75`) afterward. The target key (`GridView_8292ke7j`) turned out to already be stale (superseded by an earlier push in the same review round). Re-running the identical call with the CURRENT correct key fixed it immediately.
- **This is different from the already-documented stale-key failure mode.** A stale key on a DIFFERENT-type replacement (e.g. `ListView`→`ListView` in `KycReviewPage`, same review round) throws a loud `Bad state: ... findByKey(...) found no matches` and blocks the push entirely — safe in the sense that you can't miss it. A stale key on a SAME-type replacement (`GridView`→`GridView` here) can instead resolve to something (possibly the same type of node reached via a different path, or some fallback matching behavior not fully understood) and silently apply nothing — no error, no clear signal, just an unchanged live value.
- **Rule**: after ANY `ensureReplaced`/property-changing push, verify the SPECIFIC value in `generated_code` directly — don't treat a clean `flutterflow ai run` as proof the change took effect, especially for same-type replacements. If a value doesn't show up as expected, suspect a stale key before suspecting the DSL syntax — re-check the CURRENT key via `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/<page>.dart` (not the key you last remembered) and retry.
- **Reconfirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §65), third occurrence — and it's non-destructive.** `CastProfile`'s work-calendar `ensureReplaced` (`Column`→`Column`) was left unfrozen for one extra push (a later, unrelated `ReservationForm` dropdown fix) and re-executed against its by-then-stale key. It did not error, and — deliberately checked afterward — did NOT wipe or corrupt the existing correct content either; `generated_code` still showed the right `GridView`/wiring, unchanged. So this failure mode is a silent NO-OP, not silent data loss: whatever the first successful run produced stays intact on a stale-key same-type re-run. Doesn't reduce the need to freeze promptly (a stale-key re-run still can't apply any NEW change), but it does mean discovering one late isn't a live-content emergency.

## `ConditionalBuilder` is broken — fails server-side validation no matter how it's used; use a `Stack` with per-child `visible:` conditions instead

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §63) via `flutterflow ai run`'s own validation, not assumed.** `ConditionalBuilder(children: [Widget(..., visible: cond), Widget(..., visible: Not(cond))])` — the natural reading of its constructor signature (`ConditionalBuilder({List<DslWidget>? children, String? name, Object? visible})`) — fails validation on every use with `Every condition in the conditional builder must have a child`, even though each child clearly has both a real widget and a `visible:` condition attached. This is caught at `flutterflow ai run`'s validation stage — no partial/broken push happens, but it blocks the run entirely.
- `FFConditionalBuilder`'s own proto message carries ZERO fields (confirmed by reading the generated `.pb.dart` class directly — no fields, `hasRequiredFields = false`). No working example of this widget exists anywhere in this project's DSL history, the `references/` set, or `flutterflow ai docs` output. Treat it as an unproven/broken DSL surface, not something to iterate against.
- **Working alternative**: use a plain `Stack` (or whatever plain container fits the layout — `Row`/`Column` also work) holding the same children, each still carrying its own opposite `visible:` condition. This is the SAME underlying conditional-visibility mechanism (confirmed working via the frozen `Divider(visible: Not(Equals(...)))` pattern and `ensureEmptyState`'s `bindVisible`) — `ConditionalBuilder` adds no capability over it, and is simply broken as a wrapper. Since only one child is ever visible at a time (opposite conditions), a plain `Stack` produces the identical visual result.
- **Rule**: don't reach for `ConditionalBuilder` in this project. For "show A or show B," give both widgets opposite `visible:` expressions and place them as siblings in whatever container already fits (`Stack` for overlapping/same-position content, `Row`/`Column` otherwise).

## `CustomFunctionHandle(name:, args:, returnType:)` can be constructed directly to reference an already-declared custom function that wasn't captured into a variable

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §63).** Several `app.customFunction(...)` calls in this file don't capture their return value (`app.customFunction('x', ...)` instead of `final xFn = app.customFunction('x', ...)`), leaving no handle to pass to `CustomFunction(handle, args: {...})` later. Re-calling `app.customFunction('x', ...)` again to get a handle throws `CustomCodeDuplicateError` (or `_ensureUnique`'s equivalent) since the name is already declared.
- **Fix**: `CustomFunctionHandle`'s constructor (`{required name, required args, required returnType}`) is public and its `==`/`hashCode` are based on `name` alone — construct one directly with the SAME name/args/returnType as the original declaration (found by reading that `app.customFunction(...)` call's own `args:`/`returns:` params), without going through `app.customFunction` again. This produces a handle equivalent to the "real" one for `CustomFunction(...)` invocation purposes, with no duplicate-declaration risk.
- **Practical habit**: when declaring a custom function, capture it (`final xFn = app.customFunction(...)`) if there's any chance a LATER, separate edit might need to call it — cheap to do up front, saves reconstructing a handle later like this.

## `app.ensureEmptyState(...)`'s `visibleWhen` is inverted from its own docstring — pass `Not(...)` of what the docs say

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §62), via `generated_code` after a push, not assumed from source reading alone.** The docstring on `ensureEmptyState` reads: "The `content` target remains visible when `visibleWhen` is true; the new `emptyState` is shown for the inverse condition." The actual implementation does the opposite — `bindVisible(emptyState, visibleWhen)` then `bindVisible(content, Not(visibleWhen))`, i.e. **emptyState gets the condition directly, content gets the negation**.
- Passing `visibleWhen: State('hasMatches')` (intending "show real content when true, empty state when false," per the docstring) produced the exact opposite live: the empty-state graphic rendered for users who HAD data, and the real content only rendered for users with none. Caught by reading `generated_code`'s actual `if (...)` conditions after the push — not by reasoning about the source, which reads as if the docstring is correct.
- **Rule**: when calling `ensureEmptyState`, pass `Not(<your actual "has data" condition>)` as `visibleWhen`, not the condition directly — e.g. `visibleWhen: Not(State('hasItems'))`. After any `ensureEmptyState` push, verify the `if` conditions in `generated_code` match intent before considering it done; do not trust the docstring's stated contract for this specific helper.

## Static `patch.visible(false)` on a `Text` nested in a `Stack` silently no-ops — neither the typed patch nor the raw proto escape hatch actually hides it; blank the text content instead

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §61), via two independent mechanisms, not one attempt.** WorkPage had 4 `CircleImage` slots each with a redundant placeholder `Text` ("募集画像表示") overlaid in the same `Stack`, meant to disappear once a real image was wired. `page.update(selection, (patch) { patch.visible(false); })` compiled and pushed with zero error, but `generated_code` showed the `Text` still rendered completely unconditionally afterward, and `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --page <p> --selector-key <k> --dsl-json` showed no `visibility` field on the node at all. Suspecting the typed patch path specifically, tried the raw `page.mutateNode` escape hatch instead, writing the EXACT same proto field `widget_helpers.dart`'s own `setVisibility` helper writes internally (`node.props.ensureVisibility().visibleValue = FFBooleanValue(inputValue: false)`). Same result: compiled, pushed, zero error, zero effect on `generated_code`, zero effect on `inspect --dsl-json`.
- **This rules out a scripting mistake** — two structurally different write paths (typed patch, raw proto mutation) produced an identical silent no-op, which points at a codegen/backend gap specific to STATIC (non-dynamic, non-`FFVariable`-bound) visibility on a `Text` nested inside a `Stack`, not at anything fixable by varying the DSL call.
- **Working fix**: don't fight the visibility field — blank the text CONTENT instead (`patch.text('')`), which is already proven reliable elsewhere in this file and achieves the identical end-user-visible outcome (no leftover label) without depending on the broken mechanism.
- **Practical rule going forward**: if a static `patch.visible(false)`/raw `ensureVisibility()` write on a `Text` (especially one nested in a `Stack` alongside an `Image`/`CircleImage`) doesn't show up in `generated_code` after one push, don't spend a 3rd attempt on visibility-specific variants — switch to blanking the content (`.text('')` for `Text`, or the equivalent no-content state for other widget types) as the pragmatic workaround, and verify via `generated_code` same as any other patch.

## No binary-asset-upload capability exists anywhere in this session's toolset — `customFont`/`customIconFamily` only write a filepath STRING, they never transfer bytes

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §60), exhaustively checked rather than assumed.** When a client delivers actual asset files (fonts, SVGs, images) to apply to a live FlutterFlow project, there is currently no code path in this workspace's CLI, SDK, or MCP surface that reads a local file's bytes and uploads it into the project. Checked and ruled out: the full `flutterflow ai --help` command list; every MCP tool via `ToolSearch` (only Figma's `upload_assets` exists, unrelated to FlutterFlow projects); `app.customFont`/`app.customIconFamily`'s actual implementation (`theme_helpers.dart`'s `addCustomFont`/`addCustomIconFamily`) — reading the source directly confirms these only set a `fontFilePath`/`iconFilePath` STRING field on the project proto, exactly like any other metadata field, with no upload step attached; and `live.status` (FlutterFlow Desktop pairing), which is the one path that COULD plausibly bridge to a human-driven upload but requires Desktop running and paired on this machine (`paired: false` this session).
- **Rule**: when asked to "apply"/"publish"/"upload" a delivered asset FILE (not just register its path against an already-uploaded file), don't attempt a DSL-only solution — confirm first whether the file's bytes are already present in the live project (grep `generated_code/lib/flutter_flow/custom_icons.dart` for font/icon registrations already using that exact family name; check `generated_code/assets/` — though note this export snapshot is often empty regardless of real upload status, so an empty local `assets/` folder is NOT proof the file is missing server-side, only inconclusive). If genuinely absent, the correct path is a human-driven upload via the FlutterFlow web/desktop builder — produce a precise manifest (source file → target filename → target page/widget, flagging any file-to-slot ambiguity honestly rather than guessing) and wire the DSL side only after the human confirms the upload landed.
- **Adjacent gap, same investigation**: no SVG-rendering tool (`rsvg-convert`/`inkscape`/`cairosvg`/`convert`) is installed on this machine.
