When multiple delivered SVGs must be matched 1:1 to visually-identical/textually-identical DSL placeholder slots (e.g. 4 illustration buttons → 4 placeholders with the same label), this session cannot resolve the mapping by content inspection — disclose the ambiguity and let the human assign by eye during upload, rather than guessing a file order.

## This DSL has NO `And`/`Or` boolean combinator — only `Not` — multi-condition validation must use nested `If`s

- **Confirmed 2026-08-12 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §59), caught by checking the SDK source before running anything, not by a compile failure.** Writing a submit button that needed to validate two required fields, the natural-looking `Not(Equals(State('a'), '')).and(Not(Equals(State('b'), '')))` was drafted first — `.and(...)` doesn't exist anywhere in this SDK. Grepping `src/dsl/references.dart` (where `Not`/`Equals` live) confirms only `Not` is defined — no `And` class, no `Or` class, no boolean-combining method on `DslExpression` at all.
- **Fix**: express AND-style multi-condition validation as nested `If`s instead of a single combined condition — `If(condA, then: [If(condB, then: [...], orElse: [...]), ...], orElse: [...])` — matching the pattern this file's own multi-step validation chains already use elsewhere (e.g. KYC field-completeness checks, reservation field validation). There is no single-expression way to combine two boolean conditions in this DSL; don't look for one.

## `page.bindValue(...)` is a SILENT NO-OP for `DropDown` widgets — the SDK's own no-op-detection optimization has a per-widget-type blind spot

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §67), via `generated_code` AND a follow-up `flutterflow ai inspect --tree`, not assumed from a clean push.** `page.bindValue(dropdownSelection, CustomFunction(someFn, args: {...}))` — the public, typed API `bindValue` explicitly documents as supported for `DropDown` (alongside `Switch`/`Checkbox`) — compiled and pushed with **zero error**, but `generated_code` showed the dropdown's initial-value expression completely unchanged from before the call, and a direct `flutterflow ai inspect <id> --page <p> --selector-key <k> --tree` confirmed the SERVER-SIDE proto's `dropDown.initialOption` field was ALSO unchanged — not just a codegen blind spot (the earlier-documented `component.root`/`dropdownOptions` cases both wrote correctly server-side but codegen ignored the write), the write never even reached the real node.
- **Root cause, found by reading the SDK source, not guessed:** `_BindStringValueOp.apply` (`edit.dart:5248`) calls `_nodeSemanticallyEqualsAfterMutation` first — this mutates a THROWAWAY COPY of the target node, then compares `buildDslNodeSnapshot(project, original)` against `buildDslNodeSnapshot(project, mutatedCopy)`; if the two JSON-encode identically, the function returns `true` and `apply()` returns EARLY, **skipping the real mutation entirely** as a (correct-in-intent) no-op optimization. But `_snapshotWidgetDetails`'s per-widget-type `switch (node.type)` (`snapshot.dart:434-449`) only has cases for `Container/Card/Column/Row/Stack/Text/TextField/Button/Icon/IconButton/ListView/ProgressBar/Switch/Divider/Spacer` — **`DropDown` has no case, falls through to the default `=> const <String, dynamic>{}`** — so NONE of a DropDown's props (`initialOption`, `optionsLabels`, `optionsValues`, anything) are visible to the snapshot. Every mutation to a DropDown's props therefore looks "semantically equal" to no mutation at all, and the real write is silently discarded, no matter what the mutation actually changes.
- **Scope of the bug**: applies to `bindValue` on `DropDown` specifically (confirmed). `Checkbox` is also absent from the same switch and is likely equally affected for `_applyBoolValueBinding`, though not independently confirmed this session — treat any `bindValue`/`bindVisible`/`bindText` call targeting a widget type NOT in the `_snapshotWidgetDetails` switch list above as suspect until verified in `generated_code`. `Switch` IS in the list (confirmed safe). `bindVisible` itself is safe for ALL widget types since `visibility` is captured in `_snapshotCommonWidgetDetails`, which runs unconditionally before the per-type switch (independently reconfirmed working in this same round, §67).
- **Fix / workaround**: bypass `bindValue`'s operation pipeline entirely for `DropDown`. Compile the same DSL expression with the lower-level but still-PUBLIC `compileDslStringValueForExistingWidgetClass(project, {required widgetClassName, required targetNodeKey, required expression, App? app})` (`compiler.dart:1332`), then write the result directly onto the real node found via the public `findPage(project, name: ...)` + `findByKey(widgetClass.node, key)`, inside `app.raw((project) { ... })`:
  ```dart
  app.raw((project) {
    final widgetClass = findPage(project, name: 'PageName')!;
    final targetNode = findByKey(widgetClass.node, 'DropDown_xxx')!;
    final compiledValue = compileDslStringValueForExistingWidgetClass(
      project,
      widgetClassName: 'PageName',
      targetNodeKey: 'DropDown_xxx',
      expression: CustomFunction(someFn, args: {'x': PageParam('x')}),
      app: app,
    );
    targetNode.props.dropDown.initialOption = FFText(textValue: compiledValue);
  });
  ```
  This is a plain property overwrite (like the `dropdownOptions` raw-patch fix above) — safe to leave live/unfrozen, re-asserts the same value on every rerun.

## A widget's dynamic initial value bound to a PAGE PARAM sidesteps the `??=`-captured-before-load timing race entirely — prefer it over binding to page STATE whenever the value ultimately traces back to a param

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §67).** The established fix for the `??=`-captured-before-load bug class (documented earlier in this file — gate the widget's own `visible:` behind an `isConfigLoaded`-style flag) assumes the correct value only becomes available ASYNCHRONOUSLY (e.g. from a Firestore read in `ON_INIT_STATE`). When the correct value is actually a PAGE/COMPONENT PARAM the whole time (e.g. a deep-link target passed straight through `Navigate(..., params: {...})`), that assumption doesn't hold — a page param is a constructor-level field (`widget!.paramName` in generated Dart), available SYNCHRONOUSLY from the very first frame, with no load step at all. Binding the widget's initial value directly to the param (instead of to a page-state field that gets SET FROM the param inside an async `ON_INIT_STATE` action) sidesteps the entire race by construction — correct regardless of whether codegen ends up evaluating the binding in `initState()` (confirmed for `TextField`: `TextEditingController(text: widget!.prefillDate)`) or lazily in `build()` via `??=` (confirmed for `DropDown`: `_model.xValue ??= functions.someFn(widget!.prefillParam)`) — and needs no new page-state flag or visibility-gating, avoiding both the added complexity and the brief flash-of-empty-form the gate approach introduces.
- **When the widget needs a DIFFERENT value when the param is absent** (e.g. a dropdown that must keep showing its existing hardcoded default when there's no deep link, not go blank) — bind to a small custom function that returns the param when non-empty, else the literal fallback (matching whatever the corresponding state field's own default already is, so the two can't silently diverge): `CustomFunction(fn, args: {'x': PageParam('x')})` where `fn(x) => x.isNotEmpty ? x : '<same literal as the state field's own withDefault(...)>'`. Do NOT read the state field itself as the fallback (`State('resStartTime')` as a fallback source) — on the very first build the state field may ITSELF still hold its own default rather than a value set by an as-yet-unresolved async load, which happens to be harmless only by coincidence (default happens to match); reason from the literal, not from a state field that might have its own timing story.
- **Related but separate finding**: a page/component param CANNOT be referenced by a `TextField`'s DSL constructor directly (no such param exists — see the `TextField` entry earlier in this file) nor via `EditWidgetPatch.initialValue` (bool/num only) nor via `bindText` (`Text`/`Button` only, throws for `TextField`) — the only way to bind a `TextField`'s dynamic initial text (whether to state or to a param) is the raw proto field `FFTextField.initialText` (`node.props.textField.initialText = FFText(textValue: FFStringValue(variable: <FFVariable>))`, NOT the deprecated `legacyInitialText`). For a PAGE-PARAM-sourced variable specifically (as opposed to the already-documented page-STATE-sourced shape elsewhere in this file), replicate the SDK's own internal (non-exported) `varFromPageParam` helper (`variable_helpers.dart:31`) using only public proto types:
  ```dart
  FFVariable(
    source: FFVariableSource.WIDGET_CLASS_PARAMETER,
    baseVariable: FFBaseVariable(
      widgetClass: FFWidgetClassVariable(
        paramIdentifier: FFIdentifier(name: 'paramName', key: '<param's own key from lib/flutterflow_project/pages/<slug>.dart>'),
      ),
    ),
  )
  ```
  Confirmed in `generated_code` afterward: `TextEditingController(text: widget!.prefillDate)` — correct in both the deep-link-present and deep-link-absent cases automatically, since the param's own declared default (`.withDefault('')`) already IS the pre-existing no-deep-link behavior.

## `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` cascade into REAL duplicates when multiple independent calls across separate pushes all target the SAME fixed anchor — not just `ensureInsertedBefore` alone

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §72) building the admin nav menu.** Five separate tasks, each adding one new admin-dashboard nav button over several distinct pushes, each independently wrote `page.ensureInsertedAfter(page.findByKey('Button_ecphn4d3'), Button(...))` — same literal anchor key every time (the original, never-moved first button). Since the whole `dsl/edit.dart` file re-executes top-to-bottom on every push, all 5 calls re-run together on every subsequent push, in file order. `ensureInsertedAfter` always inserts directly adjacent to its anchor, so each call — being later in the file, hence running after earlier ones on every push — pushes the previously-inserted button one slot further from the anchor. On the NEXT push, an earlier call's own idempotency check (is my widget already the anchor's immediate next sibling?) then finds a DIFFERENT button occupying that slot and, not matching, re-inserts itself. This cascades: each push after a second call joins the same anchor duplicates every button already anchored there — confirmed via `lib/flutterflow_project/pages/admin_dashboard_page.dart`: 21 total children for 9 intended unique buttons, with duplicate counts scaling directly with how many pushes had occurred since each button was first anchored (earliest-anchored buttons had accumulated the most duplicates).
- **This is a DIFFERENT bug from — and does not contradict — the earlier `ensureInsertedBefore` duplication finding elsewhere in this file/`PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md`, and it does NOT mean `ensureInsertedAfter` is unsafe in general.** Every prior confirmed-safe use of `ensureInsertedAfter` in this project anchored to a DISTINCT widget per call — no two calls ever targeted the same anchor key. The actual rule: **`ensureInsertedAfter` (and `ensureInsertedBefore`) are only safe when each call's anchor is unique to that call.** The instant a SECOND independent call — added in a later push — targets the same anchor as an earlier one, the earlier call's own adjacency-based idempotency check breaks on the next push. This applies regardless of which of the two insert primitives is used; the bug is "multiple independent calls sharing one anchor across pushes," not a defect specific to either primitive.
- **Standing rule going forward**: any page/list that accumulates more than one structural insertion OVER TIME (e.g. an admin nav menu that grows a button per task, added across separate sessions/pushes) must use a single `page.ensureReplaced(...)` reconstruction of the whole container, EXTENDED IN PLACE on each new push (add one more child to the same literal list) — never a growing chain of `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureInsertedBefore` calls that all target the same fixed anchor. If you don't yet know whether a future task will add another sibling to the same spot, default to `ensureReplaced` from the start rather than `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` — the cost of guessing wrong (a growing anchor-collision chain) is a live, user-visible duplication bug.
- **Fix recipe once duplication is found**: freeze (comment out in place, with a note pointing at the consolidated fix) every offending `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` call sharing the anchor, then add ONE `page.ensureReplaced(page.findByKey(<container's current key>), Column(name: '<required — see next point>', children: [...every intended child, exactly once...]))`.
- **`ensureReplaced` requires the replacement ROOT widget to have a non-empty `name:`** (confirmed 2026-08-13, first `ensureReplaced` call in this project targeting a plain `Column` rather than a `ListView`) — omitting it fails with `"ensureReplaced requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name."` `ListView`'s own prior `ensureReplaced` uses always had `name:` set already (needed for its own `source:`/`itemBuilder:` identity), which is likely why this requirement hadn't surfaced before.
- **Verification for this bug class**: don't trust push success alone — count actual children in `generated_code/`'s widget file (`grep -c "FFButtonWidget("` or equivalent) and in the typed SDK's page file (`grep -c 'type: "Button"'`), and diff the unique button-text set against the intended list. This is exactly the check that caught the bug in the first place (found while verifying an unrelated task's push, not from a user report).

## `ListView` in this DSL is ALWAYS dynamic — it has no `children:` parameter at all, unlike `Column`/`Row`/`Stack`

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §72) building the admin ledger view.** Writing `ListView(children: [...])` to hold a mix of static text/headers and nested dynamic sub-lists fails to compile: `No named parameter with the name 'children'`. Confirmed by reading the SDK's own `ListView` class (`widgets.dart`) — its constructor takes only `source:` (required) + `itemBuilder:` (required), plus `spacing`/`shrinkWrap`/`horizontal`/`padding`/`name`/`visible`. There is no static-children mode at all, unlike `GridView` (same shape — `source:`+`itemBuilder:` only) or `Column`/`Row`/`Stack` (all take `children:`).
- **Fix**: for a scrollable container that mixes static content (headers, summary text) with one or more nested dynamic lists, use `Column(scrollable: true, children: [...])` as the outer container instead of `ListView` — put the static widgets and the nested real `ListView`(s) (each with their own `source:`/`itemBuilder:`) directly in the `Column`'s `children:`.

## GENERALIZED LESSON: any TWO structural mutations (of any kind) sharing a target across separate pushes are unsafe — not just `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` sharing an anchor, and the failure mode isn't always silent duplication

- **Confirmed 2026-08-13 (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, `PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md` §80) during a fresh-eyes adversarial re-review, extending the §72 finding.** §72 documented that multiple `ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before` calls sharing one anchor cascade into silent duplicate widgets. This entry found a SECOND instance of the same underlying bug class — two `ensureReplaced` calls on the SAME conceptual list, added in different tasks/pushes, one never frozen when the other was added — but the FAILURE MODE was completely different: not silent duplication, but a hard `compileDslApp` validation failure ("Custom action argument X is not set properly", "Invalid value for argument item") on a block that hadn't been touched in the failing push at all. Root cause: the FIRST `ensureReplaced` call (earlier in the file) ran first each push, rebuilding its target subtree under a fresh key; by the time the SECOND `ensureReplaced` call (a different task, hardcoding an OLDER key) ran, its `findByKey(...)` target no longer existed — silently resolving to nothing, and the itemBuilder closure downstream became invalid.
- **The generalized rule, superseding the narrower §72 framing**: it is unsafe for ANY TWO structural mutation calls — `ensureInsertedAfter`, `ensureInsertedBefore`, `ensureReplaced`, in any combination — to target the same conceptual widget/subtree across separate pushes, full stop. The specific SYMPTOM depends on which primitives are involved and can be silent (duplicate content, as in `ensureInsertedAfter`+`ensureInsertedAfter`) or loud (a hard validation failure, as in `ensureReplaced`+`ensureReplaced`) — don't assume "no error" means "no bug," and don't assume a validation error's own stated location (e.g. "custom action argument X") points at the actual root cause; it may be a downstream casualty of a completely different, earlier structural mutation silently invalidating a key the failing block depends on.
- **Diagnostic technique that found this**: when a validation error's stated location doesn't correspond to anything actually edited in the current push, don't trust the error message's framing — grep the WHOLE file for every structural-mutation call (`ensureInsertedAfter`/`Before`/`Replaced`) targeting pages/collections near the error site, and check for more than one call sharing a `findByKey(...)` target (even across widely-separated tasks/line-ranges). Isolate via targeted reverts of EVERY recent change individually before concluding a specific change is the cause — in this case, 3 separate isolation pushes each ruled out an innocent recent change before the actual (unrelated, months-old) duplicate block was found.
- **Standing rule reaffirmed and generalized**: any page/list that accumulates more than one structural mutation over its lifetime must use a SINGLE reconstruction (via whichever primitive fits) that is EXTENDED in place on each subsequent change — never a second, independent call of any kind targeting the same widget. When adding one more child to an existing list/menu that already has its own `ensureReplaced`/`ensureInsertedAfter` block from an earlier task, edit that ORIGINAL call in place; do not write a new one, even if the new one's own logic is completely correct in isolation.

## Never change an already-live custom action/function's ARGUMENT LIST and its calling widget's wiring in the SAME push

**Confirmed 2026-08-14 (`icoccha-new-mockup-9a6ing`, feature-build session, §37 "4 discrete consent checkboxes"), after 3 failed push attempts on the same change.** Needed to add 4 new named boolean parameters to 2 already-live custom actions (`checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete`, `callCompleteOnboarding`) and rewire the one button that calls both.

- **Attempt 1**: froze the original `app.customAction(...)` declarations, added `app.raw((project) { updateCustomAction(project, name:, arguments: [...raw FFParameter list...], code: r'''...''') })` (each `FFParameter` built exactly the way the SDK's own internal `_compileNamedParameter` helper does — `FFIdentifier(name:, key: generateRandomAlphaNumericString())` + `dataType:`, confirmed by reading the compiler source directly, not guessed), and in the SAME push rewired the button's `CallCustomAction.named(..., arguments: {...new 4 names...})`. `flutterflow ai test` passed locally; `flutterflow ai run` FAILED server-side `compileDslApp` validation with "Custom action argument X is not specified" for **every** argument on both actions — not just the 4 new ones, the original ones too.
- **Attempt 2**: added `args: {...}` to both `CallCustomAction.named(...)` calls (per its own doc comment: passing `args:` makes the call site validated client-side against an explicit type map, instead of "deferring to the compiler's project-fallback path" when `args:` is omitted). Exact same failure, unchanged.
- **The critical discovery**: a THIRD push that reverted the two actions' argument lists back to their ORIGINAL signature (no `arguments:` override at all in `updateCustomAction`, i.e. code-only change) failed with `Unknown argument(s) for custom action "checkBasicInfoFieldsComplete": consentAgreed. Declared args: accountType, agreedBusinessCommission, agreedPersonalData, agreedPrivacyPolicy, agreedTos, birthDate, city, gender, prefecture.` — **proof that attempt 1's argument-list mutation had actually SUCCEEDED and persisted server-side**, despite that same push having been reported as a validation FAILURE. A "failed" push is not proof nothing landed — some proto mutations (specifically: an action's own argument-list change via `updateCustomAction`) can commit independently of whether the OVERALL push's validation (specifically: a widget referencing those args) passes.
- **Root cause (inferred, not exhaustively proven, but consistent with all observed symptoms)**: `compileDslApp` most likely resolves a button's `CallCustomAction` argument-name-to-parameter-key mapping against a snapshot of the target action taken BEFORE same-run `app.raw(...)` mutations are applied within that same push — so a button referencing brand-new argument names added earlier in the SAME script run can never resolve correctly, regardless of `args:` hints on the call site. This is a DIFFERENT failure shape from the already-documented `ensureInsertedAfter`/`ensureReplaced` anchor-collision class above — same underlying theme (a same-push ordering/snapshot assumption silently violated), different primitive (`updateCustomAction`'s `arguments:` vs. structural widget mutations).
- **The actual fix, once diagnosed**: split into TWO SEPARATE pushes — push A changes ONLY the action's argument list (`updateCustomAction(..., arguments: [...])`) with **no** corresponding button-wiring change in that same push; push B (a subsequent, separate `flutterflow ai run`) updates ONLY the calling widget to reference the now-already-live new argument names, with **no** further `arguments:` changes to the action in that same push. Confirmed working: the push that ONLY changed the button (referencing the argument names already confirmed live from the earlier attempts) succeeded cleanly.
- **Standing rule**: never combine "change an existing custom action/function's argument LIST (add/remove/rename a parameter, via a raw `arguments:`/`parameters:` override on `updateCustomAction`/`updateCustomFunction`)" and "change a widget's wiring to use those new argument names" in the same `flutterflow ai run` push — even though both are valid Dart/DSL and pass `flutterflow ai test` locally. Land the argument-list change alone first, confirm the push succeeds, THEN wire the calling widget in a follow-up push. (Pure CODE-only changes to an action/function, with an UNCHANGED argument list, remain safe to combine with anything in one push — this constraint is specifically about ARGUMENT LIST changes racing against SAME-PUSH references to the new names.)
- **Diagnostic technique that found this**: when a "Custom action argument is not specified" error lists EVERY argument (not just newly-added ones) as missing, suspect this same-push ordering race rather than a malformed `FFParameter`/`args:` shape — the raw parameter construction itself was correct both times (confirmed by the later "Unknown argument(s)... Declared args: [exactly the intended new list]" error, which could only have been produced by a correctly-formed, successfully-applied argument list). If a "failed" push's error references argument names or state that don't match what you just wrote, consider that the failure may be a stale-snapshot artifact of a PARTIALLY-persisted earlier change, not proof the current script is wrong — a follow-up probe push (deliberately reverting one side of the change) can distinguish the two.

- **`If(condition, then:, orElse:)` requires `condition` to resolve to a BOOLEAN — passing a nullable `String` action output (e.g. an upload action that returns the uploaded file's URL, or `null` on failure) fails `compileDslApp` with "Conditional execution for action is improperly set," naming the specific button/widget, not a generic error.** Confirmed 2026-08-14 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §85): `If(ActionOutput('uploadShopPhotoResult'), then: [...], orElse: [...])` where `uploadShopPhotoResult` is a `String?` (a photo-upload action's return value) failed exactly this way. **Fix**: don't treat a non-bool action output as if truthy/falsy — insert the same `isNonEmptyString` custom-action check this project's own KYC upload flow already established (`pickAndUploadImage` → `CallCustomAction.named('isNonEmptyString', arguments: {'value': ActionOutput('uploadResult')}, outputAs: 'uploadSucceeded')` → `If(ActionOutput('uploadSucceeded'), ...)`) rather than inventing a new check — this exact 3-step chain is the established, reusable pattern for "did this action produce a real (non-null, non-empty) value" anywhere in this codebase, not just for image uploads.

- **`ensureReplaced` requires the REPLACEMENT widget's root to have a non-empty `name:`** — the SDK validates this before accepting the call and rejects a nameless root with `"ensureReplaced requires an inserted or replacement root widget with a non-empty name"`. Confirmed 2026-08-14 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §88) wrapping a `ListView` in `Expanded(...)` to fix a real layout crash — `Expanded`'s constructor takes its child POSITIONALLY with `name:` as a trailing named parameter (`Expanded(this.child, {this.flex, String? name, ...})`), so writing `Expanded(name: 'X', child)` is invalid Dart (named arguments must follow positional ones in a call) as well as the wrong shape for this validation anyway — the correct form is `Expanded(child, name: 'X')`. Easy to get backwards when retrofitting a `name:` onto an already-written nested widget tree (natural instinct is to add it as the first/most-visible line). Any widget being newly introduced as an `ensureReplaced` root (not just `Expanded` — the same applies to `Flexible`, `Container`, or anything else with a positional-then-named constructor) needs this checked before pushing, not discovered from the push's own error.

- **`ensureReplaced` is safe to use even when the REPLACEMENT widget's type differs from the original anchor's type** (e.g. swapping a page's top-level `ListView` for a `Column` that now wraps `[Button, Divider, Expanded(ListView)]`, to add a page-level action button above a list that has no existing header widget to `ensureInsertedAfter`) — confirmed 2026-08-14 (PROJECT_KNOWLEDGE.md §84) by reading the SDK's own `_ReplaceSpec`/`_ReplaceOp`/`_ReplaceAlreadyAppliedNoop` source directly (`.flutterflow/sdk/flutterflow_ai/lib/src/dsl/edit.dart`), not by trial-and-error. The mechanism: the FIRST run replaces-by-key unconditionally, regardless of whether the new widget's type matches the old one. Every LATER run, once that original key no longer resolves (because the first run's replace already consumed/regenerated it), falls back to `_ReplaceAlreadyAppliedNoop`, which searches the WHOLE widget tree for a node matching the REPLACEMENT's own `name`+`type` (NOT the original anchor's name/type) — found + semantically equal → no-op; found + different → update in place preserving key; not found at all → throws. **Practical rule**: give the new (differently-typed) replacement widget a stable, distinct `name:` that never changes across future pushes, and every rerun self-heals correctly no matter how deeply it's now nested relative to the original anchor. This is the same underlying self-heal mechanism already documented for same-type replacements (§81's "`ensureReplaced` self-heals via `_ReplaceAlreadyAppliedNoop`" note) — this entry adds the confirmation that it also covers type changes, since that wasn't obvious from behavior alone and matters whenever a "wrap this existing widget in a new container" edit is needed on a page with no pre-existing sibling to anchor an insertion against.
